Chapter 244: The Final Destination

"It's like this," Luana said, looking a little embarrassed. "My son Travis set out with a Pikachu last year. He's been traveling for nearly a year, but I haven't heard a thing from him. Not one message. I can't help worrying."

Ethan raised an eyebrow. "You want me to find your son?"

"Yes." Luana gave a rueful smile. "I have to run both the Gym and the hotel. I can't spare the time to go looking. If you happen to meet him on your travels, could you please tell him to come home soon?"

No word at all? Had Travis run away, or simply set off on a journey? Ethan thought it over. Whatever the reason, he ought to have sent at least a message or made a call. Something about the family's relationship might have gone wrong, or perhaps Travis had quarreled with them before he left.

"His name is Travis, right? If I meet him, I'll encourage him to come back."

"Thank you. He's stubborn and likes to make up his own mind. If he won't come home, please at least ask him to call me."

"I will, but don't count on me finding him. It's a big world."

Luana did not seem too worried about that. "You're both going to Johto to challenge the Silver League. When the Silver Conference begins, there's a good chance you'll meet."

"He's challenging the Silver League too?" Ethan was surprised. "That is a coincidence. All right, I'll keep an eye out."

He did not promise more. Travis might never enter the Conference, or something might delay him along the way. Ethan did not want to give Luana false hope.

Delighted that he had agreed, she bowed slightly. "I'm counting on you. Thank you!"

...

Ethan and Sabrina left Kumquat Gym, climbed onto Charizard, and headed for their final destination: Pummelo Island.

It was the Orange League's headquarters. Drake, the League's strongest Trainer and the pride of the islands, waited at Pummelo Stadium for challengers.

Charizard carried them through the wind. High above the sea, watching clouds drift past, Ethan said, "Time goes quickly. Before I knew it, our journey through the Orange Islands was almost over."

One arm rested around Sabrina. After sitting so long, he grew bored, and his hand began to wander. Sabrina gave it a light pat. Ethan grinned and behaved himself.

"You sound as though you'll miss it here," she said dryly.

"Of course I will. The weather's warm, the scenery's beautiful, and it's a wonderful place to travel. Most of all, I'd like more time with you."

His sudden, rather corny declaration brought a small smile to Sabrina's face. She said nothing, only leaned back against him.

The Orange Islands held happy memories for her too. Here, she and Ethan had become lovers. They enjoyed the quiet closeness for several minutes before she suddenly looked down at a spot on the sea.

"I think that's Ash down there."

"Ash?" Ethan looked and saw a small dark shape moving over the water. He guided Charizard lower. It was a Lapras carrying Ash and his friends in the direction Ethan and Sabrina had just left.

"Hey, Ash! Misty!" Ethan called.

They stood and looked up at his voice. "Ethan!" Misty waved excitedly.

Once Charizard was level with Lapras, Ethan leaped onto its back. Lapras dipped a little beneath the weight of four passengers.

"Where are you headed?" Ethan asked.

"To Kumquat Island, of course! We're going to challenge Kumquat Gym," Ash said, rubbing his nose.

"So you already have three badges? You've made good time."

Ash could hardly wait to ask, "What about you, Ethan? Have you got your fourth?"

"Of course he has, you idiot," Misty said before Ethan could answer. She rolled her eyes at Ash, her voice full of admiration. "Ethan's much stronger than you. And didn't you see that he came from the direction of Kumquat Island?"

Ash scratched his head and laughed awkwardly. Misty had pointed out the obvious.

"She's right," Ethan said with a wave of his hand. "I've reached the end of the course. Next comes the final boss."

"Aw." Ash sighed. "I hurried as fast as I could, and I'm still a step behind you."

"Don't worry about it. Whether you get there a little earlier or later makes no real difference. It's only a few days."

As they spoke, they noticed Tracey leaning over the side with a glass bottle. He lowered its opening into the sea and filled it.

"What are you doing?" Misty asked.

"Collecting a sample," Tracey said as he screwed on the cap. "I'm a Pokemon Watcher, remember? Comparing seawater and studying the habitat here is part of my work."

Then Ash's Pikachu seemed to spot something. It cried out toward the water beneath them.

They looked down. A huge dark shape was approaching quickly below the surface. Its outline resembled a whale or some other large sea creature. Before anyone could react, it passed beneath Lapras and surfaced ahead of them in a burst of spray.

It was a roughly built submarine.

Chapter 245: A Wave of Magikarp

The submarine's hatch opened. Under everyone's gaze, an elderly man emerged. He had a thick handlebar mustache, small round glasses, and dark skin. He was short and looked at least sixty or seventy, yet he was remarkably lively. His eyes shone with intelligence.

As soon as he climbed out, he produced an assortment of strange little devices. He measured the wind, received a signal with a small satellite dish, and kept himself so busy that he seemed not to notice anything else.

Then he glanced aside and saw the group nearby. "Oh! You young people over there, you'd better be careful!"

The warning came without explanation. Ethan and the others stared at him.

"Careful of what?" Ash asked.

The old man ignored the question. He straightened and spoke to himself. "Wind conditions good. Water temperature acceptable. No change in the current. Solar spectrum normal. They'll be here soon!"

None of it made sense to the others. They looked at one another, puzzled.

"They? Who's coming? Who is this man?" Misty whispered.

"I don't know," Tracey said. "It looks like he's doing scientific research."

Before they could ask more, the sea around them seemed to boil. Bubbles and spray burst from the surface. The waves grew rougher, and tens of thousands of Magikarp suddenly surged up from below.

"M-Magikarp?"

"Where did so many come from?"

Ash and his friends gaped. The Magikarp paid them no attention. Every one of them swam in the same direction, as though following an unseen command.

Only then did Ethan understand the old man's warning. The Magikarp formed a dense red wave. They meant no harm, but anyone who fell into the water now could be battered badly by the sheer mass of fish.

Lapras shivered beneath the group. The sight had frightened it, and it stopped swimming, bobbing with the current. Ash hurried to soothe it.

Ethan steadied himself. Once the first shock passed, he noticed that the old man on the submarine was not surprised at all. He looked excited.

Tracey had kept his attention on the Magikarp. After studying them for a while, he spotted something too. "What are those rings around their tails?"

Ethan looked away from the old man. Many Magikarp had colored bands around the base of their tails. Some were red, some yellow, and some blue. Apart from their colors, the rings looked alike. Who had put them there?

The old man watched through binoculars for a time, then spoke to himself in a voice far more excited than his years suggested. "Targets confirmed! Speed and direction exactly as predicted. Excellent. Emergency dive! The pursuit begins!"

He closed the hatch and started the submarine again, following the Magikarp. They watched it move away until it disappeared from view.

"What was that about?" Misty said. "He rushed in and rushed off. I don't understand any of it!"

Tracey frowned, as though something had come back to him. "Wait. That direction..."

He took a map from his bag and checked the coordinates. His finger stopped on a small island. "That's it! The Magikarp are headed this way, and Rind Island lies right along their route."

"Rind Island?"

The name was new to Ethan, Ash, and Misty. Tracey, who came from the Orange Islands, explained. "As far as I remember, it's small and uninhabited. Hardly anyone knows about it, much less visits. I only heard my grandparents mention it a few times when I was little."

Tens of thousands of Magikarp and a mysterious old man in a submarine had caught Ash's interest. "Something must be happening. Let's go and see!"

Misty and Tracey agreed. They wanted to know what was going on too.

Ethan thought for a moment and decided his own journey could wait. He told Sabrina what they were doing and asked her to follow on Charizard. He stayed on Lapras with Ash and the others as they headed toward Rind Island.

Spray flew around them. Lapras avoided the Magikarp and fought to keep its balance in the disturbed water. On its back, Ash and his friends argued excitedly over why so many Magikarp had gathered. Their guesses grew less convincing by the minute.

Ethan listened without joining in. He had known that meeting Ash made it likely he would stumble into another adventure. It seemed to have happened again.

He did not mind. To Ethan, travel was made of unfamiliar experiences. Taking part in strange events was how people tested themselves and grew.

The constant splashing interrupted his thoughts. Magikarp were hardly impressive in an ordinary battle, but in the sea they could swim at a respectable speed. Even carrying so many passengers, Lapras worked hard to keep up. One by one, the Magikarp overtook it until they were following at the rear of the vast school.

Fortunately, after ten or fifteen minutes they could already see Rind Island in the distance. They would not lose their way now.

Chapter 246: Swimming Upstream

From a distance, Rind Island looked covered in forest. Cliffs of varying heights ringed its shores, forming a natural barrier between its interior and the open sea. A low mountain rose in the middle.

Tracey had been right: it appeared to be uninhabited. At least from outside, Ethan could see no sign of human activity.

The Magikarp did not swim past the island. They poured into a river mouth and headed inland. Ethan looked around and spotted the submarine they had seen earlier, moored in a sheltered bay. Its hatch was closed and it sat motionless. The old man must have gone into the island after the Magikarp.

"What now?" Ash asked.

"What else?" As Lapras approached the shore, Ethan was the first to jump off. "We've come this far. Let's go in and see."

Once everyone had landed, Ash recalled Lapras. Sabrina brought Charizard down and joined Ethan. The group set out on foot along the riverbank.

The river was packed with Magikarp as far as they could see. The fish fought their way upstream, their red bodies filling the water in a spectacular procession.

"Wow. You don't see a sight like this every day," Misty said over the constant splashing.

Ethan nodded. It reminded him of stories from his old home about fish returning from the sea each year to lay their eggs farther inland. Perhaps the Pokemon here had a similar habit. He took out his phone and recorded the scene.

While they watched the river, the old man's voice came from behind them. "What are you young people standing around for?"

They turned. The submarine's pilot was approaching with more than a dozen boxes and bags piled on his back. They had been following his trail, yet somehow he had come up behind them. His luggage stood higher than he did. How could such a small old man carry it all and still move so easily?

Seeing them stare, he began distributing the boxes among them without asking. In a moment, even Sabrina and Pikachu had a small box apiece.

Ethan and the others were caught off guard. The stranger had evidently decided they were free porters.

"Hey, sir, what are you doing?" Ash asked, holding a large box in his arms.

The old man did not answer. Now carrying only a small backpack, he strolled along the river toward the forest. After a few steps, he noticed they had not followed and beckoned them on.

"Come along. Don't just stand there!"

Come along to where? They exchanged baffled looks, then followed him.

"Wait a minute! Will you tell us what you're doing?" Ash called.

The old man did not even slow down. Misty looked down at her box and sighed helplessly. "What is going on?"

"Let's see where he's taking us," Ethan said. "We'll probably understand when we get there."

He glanced at Sabrina. She still held her box and looked calm. In a low voice, he asked, "You're not angry, are you?"

She shook her head. In the past, she would have used her psychic powers to send a stranger this rude flying through the air. Now such a small imposition hardly mattered to her.

They continued beside the river, deeper into the forest. The ground rose from a gentle slope to a steep climb. They were ascending the mountain they had seen from the sea.

The change in height made the current stronger, yet the Magikarp kept swimming against it toward higher ground. Ethan was beginning to understand. If they were not going to spawn, something else must be drawing them.

After several more minutes, Misty's curiosity overcame her. She hurried up beside the old man. "Who are you, sir?"

He looked at her friendly face and smiled. "I'm Quincy T. Quackenpoker, a Pokemon Watcher."

A Pokemon Watcher? The others all looked at Tracey. He shook his head. Despite sharing the profession, he had never heard Quackenpoker's name.

Misty lifted the box in her hands. "What's in all these?"

"Isn't it obvious? Equipment for observing Magikarp!"

"Equipment to observe Magikarp?"

"Of course." Perhaps Misty reminded him of his granddaughter, for he explained a little more. "I prepared every piece especially for watching these Magikarp."

Tracey's interest flared. He hurried to the river's edge and pulled out his sketchbook. "So I was right! These Magikarp must have some special ability or behavior. I'll observe them too."

He had stepped too close. A Magikarp leaped from the water, startling him and splashing him in the face. His enthusiasm cooled at once.

"Foolish boy," the old man snorted. "Those are ordinary Magikarp. There's nothing worth observing about them."

"Huh?" Tracey asked, ignoring the water on his clothes. "Didn't you just say all this equipment was for Magikarp? If they're ordinary, why bring it?"

Chapter 247: The Lake of Birth and Evolution

Quackenpoker did not explain further. He checked the time, frowned at how much had passed, and said, "We can't waste any more time here. You'll understand when we arrive. Come on, hurry."

He turned and quickened his pace toward the summit.

After spending a little time with him, Ethan and the others knew he meant no harm. He was simply an eccentric researcher who paid little attention to ordinary manners. They still had plenty of questions, but with Quackenpoker walking away, they followed.

Beside them, the Magikarp continued swimming tirelessly against the current. Soon the school reached a cluster of three small waterfalls. They tried to climb, but the water rushed straight down. Even at full effort, many Magikarp were swept off partway up. Only a few thrashed hard enough to overcome the force and reach the water above.

Ethan stopped again. For the Magikarp, this was an enormous obstacle. He could hardly believe he was seeing such a sight. They were working too hard for this to look like a simple spawning migration. It reminded him of the old tale of carp leaping the Dragon Gate.

In this world, though, a Magikarp that made it over the falls would not become a dragon soaring through the clouds. It would become a fierce and terrifying Gyarados.

He took out his phone to film. In the short time he watched, hundreds of Magikarp failed, exhausted, and were washed downstream. The others kept coming, one wave after another, throwing themselves at the falls.

Tracey turned to watch too. "Mr. Quackenpoker, what's at the top that makes them work so hard?"

"Only an ordinary lake," Quackenpoker answered without stopping. "It doesn't look like much. I call it the Lake of Birth and Evolution."

"The Lake of Birth and Evolution?" the others repeated.

"That's where these Magikarp were born. Every year at this time, they come back to complete their evolution."

Evolution? Ethan stared. An ordinary Magikarp did not evolve easily. It often required a Trainer's careful raising and training. Was Quackenpoker saying that a Magikarp only had to return to this lake to evolve?

If that was true, the place would seem magical to anyone raising Magikarp. Ethan thought like a Trainer, though, and his interests were not quite the same as everyone else's. Tracey, as a Pokemon Watcher, saw a different question.

"Mr. Quackenpoker, how many Magikarp make it up each year?" he asked eagerly.

Quackenpoker gave him a thoughtful look, glanced back at the falls, and shook his head. "It varies. From what I've seen in past years, at most one in ten gets over the waterfalls and reaches the lake."

Only one in ten? The number surprised them, though the force of the water made it understandable. Given the size of the school, that could still mean nearly a thousand Magikarp reached the summit.

"Do all the ones that reach the lake evolve?" Ash asked, astonished.

"Don't be ridiculous." Quackenpoker rolled his eyes, amused and annoyed at once. "Getting there doesn't guarantee anything. Only one in ten of those Magikarp evolves into Gyarados."

One tenth of one tenth. That would still produce around a hundred new Gyarados, hardly a small number.

Ash scratched his head, about to ask more. Quackenpoker waved him off. "Enough questions. If we don't move, we'll miss their mass evolution!"

Ethan and the others fell silent. Now that they had come this far, they wanted to see how the Magikarp evolved together. They hurried on.

None of them noticed a clump of bushes moving shortly after they left.

"Hey, did you hear all that?" a voice asked from inside. It sounded almost human, though a little raspy. Team Rocket's trio poked their heads out.

"Of course I did!" James's eyes widened with excitement. "A lake of evolution! What an amazing discovery!"

"Idiot," Jessie said, folding her arms and giving him a sidelong look. "It's an opportunity."

"Oh? How?"

Jessie lifted her chin. "If it's a lake of evolution, we catch enough Magikarp and throw them in. Then we'll have lots of powerful Gyarados! With those, we can build a magnificent, earthshaking, unbeatable, greatest-ever Gyarados army!"

"A Gyarados army?" James pictured himself commanding a whole force of them. "The Boss would finally take notice of us! We could even hold our heads high in front of that woman with the watermelon-rind glasses!"

"Exactly!" Jessie clenched her fists, eyes blazing. "With a Gyarados army, Team Rocket will be unstoppable. No one will stand a chance against us!"

"But..." James hesitated. "Will it really be that easy? Gyarados and we have never gotten along."

"Don't worry. I've thought of that," Jessie said with a confident laugh. "We catch them while they're still Magikarp. Then they can't disobey us after they evolve!"

Chapter 248: The Magikarp's Home Island

Ethan and the others followed the river up through the forest. After another ten minutes or more, they finally reached the summit of Rind Island's mountain.

The ground leveled out. Dense forest surrounded a broad, clear lake in the center, and water spilled from it toward the slopes below.

"Here we are. The Lake of Birth and Evolution!"

Quackenpoker set down his backpack. Only a few Magikarp were swimming around the lake, and the mass evolution had not begun. Relieved that they had made it in time, he relaxed.

Misty dropped onto a large rock with a long breath. She had climbed all this way carrying a heavy box. At last she could rest.

Everyone looked at the lake. As Quackenpoker had said, it appeared perfectly ordinary. Had they come upon it by themselves, they would never have suspected that anything remarkable happened here.

Sabrina glanced around. "It's strange. Why can't I see any other Pokemon?" she asked Ethan quietly.

He noticed the silence then. A forest this large ought to have wild Pokemon, and a lake should attract other Water types. Yet the entire summit was quiet.

"Maybe the Magikarp migration disturbed them," he suggested. "They might have moved away or hidden until it's over."

Sabrina nodded without committing herself. They had too little information to be sure.

Quackenpoker was already taking equipment from the boxes and setting it up beside the lake. There were devices shaped like cameras, a laptop, and something resembling a satellite receiver. Ethan and his friends gathered around. None of them knew enough to identify every instrument from its appearance.

"Mr. Quackenpoker," Tracey asked politely, "what do these machines do?"

Quackenpoker seemed pleased by the question. With no sign of evolution yet, he had time to answer. "They automatically analyze all sorts of data on the Magikarp: weight, growth patterns, bone density, muscle fatigue, and more. They're powerful tools."

Ten-year-old Ash was lost among the technical terms. He scratched his head. "That's amazing. I don't understand what you said, but it sounds impressive!"

Quackenpoker laughed. Praise from a child pleased him.

Splash! Splash!

Several red shapes broke the surface as more Magikarp came up the river and entered the lake. The group watched until the water settled. Then Ethan turned back to the old man, who handled his equipment with practiced ease.

"How long have you been a Pokemon Watcher?"

Quackenpoker thought for a moment. "I've forgotten the exact number of years. It must be fifty by now."

"Fifty?" Misty stuck out her tongue in amazement. To stay with a profession for fifty years took dedication. Even if he had started at fifteen or sixteen, as Tracey had, he would be in his sixties or older now.

It seemed hard to be out in the sun and wind at that age. What if he died on some deserted island? Misty caught herself and hurriedly shook away the disrespectful thought.

Quackenpoker was busy with his instruments and noticed nothing. Even if he had, his manner suggested he would have ignored it.

Tracey admired the weathered veteran. "That's incredible. How long have you been observing Magikarp?"

"Not long. Only three years," Quackenpoker replied lightly, as though the time were nothing more than a number.

Ethan looked at him with fresh respect. At Quackenpoker's age, many people would choose to stay home and enjoy their families' company. Few would still travel to remote islands to study Pokemon. A person's life held only so many years, and he had devoted three of them to these Magikarp. His love of Pokemon was plain.

Misty trained Water-type Pokemon, but even she could not imagine giving Magikarp that much attention. She admired him while still wondering whether such an ordinary species really needed three years of study.

Tracey remembered the colored rings. "What about the bands we saw on some of their tails?"

"I put them there," Quackenpoker said. "They identify individuals by the year they left the island. Red means one year ago, blue means two, and yellow means three."

"All these Magikarp were born here?"

"That's right. Magikarp born on this island swim out to sea as they grow. No matter how far they travel, they return at this time each year. Rind Island is their home."

Ethan had never known Magikarp could have a home island. They lived all over the world, though, so perhaps this migratory behavior belonged only to the ones in the Orange Islands.

The light of sunset fell across the group. More Magikarp were gathering in the lake, but none had begun to evolve.

"This isn't good," Quackenpoker said, checking the time again. "There are fewer Magikarp than I expected. It looks as though we'll have to camp here tonight."

Chapter 249: Camping by the Lake

"Mr. Quackenpoker, does the number of Magikarp affect when they evolve?" Tracey asked.

"It does," Quackenpoker said. "There's a mysterious power in this lake. The more Magikarp gather, the more likely they are to trigger it and evolve together. With too few, it can take a long time, perhaps until tomorrow."

"I see."

The sun was sinking toward the horizon. Since they would have to spend the night outdoors, everyone set to work before dark.

Quackenpoker continued operating his equipment and observing the Magikarp. Ash and Misty gathered firewood and lit a campfire. Tracey brought out the cooking gear and took charge of dinner. Ethan and Sabrina pitched their own tent and Quackenpoker's.

Tracey made curry rice with a fruit salad. The Pokemon gathered the fruit in the nearby forest; Ash and his friends had brought the curry, potatoes, rice, and other ingredients themselves. They camped and cooked far more often than Ethan, who tended to stay in hotels or eat at restaurants, so they traveled prepared.

"This is delicious!" Quackenpoker said after tasting the fragrant curry. The mild spice surprised him. "What luck! I was going to make do with instant noodles. I never expected a meal like this on an uninhabited island."

Across the folding table, Ethan took a generous spoonful of rice covered in sauce. It really was good, appealing in taste, smell, and appearance. Tracey was a capable cook, though still not quite as good as Brock, who usually handled Ash's meals.

They talked as they ate. The pleasant dinner stretched beyond an hour, and the moon was high by the time they finished. Misty brewed a pot of hot tea, and everyone gathered around the campfire.

Quackenpoker held his warm cup and looked at Tracey. "Young man, you're a Pokemon Watcher too, aren't you?"

"Huh? How did you know?"

"Never underestimate an experienced Watcher's eyes." Quackenpoker blew aside the tea leaves floating in his cup, looking pleased with himself. "I saw your sketchbook and binoculars down by the river. You look at things the way a Watcher does."

"So that's how." Tracey had never tried to hide his profession, and he did not mind Quackenpoker recognizing it.

After an evening together, the old man's manner toward all of them had softened. He smiled kindly and held out his hand. "May I look at your observation notes?"

"Of course." Tracey gave him his sketchbook and the notes he carried with it.

Quackenpoker read through them carefully. At last he nodded in approval. "Very good. You've studied all kinds of Pokemon, and your observations are detailed. Keep at it. I believe you'll become an excellent Pokemon Watcher."

The two discussed their knowledge and experiences. Tracey learned a great deal from the older Watcher. Quackenpoker did not leave Ethan and the others out. Over tea, he told them stories of unusual events from his travels. He had visited regions all over the world, and Ash and his friends listened in amazement to adventures they could hardly imagine.

More than a hundred Magikarp entered the lake during that time, but there were still too few to trigger the mass evolution.

After ten o'clock, everyone grew sleepy. Ethan and Tracey began discussing who would keep watch, but Quackenpoker refused before they could settle it.

"I'll stay up alone," he said firmly. "To be honest, I wouldn't feel comfortable leaving the Magikarp measurements to you."

He was blunt, but they understood. He had studied these Magikarp for three years and waited a long time for tonight. They might evolve at any moment. He wanted to observe them himself rather than entrust the crucial hours to anyone else.

Tracey first tried to persuade him to rest for his health. Quackenpoker would not change his mind, so the others let him sit beside the fire through the night. Ethan understood him. At a moment this important, he too would want to see it with his own eyes.

Ethan and Sabrina washed up by the river, then went into their tent. Ash and Tracey spread their sleeping bags at a comfortable distance from the fire and climbed in.

Misty alone stayed where she was. She looked at Ethan and Sabrina's tent, her expression shifting as a thought occurred to her.

Why were they sleeping in the same tent? She did not know exactly what their relationship was, but sharing a tent so openly suggested they were close. Could Ethan and Sabrina be a couple?

She made up her mind, lifted the tent flap, and ducked inside.

Ethan was spreading a blanket on the ground. Camping was colder than sleeping indoors, and without enough bedding they might wake shivering before morning.

He looked up in surprise when Misty entered. Sabrina frowned but said nothing.

"Ethan..." Misty smiled at him.

"What is it?" he asked.

She hesitated. Then, seeing that Sabrina was already in her sleepwear, she found the nerve to ask, "It's windy outside. Could I sleep in your tent tonight?"

Ethan smiled ruefully to himself. She had already come in; he could hardly send her back out. He did not want her left in the cold, either. Ignoring Sabrina's knowing look, he smiled at Misty.

"Of course. The tent's big enough for four people."

Chapter 250: A Late-Night Talk in the Tent

Yes!

Misty almost laughed aloud when Ethan agreed to let her sleep in the tent. She had no wish to lie next to Sabrina, so she sat down on Ethan's other side without worrying about how it might look. He was now between the two of them.

She and Ethan had hardly spent any time alone since parting at the Indigo League. Here was a rare chance to talk without Ash getting in the way. She had so much to say that she chattered like a happy bird, scarcely minding that Sabrina was there. Before long, the tent was filled with her voice and laughter.

Sabrina gave her a sidelong glance. She did not want to interfere with Ethan's friendships. Besides, Misty was still a young teenager and no threat to their relationship. Sabrina did not enjoy listening to the two of them laugh together, though. She took headphones from her bag, put them on, and lay down with her eyes closed.

Misty noticed. With Sabrina no longer listening, she became even more open about how much she had missed Ethan.

"Ethan, guess who I dreamed about last night?"

"Who?" he asked, though he could guess.

"You, of course!" Her cheeks turned pink, but she met his eyes. "I dreamed about when we went to the hot springs on Cinnabar Island. Isn't it funny? Only a few hours later, we met again. My dream came true!"

The hot springs on Cinnabar Island. Ethan remembered that visit, and his nose began to itch. She had brought it up on purpose.

If Misty were a few years older, her hints might have been harder for him to brush aside. As it was, she was much too young. He pretended not to understand, gave a few vague answers, and quickly changed the subject.

Misty did not seem to notice. Even if she had, simply talking with Ethan made her happy.

A light breeze stirred the tent and made its wind flap quiver. Sabrina fell asleep, so Ethan lowered his voice. He and Misty chatted about other things until after eleven, when Ash and Tracey began snoring outside. Then he urged her to sleep.

"You need your rest, Misty. You won't grow properly if you don't get enough sleep. And you don't want your skin to lose its glow, do you?"

"All right..."

Reluctantly, she lay down and grew quiet. Sleeping so close to Ethan felt like a dream. They were in the same small tent, breathing the same air. Was this what it meant to live under one roof?

Ethan switched off the portable lamp. The tent went dark. Misty listened to his steady breathing but kept tossing and turning. After a long while, she rolled over to look at his face.

There was too little light to see more than his outline through a gap in the tent. She smiled to herself anyway. She thought he looked handsome even in profile. If only she could see him every day.

Her thoughts wandered until sleep finally caught up with her. She drifted off with a contented smile.

Ethan let out a breath. Misty might be young, but she spoke her mind and was bold enough that she could be harder to handle than Sabrina. Now that she was asleep, he could rest too.

...

Late that night, the camp was still. The only sound came when Quackenpoker added wood to the fire and the logs crackled.

Knowing that his age made it harder to stay awake all night, he had brewed himself a pot of strong tea. Its bitter taste and the stimulating compounds in it helped him resist sleep. A hot mouthful also drove off the cold and warmed him through.

A noise from the lake caught his attention. Had the Magikarp begun to evolve together? He sprang up and looked toward the water.

...

In the tent, Ethan heard a low mechanical hum through his sleep. At first he ignored it, but it went on until he could no longer rest. He forced his eyes open, felt around for the portable lamp, and switched it on.

It was only three in the morning. He had slept for less than four hours.

He was about to get up and see what was happening when he realized something was wrong. Looking down, he found Misty had somehow slipped into his bedroll. He was holding her in his arms.

Ethan went rigid. He had grown used to sleeping beside Sabrina and must have mistaken Misty for her in his half-asleep state. At least he had not done anything worse, though Misty's pajamas had become a little disarranged.

Her brow tightened as if she might wake. Ethan quickly drew his hand out from beneath her and acted as though nothing had happened.

A few seconds later, Misty opened her eyes and saw Ethan getting dressed. She sat up, still sleepy.

"Ethan? What's wrong? Did something happen?"

She felt a draft at her collar and realized that one button of her pajamas had come undone. She covered herself and turned slightly aside. Nothing else seemed out of place.

How had she ended up in Ethan's bedroll?

Before she could think it through, he answered. "I'm not sure. There's a noise outside. Let's go have a look."

Sabrina woke too. All three got dressed and left the tent. The night air was cold enough to sting, but it drove the last of Ethan's sleepiness away.

Ash and Tracey were climbing out of their sleeping bags, yawning. They looked at one another. None of them knew what had happened.

The campfire still burned, though much smaller than before. Quackenpoker stood beside the lake with binoculars, watching the water.

Chapter 251: The Magikarp Submarine

Ethan and the others joined Quackenpoker at the lakeshore. They did not need binoculars to see what was happening. Under the clear moonlight, one patch of the Lake of Birth and Evolution looked as though it were boiling. Bubbles rose and burst across its surface.

The maddening mechanical hum came from beneath them.

Misty pulled her clothes tighter against the cold. "Mr. Quackenpoker, does this mean the Magikarp are starting to evolve?"

He hesitated. "They should begin evolving around this time, but that patch of water isn't behaving normally. It doesn't look like a sign of evolution."

His voice sounded a little hoarse. Ethan glanced over as the old man lowered his binoculars. Quackenpoker seemed alert despite staying up all night, but his eyes were bloodshot.

The rumbling grew louder, and the lake churned more violently. Before anyone could work out what it meant, a gigantic Magikarp burst through the surface, beams of light blazing from its eyes.

Splash!

Water streamed off its body and down its gaping mouth. For a moment, it looked monstrous. Then Ethan and the others noticed the seams in its metal plates, the screws set into its scales, and the searchlights mounted where its eyes should have been.

It was no giant Pokemon. It was a Magikarp-shaped submarine.

"A submarine?" Quackenpoker stared. "Why would one be here?"

Ethan's gaze moved to its front. Between the two searchlights was a red letter R. That settled it. He had seen the trio's familiar vehicle plenty of times. Where Ash went, Team Rocket never seemed far behind.

"Not them again," Ash said through clenched teeth.

"Team Rocket?" Quackenpoker had never heard the name. "They don't sound like good people. Who are they?"

"Don't ask!" Ethan called, but he was too late.

The submarine's lights flared. Voices boomed over a loudspeaker.

"Since you asked so sincerely, we'll gladly tell you!"

Ethan's heart sank. He was not afraid of what Team Rocket might do. The problem was that speaking to them seemed to trigger their favorite routine, and once it started, they would recite their motto at full volume without a trace of embarrassment.

Sure enough, they had begun.

"To protect the world from devastation!"

"To unite all peoples within our nation!"

"To denounce the evils of truth and love!"

"The cute and charming villains!"

"I'm Jessie!"

"And I'm James!"

"We are Team Rocket, blasting through the galaxy!"

"A white hole! A white future is waiting for us!"

"That's right, meow!"

Ethan could not see them inside the submarine, but their voices made it easy to imagine how smug they looked.

"We've heard that speech enough times!" Ash shouted. "Team Rocket, what are you doing here?"

"Building our Gyarados army, of course!" Jessie replied. "When it's ready, Team Rocket will rule the world. Everyone will tremble beneath us! Ahahaha!"

"A Gyarados army?" Tracey demanded. "So that's your plan. Are you the reason the Magikarp haven't evolved yet?"

"You make it sound so nasty," James said, pushing Jessie aside to take the microphone. "This isn't a protected area or anyone's private property. We got here first. We didn't steal a thing. We caught those Magikarp fair and square!"

More Magikarp were still swimming into the lake, but after hearing James, Ethan saw how sparse their numbers had become. There were fewer than he had seen earlier.

"You caught them?" Quackenpoker was furious. "So you are behind this! Release those Magikarp at once!"

"Mind your own business, old man," Meowth sneered. "Hand over your Pokemon and Pikachu while you're at it, or we'll make you sorry!"

Ethan could not understand how the trio could still challenge them so boldly after failing so many times. Each defeat seemed to make them more determined. Give them a short rest, and they would be back, full of energy. Whatever their aim, that refusal to give up was almost admirable.

Quackenpoker was not intimidated. "Not a chance! Release the Magikarp, or I'll call the police!"

"The police?" The trio broke into laughter. Jessie could barely catch her breath. "Old man, we're criminals! What good will calling the police do? This island is far from any town. We'll be gone long before anyone gets here."

Quackenpoker was a first-rate Pokemon Watcher, but outside his field he could be surprisingly naive. Out here, he thought a call to the police would solve an immediate threat. Even Ash knew better.

Ethan had understood the hard lesson on his first day in this world. When danger was in front of you, waiting for someone else was no use. You needed the strength, and the Pokemon, to deal with it yourself.

He patted Quackenpoker on the shoulder and shook his head. "There's no point arguing with them. Right now, we have to handle this ourselves."

Chapter 252: Team Rocket Came Prepared

"Well..." Quackenpoker hesitated. He was desperate to save the captured Magikarp, but he was an ordinary Pokemon Watcher, not a skilled battler.

When he had caught Pokemon poachers before, shouting at them had usually been enough to make them flee in guilty haste. He had never faced an organization brazen enough to stay and fight like Team Rocket.

While he stood there uncertain, the trio kept working. A fishing net shot from the top of their Magikarp submarine. It swung across the nearby water, caught several more Magikarp, and hauled them into the submarine's mouth.

Quackenpoker could not stand by any longer. He looked to Ethan and the others. "Please. Can you get those Magikarp back?"

"Of course!" Ash spun his cap around and pointed at the submarine. "We're not letting them get away with this. Pikachu, let's beat them!"

"We'll help too!"

Misty sent out Starmie, and Tracey released Marill. With Pikachu, they rushed toward the submarine.

Pikachu leaped onto Ash's shoulder, sprang from his arm onto Starmie, and rode it across the water.

Marill looked small, but it was capable too. The round Water- and Fairy-type Pokemon was sometimes called a water mouse. Its fur resisted getting soaked, and the tip of its tail was said to hold an oil lighter than water. Even a strong river current would have trouble pulling it under.

Ethan took out a Poke Ball. "Charizard, you go too!"

Blue light flashed. Charizard spread its wings and breathed fire as it attacked Team Rocket from above.

Quackenpoker watched anxiously. Charizard looked formidable, but the others seemed so small. "Are those Pokemon strong enough? These criminals don't look easy to beat. Shouldn't you send out a few more?"

"Don't worry," Tracey said confidently. "Pikachu and Marill are strong. They can handle this."

He was not boasting or dismissing the danger. From their previous encounters with Team Rocket's trio, four Pokemon ought to be more than enough. Quackenpoker was not entirely convinced, but for now he had to trust them.

The Pokemon reached the submarine. Pikachu fired Thunder Shock, Starmie spun into it with Rapid Spin, and Marill struck with Tackle. All three attacks hit at once.

Bang! Bang-bang! Bang-bang-bang!

The metal plating held, but the repeated blows made an alarming pounding sound inside.

Team Rocket fought back. Two mechanical arms extended from the submarine's roof and reached for Pikachu and the others. However fast the arms swung, the Pokemon dodged them.

"Darn it!"

Warning lights flashed in the cockpit. James and the others gripped their controls as the vessel shook.

They had spent a great deal of their hard-earned savings upgrading what had once been a simple submarine. They had expected that preparation to make their plan easier. Instead, Ethan's group was putting the machine in danger already.

"This isn't working," Meowth said. "Time for our secret weapon!"

He slammed a paw onto a red button. Gears clacked. To the astonishment of everyone on shore, the submarine began to change shape. Its Magikarp form unfolded into a Gyarados-shaped battle machine.

"What?!"

Ash and Tracey stared. They had never expected a submarine to transform.

"Ha! We came prepared!" Jessie called smugly. "You're not the only ones who can evolve. Now face the power of our mechanical Gyarados!"

The machine looked fierce. It could fire electricity from its mouth, and its two mechanical arms, odd as they looked on a Gyarados, made it more dangerous. The arms nearly caught Marill several times. Each time, Pikachu reacted quickly enough to save it.

What a nuisance. Ethan shook his head. Team Rocket's technology was impressive. Electricity streamed across the lake from the machine's mouth, leaving Starmie and Marill almost nowhere to dodge.

For all their limited funds, the trio had built a remarkable machine. They seemed capable of nearly anything except defeating Ash.

Crackle!

Another electrical discharge spread over the surface. Marill and Pikachu moved aside, but Starmie was caught and shuddered in pain.

"This won't do!" Misty cried. "They're at too much of a disadvantage in the water. We have to draw that mechanical Gyarados onto shore!"

"But how?" Ash scratched his cheek. "It isn't moving."

He was right. Team Rocket knew the advantage the lake gave them. They stayed in the water and kept firing electricity, showing no intention of coming ashore.

"Charizard, use Flamethrower!"

Ethan tried attacking from the air. Fire washed over the mechanical Gyarados, but it dived under the water and surfaced again, the flames extinguished.

Team Rocket really had prepared for this. Even Pikachu's Thunder Shock seemed to do little to the machine. Its shell would not be broken quickly.

Tracey sighed. "It's stronger than I expected. We'll have to find a weak spot."

"Look there!" Quackenpoker pointed at the machine's underside.

Ethan and the others followed his finger but saw nothing unusual.

"That part is bulging," Quackenpoker explained. "The captured Magikarp must be stored there. They needed space for them, so the armor around that compartment should be thinnest. Break it open, and we can free the Magikarp!"

The others nodded. Quackenpoker had spotted the machine's likely weak point in moments. For all his uncertainty about battle, his trained eye as a Pokemon Watcher was impressive.

Chapter 253: A School of Gyarados

Ethan exchanged a look with Ash and the others. They had no better plan for now. Quackenpoker's suggestionto strike the mechanical Gyarados in its bellygave them their best chance.

They nodded and called out together.

"Charizard, use Flamethrower!"

"Pikachu, Thunder Shock!"

"Starmie, use Swift!"

"Marill, Bubble Beam!"

The four attacks converged on one spot. Fire, electricity, stars, and bubbles struck the mechanical Gyarados in a tremendous blast.

Boom!

Its shell broke open. The combined attack punched through the armor and seemed to damage the machine's power system too. A moment later, the Gyarados that had been so threatening exploded.

Boom!

Jessie, James, and Meowth had no time to react in the cockpit. The blast hurled them into the sky. They dwindled into three bright points and disappeared into the distance.

"What a horrible feeling!"

"How did it turn out like this again?"

Their cries faded away. Ash and the others breathed a little easier, but they had no time to celebrate. A chain of explosions ran through the container holding the captured Magikarp.

Bang! Bang!

The Magikarp flew into the air. As they fell, they seemed to fill the sky like a sudden shower of red flowers. More than a hundred wriggling fish splashed back into the lake.

"Oh, no!"

Quackenpoker's expression changed. He had not expected their attack to put the Magikarp in danger. If the Pokemon were hurt badly enough to miss their annual mass evolution, he would never forgive himself.

Fortunately, the blasts had not engulfed them. The Magikarp had been caught at the edge of the explosions and suffered only minor injuries.

As the last one landed, a brilliant pillar of light erupted from the lake. Then a second appeared, a third, and more than a dozen others. The beams shot straight through the night toward the clouds. They lit the lake, the campsite, the forest, everyone's faces, and even the rest of Rind Island.

"Yes! The Magikarp are finally evolving together!" Quackenpoker stared wide-eyed, delight breaking through his worry. He hurried back to his equipment to record it all.

Ethan and the others knew what each pillar meant. A Magikarp was evolving beneath every one. Tracey watched the surging light in awe.

"No battle experience, no Trainer helping them. Is this the power of the Lake of Birth and Evolution? It's incredible."

Charizard and the other Pokemon returned to their Trainers. Together they watched the rare sight.

After a while, Ethan sensed something was wrong. Enough Magikarp had gathered to begin the mass evolution, but what had happened before it was nothing like what any of them had hoped for.

When the light vanished, a group of fierce Gyarados filled the lake. Every one of them looked angry. Their eyes burned with rage.

Quackenpoker was so absorbed in the successful evolution that he missed the change in their behavior. The Trainers noticed at once.

"Ethan..." Misty swallowed. As a Water-type Trainer, she was especially sensitive to Gyarados. "Why do they look so frightening?"

Ethan gave a helpless smile. "They struggled up those waterfalls, barely caught their breath, and Team Rocket captured them. Then our fight caught them in an explosion and sent them falling through the air. I'd be angrier than they are."

As if to prove him right, the Gyarados turned toward the group on shore. Their hostility was almost palpable.

"This is bad!" Tracey cried. "They're looking right at us!"

"They don't think we were the ones who caught them, do they?" Ash asked, alarmed.

There was no time to find out. The Gyarados closed in. More than a dozen enormous jaws opened, gathering destructive energy inside.

Ethan recognized the move. "Hyper Beam! There are too many of them. Everybody, get out of the way!"

He grabbed Sabrina with one hand and Misty with the other and ran. Behind him, Ash fled with Pikachu. Tracey started to follow, then saw Quackenpoker still working at his equipment. He hurried back and caught the old man by the wrist.

"Mr. Quackenpoker! We have to leave now!"

The alarm in Tracey's voice made Quackenpoker look up. Only then did he notice the Gyarados.

"What? How did this happen?"

He tried to pack his instruments, but there was no time. Tracey pulled him away.

"Wait! My equipment!"

To Quackenpoker, three years of research were more precious than his own life. He found the strength to break free, spread his arms, and tried to shield the instruments with his frail body.

Ethan clicked his tongue. The old man's dedication was admirable, but his disregard for his own safety was maddening. Ethan could not abandon him.

"Ash! Stop running! Help protect Quackenpoker!"

Ash turned, saw what was happening, and stopped. Misty and Tracey joined them. They threw more Poke Balls, releasing their remaining Pokemon. Working together, the Pokemon launched attacks toward the incoming Hyper Beams.

The beams were almost upon the camp. Their counterattack swept forward in a band of colors and met the Gyarados' attacks just in time.

Bang!

So much energy colliding produced a deafening shock wave. At its center, a light as brilliant as the sun flared across the lake.

Chapter 254: Stopping the Rampage

A shock wave as powerful as a hurricane swept across the campsite, flinging everything into the air. Tents, sleeping bags, equipment, and stones of every size scattered. Even the trees at the edge of the forest swayed as if they might be torn from the ground.

The wind whipped Ethan's bangs. Sabrina waved a hand, raising an invisible barrier before the group. It stopped the blast's aftershock from reaching them, though its force still jolted their bodies.

Misty, the lightest among them, lost her balance. Ethan caught her before she fell. She stayed close to him for a moment, sheltered against his chest.

"Ptooey!" Ash spat sand from his mouth. Dust covered his face and clothes. When he looked toward the explosion, he found that the gently sloping lakeshore had become a round crater more than ten meters across. Fresh earth, broken stones, and exposed roots lined its sides. Lake water was already pouring into it, and before long the hole would be full.

Tracey and the others stared. "That explosion was powerful enough to change the landscape!"

Ethan let out a shaken breath. "It was far too close. We only just blocked it."

As the dust settled, he looked past the crater toward their camp. Quackenpoker's equipment was there, along with all their luggage. Losing any of it would have been a serious blow. Fortunately, their belongings were merely strewn about. The campsite was a wreck, but the things themselves had survived.

The Gyarados had recovered from the pause after using Hyper Beam. They swam toward the shore. Quackenpoker, meanwhile, was intent on checking his instruments and data for damage. He showed no sign of leaving.

Ethan could see no way around it. If they would not abandon Quackenpoker, they would have to fight.

He stepped in front of the old man with Ash and the others, putting themselves between him and the lake. Their Pokemon moved forward in turn, shielding the humans. Everyone braced for the approaching Gyarados.

As the distance closed and battle seemed inevitable, Ash gritted his teeth. He took a few steps forward and held out a hand.

"Gyarados, listen to me! We're trying to help you. Team Rocket caught you, not us"

It was just like Ash to try talking to them. The enraged Gyarados were in no state to listen. They glared at him and opened their mouths, ready to attack again.

They could not wait any longer. Ethan waved to the Pokemon beside him. "Go! Stop them!"

More than twenty Pokemon belonging to Ethan, Misty, Ash, and Tracey surged forward. Some flew, some ran, and others swam out to meet the Gyarados. They showed no fear of their opponents' size. A moment later, the two sides collided.

"Rrragh!"

"Pika, pika!"

"Rrroar!"

Cries rang along the lakeshore as attacks crossed in every direction. With so many Pokemon fighting in such a small area, the scene grew too chaotic for the Trainers to direct each one. The Pokemon had to fight on their own.

Fortunately, their partners were seasoned battlers. The Gyarados had exhausted themselves climbing the waterfalls before evolving, and rage had clouded their judgment. They had no coordination or technique. They simply struck at anything that was not another Gyarados. Attacks of every color flew across the battlefield.

In the middle of it all, another voice rose.

"Psyduck! I didn't tell you to come out! Stop leaving your Poke Ball on your own and making things worse!"

Misty stepped away from Ethan, exasperated. He followed her gaze and saw Psyduck standing in place, its head tilted as it stared blankly at the Gyarados. It made no move to attack and seemed equally unaware that it should dodge.

Misty pulled out its Poke Ball and recalled it before it could get hurt. Ethan could not help laughing. Psyduck was as reliably oblivious as ever.

When he looked back at the battle, neither side seemed to have the upper hand. They were unlikely to defeat the Gyarados quickly. His hand went almost unconsciously to the Great Balls at his left hip.

Even now, he had no intention of sending out the three legendary birds. One Thunder from Zapdos might knock out every Gyarados still in the water, but those Pokemon were his trump cards. He would not reveal them unless the danger truly demanded it.

As the fighting continued, Pokemon on both sides took injuries. Ethan and the others watched anxiously. Then a stream of fire flew over their heads, forcing them to duck.

Ash's Charizard had just taken a painful Hydro Pump. It had lost its temper and was attacking without regard for anyone around it.

"Hey, Charizard! Watch where you're aiming!" Ash shouted. As usual, Charizard ignored him.

Ethan had thought their relationship might have improved by now. Clearly, they still had work to do.

Haunter emerged from Ethan's shadow. It had been one of the few Pokemon content to watch from the sidelines, expecting an entertaining fight. Now that the battle was slipping out of control, it stayed close to Ethan to guard him.

Sabrina might protect him as well, but Haunter was Ethan's Pokemon. It would not leave its Trainer's safety entirely in someone else's hands.

Chapter 255: Frightening Growth

Quackenpoker finished checking his equipment and joined Ethan and the others. He frowned at the battle, worry plain on his face.

"How are your instruments?" Ethan asked. "Did anything break?"

"They're all right. A few scratches, nothing serious." Quackenpoker adjusted his glasses. "The Gyarados aren't angry by choice. Something outside their control provoked them. We have to stop this rampage without hurting them."

"We can't settle this peacefully now." Ethan looked at him. For all his years, Quackenpoker could be astonishingly naive. "The only way to calm them is to defeat them. We can't hesitate and hope for some other solution."

Quackenpoker's face flushed. As a Pokemon Watcher, he naturally wanted to protect the Pokemon. Being contradicted to his face by a younger man stung, too. He had known even as he spoke that his wish was unrealistic, but he had wanted to try.

"Even so"

"There is no 'even so,'" Ethan cut in, his voice hard. "If we hesitate, they'll do more damage. We have to stop them now. Mr. Quackenpoker, battling isn't your field. Please stay out of the way."

The rebuke was harsh. Quackenpoker opened his mouth, then sighed and quietly stepped aside.

The fight remained deadlocked. Ethan had expected the trouble to end once Team Rocket was defeated and the Magikarp were freed, just as Ash and the others had. None of them had anticipated this.

He needed a way to break the stalemate. Ethan reached into the pouch at his right hip. Besides three Great Balls, it held two ordinary Poke Balls. One contained Dratini. With so little battle experience, Dratini did not belong in a fight this chaotic.

As for the other...

He drew out the ball, weighed it in his hand, then threw it forward. "Go, Gyarados!"

A flash of blue light filled the crater, now flooded with lake water, and his Gyarados appeared there.

Ethan had kept it back for two reasons. With so many wild Gyarados in the battle, he feared Ash's and the others' Pokemon might mistake his for an enemy. He also worried that the sight of so many of its own kind might send it into a rage and make it attack indiscriminately. Now he could no longer afford to hold it back.

"Gyarados, snap them out of it!"

"Rrrroar!"

Gyarados lifted its head and roared. The sheer force of its voice eclipsed the cries of the wild Gyarados and seemed to roll across all of Rind Island. The battlefield fell still. Ash's Pokemon and the enraged Gyarados alike felt the pressure of it. Timid ones, including Tracey's Marill and Misty's Goldeen, startled and turned to look.

The darkest part of the night had passed. In the faint light of approaching dawn, Ethan's Gyarados rose from the water amid drifting mist, an imposing figure.

It looked much like the wild Gyarados, but appearances were misleading. It had fought countless battles, including intense matches in the Indigo League. Its strength and presence were on another level from these newly evolved Pokemon.

Once it took in the scene, it showed no hesitation over fighting its own kind. It opened its huge jaws and bit into one wild Gyarados's long neck. Sharp teeth pierced the skin. The other Gyarados cried out in pain and turned instinctively to bite back.

Ethan's Gyarados still had its wits. It released its grip and leaned away, leaving the bite to close on empty air. Then it twisted and slammed its great tail across its opponent's face.

Bang!

The blow rang out across the lake. The wild Gyarados spun away. Ethan's partner was already swimming toward a second one. It struck with the force of a charging beast and knocked that Gyarados down too.

Even without losing its temper, Ethan's Gyarados was fiercer than the wild ones. It tore through them like a wolf among sheep. None could match it. A second fell, then a third.

As the wild Gyarados dwindled, the other Pokemon sensed that the battle had changed. Almost at once, they stopped attacking and backed away, leaving the field to Ethan's Gyarados and its own kind.

Ash and the others could see it was using no elaborate tactics. It was fighting on instinct and unleashing its strength.

"Ethan's Gyarados is incredible," Ash murmured. To him, it looked less like a battle than a merciless struggle.

"Of course it is!" Misty was startled by its ferocity too, but her eyes shone with admiration. "It helped Ethan win the Indigo League championship!"

Quackenpoker could hold back no longer. Forgetting the earlier rebuke, he came up to Ethan. "Mr. Vale, your Gyarados is being too cruel. Please recall it!"

Even Ethan had not expected this display. His Gyarados's growth had been astonishing. The signs had been there during the Indigo League, but seeing what it could do now still took him by surprise.

He considered Quackenpoker's anxious plea for a moment, then left Gyarados in the water. At this point, victory was only a matter of time. He felt sorry for the wild Gyarados, but this was also a chance to see just how far his own had grown.

Chapter 256: An Imposing Gyarados

"Gyarados! The rest are yours!" Ethan called.

"No!"

Quackenpoker's face changed. Ethan had ignored his plea and ordered Gyarados to keep fighting. The old man reached out and took a few steps toward him, intending to stop him by force. Then two icy stares seemed to strike him at once.

Danger! A chill ran through him. He stopped and glanced at Ethan's side. The silent, long-haired woman stood there watching him coldly. Sabrina looked slender and had barely said a word all night, yet something about her presence was deeply threatening.

Haunter floated above Ethan's head, staring at Quackenpoker with a mocking grin. Its expression suggested it would attack the instant he took another step.

Under their combined gaze, Quackenpoker broke into a cold sweat. He dared neither move nor speak. He was no coward, but the warning in their eyes was unmistakable.

On the battlefield, Ethan's Gyarados heard his command and fought without restraint. The brief, fierce clash had already left several wild Gyarados defeated by the other Pokemon. Only ten remained in the lake.

With the others' Pokemon backed away from the fight, the wild Gyarados lost their other targets. They surrounded Ethan's partner, fixing their red eyes on it.

"Rrrroar!"

The sight of one of their own attacking them only fueled their fury. They roared as if they meant to tear this ally of the humans apart. Ethan's Gyarados showed no fear. It answered with a louder, more commanding roar.

One Gyarados outshouting a whole group of them was a sight Ash and the others would once have found impossible to imagine. Now they could only stare. The wild Gyarados paused at its challenge, then grew angrier and surged forward together.

Eleven Gyarados thrashed and bit at one another. The lake churned wildly. Waves rose more than ten meters, slammed into the shore, and scattered into spray.

There were many wild Gyarados, but their huge bodies got in one another's way. Only two or three could reach Ethan's partner at a time. That gave it room to maneuver and breathe, keeping the group from overwhelming it at once.

Teeth, headlong collisions, sweeping tails: the huge Pokemon fought with raw strength and instinct. The brutality of it made everyone watching uneasy. Even the ground beneath their feet seemed to tremble.

Humans and Pokemon alike watched in silence. Only the roars and cries from the lake broke the stillness along the shore.

"Ethan," Misty asked softly, "can Gyarados really take on so many by itself? Shouldn't you give it some commands?"

Ethan shook his head. He was uncertain too. "Honestly, with the same types on both sides and that many against one, I don't know what instructions would help. I'll leave this to Gyarados. If it loses, we can step in and finish the fight."

As he spoke, his Gyarados knocked down another of the wild ones. But the victory came at a cost. Bites had opened wounds across its body, and a patch of scales had been torn away. Blood ran into the water beneath it.

It seemed not to feel the pain. It opened its jaws and went after another opponent.

Such intense fighting drained its strength quickly. Ethan frowned as he watched, worry tightening in his chest. He had only meant for Gyarados to break the stalemate. He had not expected the battle to turn into a struggle among its own kind.

After letting it fight this far, recalling it now would leave the confrontation unresolved and might shake its spirit. He decided to let it fight with everything it had.

One wild Gyarados fell, then another. Ethan's partner fought on, bloodied but relentless, like a warrior refusing to yield.

At last, the final wild Gyarados collapsed before it. Everyone stared in disbelief.

"It... it won?"

"That's incredible!"

Blood covered Ethan's Gyarados, and many of its scales were damaged. It looked close to collapse itself, held upright only by sheer determination. Yet it rose among its unconscious counterparts, lifted its great head, and let out a long, ringing cry.

"Rrrroar!"

It sounded as though it were announcing to the world that it was the strongest Gyarados. At that moment the sun began to rise over Rind Island. Its first light fell on Gyarados, coating it in a pale, silvery sheen.

Someone swallowed. It was Quackenpoker. He had seen countless Gyarados, but the sight before him left him stunned.

"I've seen so many Gyarados, but never one so..." He nearly said brutal, then reconsidered with Ethan standing beside him. "So formidable. It's the first I've seen with such... such fierce strength."

Ethan's feelings were mixed. In his mind, Gyarados was still the Magikarp he had first seen in a tank aboard the S.S. Anne, or the fierce battler that had distinguished itself during the Indigo League. In so little time, it had grown beyond even those memories.

This fight had changed it further. Its confidence and strength had risen sharply.

When the lake finally fell quiet, Misty looked around and asked carefully, "Ethan... what do we do now?"

Chapter 257: The First Mega Stone

Before Ethan could answer Misty's question, Quackenpoker spoke up. "What else? They need treatment, immediately!"

He went back through the ruined camp and pulled medical supplies from an overturned crate. Then he hurried toward the Gyarados.

"They're all badly hurt, especially Ethan's. That one could die if we don't treat it now. Hey, you! Young Pokemon Watcher! Don't just stand there. Come and help me!"

Tracey blinked before realizing Quackenpoker meant him. "Oh! Right!" He ran over.

None of the others had expected Quackenpoker to know how to treat such serious wounds. Then again, he had traveled for years, watching and caring for Pokemon. He was bound to have learned something about first aid.

Ethan looked at his Gyarados. It lowered its head to meet his eyes.

"You worked hard," he told it.

Gyarados swam slowly toward him. Its injuries were severe; in places, the wounds were deep enough to expose flesh and bone. Even after healing, it would likely carry scars that never faded. Such scars could also stand as proof of what this fearsome Pokemon had endured.

With Tracey's help, Quackenpoker cleaned Gyarados's wounds, applied medicine, and carefully bandaged them. He handled every step like a professional.

While they worked, Ethan, Ash, Misty, and their Pokemon searched for the luggage the shock wave had scattered. It had blown their things across a wide area, making the search slow and tiring. After going over the surroundings carefully, they found that nothing valuable was missing, though everything was in disarray.

The hours passed as each group worked. The sun climbed higher. When Ethan checked the time, he was surprised to see that it was already ten in the morning.

Stomachs began to growl. They had slept badly and were now hungry, tired, and sleepy. Quackenpoker, who had been awake all night, was only keeping himself going through stubbornness.

Once he had finished treating the last of the wild Gyarados with lighter injuries, Tracey was finally free to make breakfast.

Ash rubbed his empty stomach. "I'm starving. I think I could eat a whole cow."

"Then go help Tracey," Misty said, giving him a look. "Get the campfire going." She was famished herself. She had managed to ignore it until Ash complained, but now her stomach seemed to agree with him.

Ash rolled up his sleeves and went to help. With everyone pitching in, the smell of food soon drifted through the camp.

Quackenpoker finished tending the other Pokemon injured in the fight and washed the blood from his hands just as Tracey brought out breakfast. He had made a generous meal to help everyone recover from their exhausting morning.

Quackenpoker sat in a folding chair and ate a few mouthfuls. He felt dizzy with fatigue and sighed. "One night without sleep and I'm already at my limit. Age catches up with you, whether you admit it or not."

He looked around at the young people and thought about everything that had happened since they met. "Thank you. Without you, I don't know how I would have brought this crisis to an end."

Ethan smiled but said nothing. If Quackenpoker had never met Ash, Team Rocket would not have followed them here. Then none of this would have happened. In a way, they were the ones who had dragged Quackenpoker and the Gyarados into trouble. At least it was over now.

"You're too kind," Tracey said, waving away the thanks. "Of course we had to help." Whatever the others thought of Quackenpoker, Tracey still respected him deeply as a Pokemon Watcher.

The wild Gyarados had all awakened after their initial treatment. No longer angry, they slipped beneath the lake's surface. Together with the Magikarp that had not evolved, they swam downriver, left Rind Island, and headed for the open sea.

The Magikarp's annual evolution gathering had finally come to an end.

After breakfast, no one needed to say that it was time to part. Quackenpoker would stay to analyze his observations and catch up on sleep. Ethan and the others were tired too, but they had rested for a few hours during the night and did not want to linger.

Quackenpoker set up his tent again and said goodbye. He was about to climb inside when he remembered something.

"Ethan..." He approached with a complicated look. "There's something I'd like to give you."

Ethan was puzzled. Why would Quackenpoker suddenly offer him a gift?

The old man searched his pack and drew out a round, translucent stone. He held it out. Ethan's eyebrows rose in surprise.

"I found this while traveling in another region," Quackenpoker explained. "It has a remarkable power. In battle, it can make Gyarados evolve into a more advanced form."

"I know," Ethan said. "It's a Gyaradosite, a Mega Stone for Gyarados."

"Mega Stone?" Ash and the others had never heard the term. They crowded around to examine the stone, which seemed to glow with energy.

"Ethan!" Ash asked excitedly. "Does this mean a Pokemon can evolve even further?"

"Yes, but only temporarily. Once the energy runs out during the battle, it returns to its previous form."

Now it was Quackenpoker's turn to be surprised. "You already know about it? That saves me explaining. I'd like you to have it."

Ethan did not reach for the stone. "Why give it to me?"

Quackenpoker held his gaze for a long moment. "You're a Pokemon Trainer, so you can use it. And your Gyarados is special. Very special. I believe this stone will do the most good in the hands of you and your Gyarados."

Chapter 258: The Winner's Cup

"Thank you."

After hearing Quackenpoker's reasons, Ethan accepted the Gyaradosite and slipped it into his pocket. He did not yet know how to use its power, but he already had a Mega Stone. It was a promising start, putting him one step ahead.

"Well, I need some sleep," Quackenpoker said. "Perhaps our paths will cross again." He turned and lifted the flap of his tent.

Tracey took a step toward him. Pokemon Watchers were few and far between, and despite their brief acquaintance, he felt a natural kinship with Quackenpoker. The old man said they might meet again, but at his age, and with their travels taking them in different directions, that seemed unlikely.

"Mr. Quackenpoker," Tracey called earnestly, "please take care of yourself!"

Quackenpoker paused. He did not turn around, but waved over his shoulder before ducking inside. Once the tent flap fell, the camp grew quiet.

Ethan and the others picked up their luggage and followed the river back to the coast. The Gyarados and Magikarp were already out of sight. After everything they had been through together, it was time for their own paths to part.

Misty found this goodbye harder than the last few. She still had to travel with Ash, though. Their friendship meant she could not simply abandon him.

She looked at Ethan, lips parting as she tried to find the words. Ash suddenly barged between them.

"Ethan! I'm heading straight for the Kumquat Gym. Just wait! I'll catch up to you soon!"

Misty nearly stumbled. Gritting her teeth, she brought her fist down on Ash's head.

Thump!

"Ow! Misty, why'd you hit me?"

Ash looked genuinely bewildered. He had no idea what he had interrupted. Misty could only glare at him and mutter that he was hopelessly dense.

"Nothing. My hand was itching, and I felt like hitting you!"

"Misty! That's awful!" Ash protested.

Their familiar bickering brought a warmth to the farewell. Ethan smiled. "Ash, there's no need to rush. Traveling is how you learn. Take it one step at a time and give yourself room to grow."

"I know, but..." Ash clenched a fist. "You're the champion. Of course I want to catch up to you!"

"You?" Misty scoffed. She was Ethan's staunchest supporter. "You'll never catch up to Ethan. Give it up."

"Champion, huh..." Ethan rested a hand briefly on Misty's head, then looked out across the sea. "The Indigo League wasn't as demanding as it could have been. I was lucky enough not to face an opponent who truly pushed me, so I won. To be honest, I think I should be aiming for you instead, Ash."

"What?" Everyone turned to him in surprise.

Ash pointed to himself. "Me?"

"Yes." Ethan nodded solemnly. "I don't remember if I've told you this before. We'll both face plenty of strong opponents in the future, but I've always believed that if anyone can defeat me, it'll be you."

Ash stared. Sabrina, Misty, and Tracey were just as astonished. It was an extraordinary vote of confidence. Ash's best result so far was reaching the top sixteen at the Indigo League. Ethan had gone through the tournament undefeated and become its champion. Everyone who knew them could see the gap between their present abilities.

Ash trembled with excitement. "Ethan, I promise I'll work even harder! I won't let you down. One day I'll become the Trainer you believe I can be!"

"I believe you," Ethan said. "Just remember that reaching that goal means taking each step seriously."

He patted Ash on the shoulder and waved toward the sky. A few seconds later, Charizard landed beside him. Ethan and Sabrina climbed onto its back.

"It's time for us to go. Safe travels, all of you. See you next time!"

Before Ash or the others could reply, Ethan nudged Charizard gently with his heels. It spread its wings and shot away from Rind Island like an arrow.

Ash and Misty watched from the beach until Charizard shrank to a dark speck. Their feelings were different, but in each of them, a fierce resolve burned.

...

Pummelo Island lay in the northwestern Orange Islands. It was small, somewhat farther north than most of its neighbors, and not especially populous. Its town was no great metropolis.

Even so, islanders held it in high regard. The Orange League headquarters and the venue for the Winner's Cup were both here, keeping Pummelo Island at the center of their attention.

After several hours in the air, Charizard landed in a forest on the island. Ethan recalled it, and he and Sabrina walked into a town whose buildings and decorations had a sporting theme.

They did not go straight to Orange League headquarters. First they found a guesthouse with a hot spring, ate a full meal, soaked for several hours, and slept through the night. By morning they had washed away the dust and fatigue of their travels.

Rested and refreshed, Ethan went to headquarters to register his challenge.

Unlike many tournaments, the Orange League's championship match had no fixed date each year. A challenger who earned all four badges first had to apply here. Once the League confirmed that its Supreme Gym Leader was available, it prepared overnight and held the Winner's Cup match the following day.

Challengers arrived often enough that some years saw more than a dozen Winner's Cup matches held one after another.

Chapter 259: The Orange League's Vice President

When Ethan and Sabrina entered the Orange League headquarters, a staff member approached them in the lobby. He looked to be in his sixties, with short silver-gray hair and a small mustache.

"Good morning. What brings you here? Are you sightseeing, or would you like to buy a souvenir?"

Ethan stepped forward. "Hello. I'd like to challenge the Orange League. Could you register me?"

The man's manner grew warmer at once. "Welcome! It's been almost a month since we last held a Winner's Cup match. It's wonderful to have a new challenger. First, though, the rules require me to see your proof of eligibility."

Proof? He must mean the four badges from the Orange Crew Gyms. If nearly a month had gone by without a challenge, the Orange League's competitive scene seemed to have stalled. That could hardly be good for its growth.

Ethan opened his bag, took out his badge case, and showed the man four different shell-shaped badges.

The staff member passed a small scanner over each one. A moment later, Ethan's details appeared on the computer screen. They even check for counterfeits, he thought. He glanced over and saw his photograph, his home region of Kalos, and his Indigo League championship listed there.

"Oh! You're the Trainer who just won the Indigo League?" the man said, delighted. "I thought you looked familiar."

People who worked for a League kept up with Pokemon news. They heard who had won in each region, which competitors were favorites, and which Trainers had become famous. The larger regions crowned champions every year, but no champion had ever come here to challenge the Orange League.

"That championship is in the past," Ethan said with a wave. "Right now I'm an ordinary challenger, starting on the same footing as anyone else. Please don't make too much of it."

The man did not take the modest words literally. Ethan was hardly an ordinary challenger. The Orange League was smaller, more remote, and less populous than the major Leagues. Its influence and viewing figures lagged well behind theirs.

An Indigo League champion entering the Winner's Cup was news worth publicizing. If Ethan won, his reputation would be confirmed. If he lost, it would show that the Orange League could stand against a major League. Either result would draw attention here.

The man's smile widened. "How rude of me. I forgot to introduce myself. I'm Carl, vice president of the Orange League."

Vice president? Ethan had assumed he was a receptionist. When he had first walked in, he had wondered why an older man was handling arrivals rather than the young women he usually saw at front desks. Now he understood.

"The vice president himself is registering me? I'm honored." Ethan held out his hand.

Carl shook it firmly. "We don't get many challengers. I can handle the desk by myself, so there's no need to keep a large staff here."

He smiled again. "You've had a long journey. The Orange Islands have warm sunshine and beautiful scenery. If you have time, I hope you'll enjoy them."

"I already have," Ethan said. "I've seen scenery I wouldn't find elsewhere, and caught some strong Pokemon. It's a lovely place to travel."

Carl was pleased. Then his gaze shifted to Sabrina. "And the young lady with you?"

"She's my traveling companion, not a challenger." Ethan turned to introduce her and found her staring at a Dragonite statue beside them.

The statue looked as though it had been cast in iron. It was a dark metallic green, carefully made, with Dragonite's wings spread and its claws extended to either side. Its eyes faced straight ahead as though it were guarding something.

Seeing their interest, Carl said, "The people here have long revered Dragonite as the island's guardian. Our current Supreme Gym Leader happens to have a powerful Dragonite himself. Since taking the position, he has never been defeated in the Winner's Cup."

Pride filled Carl's voice when he spoke of both the Pokemon and its Trainer.

Undefeated? Ethan thought. What a coincidence. So am I.

Carl entered a few details into the computer. "That's your registration complete. Under the rules, your match will be tomorrow at ten in the morning. Before then, let me show you the venue."

"I'd appreciate that."
