Carl came out from behind the desk and headed for the door. Ethan looked at the empty counter. "Is it all right to leave? What if another challenger arrives while we're gone?"

"Don't worry." Carl gave a small smile, confident but faintly rueful. "As I said, we haven't had a challenger in a long time. A few hours away from the desk won't matter."

Chapter 260: Pummelo Stadium and the Palace of Victory

Carl led Ethan and Sabrina out of Orange League headquarters and up the stone steps of a nearby hill.

The hill was neither high nor heavily wooded. Its bare slopes made the imposing building on its summit visible even from the sea beyond Pummelo Island. This was Pummelo Stadium, built for the Winner's Cup.

The stadium resembled an ancient Roman coliseum. Two tiers of high outer walls were lined with columns, giving it an air of ceremony and grandeur.

At the top of the steps, Ethan looked down over the whole of Pummelo Town. He had noticed on arriving the day before that most of its shops and houses had white walls and red-tiled roofs. The consistent, European-style buildings gave the place the feel of a town devoted to sport, exactly the atmosphere the Orange League seemed to want.

"This way, please." Carl led them inside.

They passed through a long, narrow spectator passage. Beyond it, an enormous arena opened before Ethan, with a standard, plain battlefield in the middle. The stadium was empty apart from the three of them, and the silence made it feel almost deserted. Dragonite statues stood here too, just as they had in the headquarters lobby. Carl had meant what he said about the island's guardian.

"Mr. Vale," Carl said, pointing ahead, "this is where you'll battle tomorrow. The design may look ancient, but we have screens, lighting, sound, and all the other facilities a match needs. It seats twenty thousand spectators. It's our biggest and best venue."

Carl would not have bothered with so much detail for every challenger. For an Indigo League champion, he was happy to explain. Ethan nodded and offered a few polite responses. He could hear Carl's genuine pride.

Still, twenty thousand seats felt modest beside the Indigo League stadium, which held fifty thousand. It made no difference to Ethan's state of mind. The crowd might be vast or tiny, the equipment new or old; he had come to win and would give his best even if only a handful of people watched.

He was more interested in the match itself. "Mr. Carl, what are the rules for the Winner's Cup?"

Carl's expression turned serious. "It's a full six-on-six battle. After either side has lost three Pokemon, the battlefield changes."

"Oh? I thought this plain field was where we'd fight to the end."

Carl smiled. "It's an ordinary practice field at the moment. Beneath it is a lift that lets us exchange it for fields of different types during a match."

"I see." Lifts like that were common at major events, and even many Gyms had them. Ethan had asked mostly to pass the time.

They left the stadium and approached a slightly lower building beside it. Like the stadium, it was built with stone columns and marble. A great dome covered the whole structure, making it look darker and more solemn.

"This is the Palace of Victory," Carl said. "You can also think of it as our Hall of Fame. It was built to honor Trainers who won the Winner's Cup."

They went inside. Two more Dragonite statues stood at the entrance. In the main hall, Ethan saw more than a dozen statues of other Pokemon. Most were familiar species found around Kanto and the Orange Islands, such as Venonat, Poliwrath, and Pidgeot.

Behind the statues were display cases containing rows of rectangular stone slabs. From a distance, they looked peculiar. Up close, Ethan saw that each bore impressions of Pokemon hands or feet in various sizes. Framed photographs stood in front of them.

In one photograph, a victorious Trainer crouched with a glittering gold trophy in his hands. His Pokemon surrounded him, and he smiled broadly at the camera.

Carl continued his tour. "The photos and prints belong to the Honorable Trainers who won here, and to their Pokemon. If you win tomorrow, Mr. Vale, your name and your Pokemon's prints can be preserved here forever for future visitors to see."

Forever? The idea stirred Ethan. He had come to this world from another one. At times he felt like a drifting plant without roots, with no firm sense that he belonged here. He feared that one day he might vanish as suddenly as he had arrived, leaving no trace.

A name and a photograph in this hall would be a mark that he had truly been here. The thought strengthened his resolve to win.

"Mr. Carl, are the statues at the front the signature Pokemon of those Honorable Trainers?"

"That's close," Carl said. "More precisely, each represents a Pokemon that gave an outstanding performance in its Trainer's Winner's Cup match."

Ethan looked around the somewhat empty hall. "The Orange League has been running for decades. There must have been hundreds of matches. Why are there so few statues and photographs here? Have so few challengers won the Winner's Cup?"

Chapter 261: A Look Around the Orange League

"That isn't quite it," Carl said, shaking his head. "The Palace of Victory was built only a little more than ten years ago. It honors the recent winners, not everyone who won before then. And do you remember what I said earlier?"

"Earlier?" Ethan raised an eyebrow. Carl had talked throughout the tour. It was hard to guess which remark he meant, though it must have been something memorable.

After a moment's thought, Ethan asked, "Is it because of the Supreme Gym Leader?"

"Exactly!" Carl laughed. "Our current Supreme Gym Leader, Drake, is so strong that no one has defeated him since he took the position. That's the main reason we've added so few statues in recent years."

So his name was Drake. Ethan did not remember much about him, or how strong he was. He knew only that Ash would eventually beat him and win the Winner's Cup.

Ash was not a reliable measure of an opponent's strength, though. One day he could defeat a legendary Pokemon; another day he might lose to a novice Trainer. If Drake had held the Winner's Cup for years, Ethan could not afford to underestimate him.

They finished their circuit of the Palace of Victory, completing their tour of the Orange League facilities. Ethan had gained a rough understanding of its history and present condition.

It had once enjoyed a brief period of glory. But the Orange Islands' remoteness, small population, limited economy, and relatively few Trainers had held it back. The League remained popular locally without making much of a mark beyond the islands. Carl's voice carried regret when he spoke of its prospects. Still, none of that dimmed the local Trainers' affection for it.

They went back down the hill to headquarters. Just as Carl had predicted, no one had come to register a challenge in the hour or more they had been away.

At the entrance, Carl smiled. "Mr. Vale, please excuse me. That's the end of our tour. We have to publicize tomorrow's match, and time is short."

He pointed toward town. "Have you found somewhere to stay tonight? There's a Pokemon Center in that direction with free rooms and meals. Most challengers stay there. It's quite comfortable."

"Thank you, Mr. Carl, but we've found a guesthouse in the town center," Ethan said. "It has a hot spring, and the food is excellent."

Carl's gaze passed between Ethan and Sabrina. He could tell they were a couple. Perhaps, he thought, they had chosen a guesthouse because it offered them more privacy than a Pokemon Center. He said no more about it.

"Then I'll see you tomorrow. I'll be waiting at the entrance to Pummelo Stadium before the match."

"Thank you. We'll see you there." Ethan held out his hand.

Carl shook it. "I won't keep you. I hope you perform well tomorrow and win."

"Thanks. I hope all goes smoothly."

Ethan and Sabrina walked toward the center of town. Once headquarters was out of sight, Sabrina gave a sudden, dismissive snort. "That Carl is a hypocrite."

"Why do you say that?" Ethan asked.

"He wants Drake to win. Yet he wished you victory to your face."

Ethan took her hand and smiled. "That's courtesy, not hypocrisy. It's how people get along. Should he tell every challenger he hopes his Supreme Gym Leader stays undefeated? The Orange League would have an even harder time attracting anyone."

Sabrina glanced at him. "I wish people could communicate more simply. If we could share our thoughts directly, without hiding our wants and feelings, there would be fewer lies."

Ethan shook his head, amused. It was an idealistic thought. A world where everyone heard everyone else's unguarded thoughts would surely be a mess.

In the town center, the warm sunlight made Ethan reluctant to go straight back to the guesthouse. Hand in hand, they went to the beach. After looking out over the sea, they rented a large parasol and two loungers and settled down in the breeze.

Ethan let a few of his smaller Pokemon out to play on the sand. Then he took out his phone and searched for information about the Winner's Cup. He read about Drake's usual Pokemon and his reputation, then looked through records of recent Orange League matches.

One rule stood out. During a Winner's Cup battle, the Supreme Gym Leader could not switch Pokemon at will. He could send out his next Pokemon only after the one currently fighting had been defeated.

Ethan, as the challenger, could change his lineup to suit the battle and bring in a Pokemon with a favorable type. Drake would have to keep fighting with whichever Pokemon was already out.

Sabrina looked up from the phone, frowning. "That puts the Supreme Gym Leader at a disadvantage. If the League wants its own Trainer to keep winning, why would it make a rule like that?"

Chapter 262: A Wager on Oranges

"It is strange," Ethan agreed. He did not understand why the Orange League had made a rule that disadvantaged its own Supreme Gym Leader. After watching several more match recordings and getting a better sense of Drake's ability, he said, "Even under that restriction, he hasn't lost. He's held on to the Winner's Cup all this time. We can't underestimate him."

Sabrina was about to answer when she frowned and turned toward the sea. "Something out there is coming this way fast."

Ethan looked too. The water seemed calm, with only a few speedboats moving about. He saw nothing unusual, but he trusted Sabrina's powers. Her psychic senses often warned them of things before anyone else noticed. If she felt something approaching, he believed her.

Seconds later, a dark shape appeared in the distance, flying so low that it seemed to skim the water. As it neared Pummelo Island, Ethan made out a muscular man riding a Dragonite.

Whoosh!

Dragonite raced toward the beach. At its rider's direction, it changed course, climbed sharply into the sky, and then dropped from the clouds toward Pummelo Stadium on the hill.

People along the shore cheered and waved. They had clearly recognized the Trainer. Ethan and Sabrina exchanged a glance. They had not seen his face, but who else would be arriving on a Dragonite? It had to be Drake.

Only a moment ago they had been studying ways to battle him. Now they had seen him in person. The crowd's continuing excitement showed Ethan just how popular Drake was in the Orange Islands.

After a while, Ethan got up and went with Sabrina to a nearby stand selling cold drinks and fruit. A man in his fifties sat there in a rocking chair, fanning himself. He stood to greet them.

"What can I get you?"

Ethan looked over the drinks and ice cream, then stopped at a pile of plump oranges. "Do oranges ripen at this time of year? I thought they were a late-autumn fruit."

"You're visiting from another region, aren't you?" the vendor said with a chuckle. "These are warm-season mandarins, a local specialty. They ripen now, and they're wonderfully juicy."

He broke one open and offered it to Ethan. "Go on, try it. I promise you'll like it."

Ethan accepted the fruit, finished peeling it, and put half in Sabrina's hand. They each tasted a segment. Juice burst into their mouths as they bit down. It was sweet with just a little tartness.

Ethan ate the rest of his half and gave the vendor a thumbs-up. "These are delicious. I think they're the best mandarins I've ever tasted."

The vendor beamed. He was a lifelong resident of the Orange Islands, and seeing visitors enjoy something from his home delighted him.

A decorated car rolled slowly down the street beside the beach. Orange League emblems covered it, and a speaker on its roof repeated an announcement.

"Attention, residents of the Orange Islands! Tomorrow at ten in the morning, Pummelo Stadium will host a Winner's Cup match, the first in some time. Bring your families. Admission is free!"

"A match tomorrow?" the vendor said. "At last! I've waited so long for one."

He began packing up his stand. Ethan was surprised. "Why close so early?"

"I've got to reserve a good seat. If I wait, they'll all be taken."

"But the announcement said everyone can get in for free."

"They can," the man said with a hearty laugh. "But if you want a good view, you have to book ahead. What fun is it sitting at the back and watching the whole battle on a screen?"

He looked Ethan over. "If you're free tomorrow, you and your girlfriend should go too. Matches like this don't come along often. You'll enjoy it."

Ethan seized the opening. "I've heard Drake is strong. Is he really that good?"

"Of course!" The vendor's voice rose with enthusiasm. "He's the best Trainer in the Orange Islands. Every one of his Pokemon is powerful, especially his ace, Dragonite."

"Then plenty of people must want to beat him. Why has it been so long since the last match?"

"Because he's too strong! Hardly anyone dares challenge him. Plenty can't even beat his first Pokemon, Ditto. You'll see. Whoever's challenging him tomorrow will get crushed, just like the others."

Ethan fell silent. The man had no idea he was speaking to the challenger. Ethan could only laugh to himself at being dismissed so easily.

"I'm not so sure," he said. "How about a wager?"

"A wager?"

"I say the challenger wins this time."

"Interesting! What are we betting?"

Ethan's gaze moved across the stand and settled on the mandarins. "Those. If I win, you give me a few dozen pounds of fresh mandarins. If I lose, I'll buy the same amount at twice your usual price. Deal?"

"Deal!" The vendor was easygoing, amused by the unusual offer, and utterly confident in Drake. "You're underestimating him, young man. Just watch. Drake will win again."

Chapter 263: A Big Publicity Push

Ethan and Sabrina left the beach and went straight back to their guesthouse. All the way there, Sabrina kept wondering why he had made a bet with the fruit vendor.

"You won the Indigo League. You're a champion. Why wager with an ordinary street vendor? If people hear about it, won't they think less of you?"

She looked genuinely concerned. Ethan smiled and shook his head. "It was a whim. I wanted to surprise him. When the match starts tomorrow and he sees that the man he bet against is the challenger, his expression will be priceless."

That was such a childish reason that Sabrina could only give him a sidelong look. "You're bored, aren't you?"

Ethan put an arm around her. "He decided Drake would win without knowing a thing about his opponent. I was teasing him a little. It's harmless. Don't let it bother you."

They crossed the street into the guesthouse courtyard and returned to their room. Ethan took off his shoes and lay on the bed, staring at the ceiling as he considered tomorrow's match. Sabrina sat on the sofa and switched on the television.

Above them, an old ceiling fan turned sluggishly. It squeaked in a way that made it sound as though it might fall at any moment.

From the recordings he had watched on the beach and the vendor's comments, Ethan had a reasonable idea of Drake's team. Ditto, Onix, Venusaur, Gengar, Electabuzz, and his ace Dragonite: those six Pokemon appeared in nearly every match. Unless something changed, Ethan expected to face them tomorrow.

Knowing the opponent's team did not make his own choices easy. Dratini was not ready to battle, and Blastoise had already been sent back. That left him only eight Pokemon to choose from. He would have to put together the best six he could.

Then he heard the television mention tomorrow's match. He sat up. A man and a woman were hosting a special segment from a studio, building excitement for the Winner's Cup.

The man wore a casual suit and spoke in a smooth broadcast voice. "As we've reported, Pummelo Stadium will host a Winner's Cup match tomorrow at ten in the morning, the first in quite some time. Come watch and cheer on both Trainers!"

His cohost, a playful-looking woman with short hair, nodded. "It's been so long since the last one. I can't wait!"

"And I've heard that this challenger is unlike the others," the man said, feigning mystery. "He has quite a reputation."

"Oh? Who is he?" the woman asked, playing along.

The man faced the camera, his words practiced and confident. "Since beginning his journey, this Trainer has competed in only one major tournament. Yet he fought his way through it with extraordinary strength, never losing a match."

"Really? Now you have to tell us who he is!"

"Tomorrow's challenger..." The man paused for effect. "...is Ethan Vale, the newly crowned Indigo League champion!"

"Ethan Vale?" The woman covered her mouth in surprise. "I never expected him to come to our Orange League! I've watched his matches. He's handsome, and his Pokemon left a strong impression on me."

"Exactly. After conquering the Indigo League, he's set his sights on ours. It seems the strength of our region has caught his attention. But when an undefeated champion meets an undefeated Supreme Gym Leader, who will come out on top? Tomorrow's match should be something special."

"I wish tomorrow would hurry up!" the woman said, clasping her hands. Someone off camera apparently signaled to her. She turned back to the lens with a sweet smile. "That's all for today's special broadcast. Remember: the Winner's Cup is tomorrow at ten, exclusively on Orange Islands TV. See you then!"

The hosts waved goodbye, and the screen switched to an advertisement.

Sabrina leaned back into the sofa and looked over at Ethan. "They're giving you quite a publicity push."

"I didn't expect this either." Ethan shook his head. "I asked them to treat me like any other challenger. Now they've made sure everyone will be watching."

He could already imagine the discussion once the match aired. Viewers from the Orange Islands would tune in, and many in Kanto would probably follow the live broadcast too. The attention put pressure on him.

He looked over the Poke Balls holding Gardevoir, Gyarados, Charizard, Jolteon, Venusaur, Crystal Onix, and Scyther. Then he glanced at the shadow at his feet, where Haunter rested. How should he choose?

He called out all his Pokemon for a brief meeting and went over the points he wanted them to remember.

That night, the match weighed on his mind enough to keep him awake, a rare thing for Ethan. To stop dwelling on it, he turned to Sabrina. They were intimate several times before exhaustion finally overtook him, and he fell asleep against her stomach.

He had prepared as much as he could. The rest would depend on how he performed tomorrow.

Chapter 264: Before the Match

By eight the next morning, Pummelo Island had lost its usual leisurely pace. People appeared from every direction and streamed toward Pummelo Stadium.

There were few local vehicles on the small island, so the roads did not seize up completely. The sidewalks were another matter. Crowds filled both sides of the streets as far as anyone could see.

Pummelo Island did not have nearly enough residents to account for them all. Many had heard about the Winner's Cup and taken boats from neighboring islands overnight. Those who lived nearby had arrived in two or three hours and found rooms where they could sleep. Others had spent the night rocked by waves and chilled by sea wind. They came ashore without even time for a hot breakfast and hurried toward the stadium, anxious to secure a seat.

The difficult journey had done nothing to dampen their spirits. Families and friends chatted excitedly about the coming match. A few visitors from other regions thought Ethan had a good chance, but most Orange Islanders supported Drake. They wanted their own Supreme Gym Leader to win, strengthen his reputation, and retain the Winner's Cup.

At 8:40, Ethan and Sabrina reached the stadium's side entrance. A hat, sunglasses, and a mask hid most of Ethan's face. Carl waited outside with a younger man in a suit. Several reporters from around the Orange Islands stood nearby with microphones and cameras. Security guards kept an eager crowd behind a cordon.

From across the street, Ethan took it all in. "Quite a reception," he said. Then he joked to Sabrina, "It looks like they're waiting for a movie star. Too bad I'm arriving on foot instead of in a car. I hardly fit the occasion."

"Getting nervous?" Sabrina asked with a smile.

"Of course not." Ethan started across the road. "They promoted the match so heavily yesterday, and now here I am, looking completely ordinary. I can imagine their supporters thinking, 'Is that all? He doesn't look like anyone special.'"

Sabrina gave him an odd look. "Why are you brooding over what they think? It isn't like you. Besides, getting an opponent to underestimate you is usually just what you want."

"True. Let them think I'm harmless until I show my strength." Ethan sighed. "But I thought about it last night. Drake represents the Orange League, and I carry the title of Indigo League champion. I may have come here on my own, but if I win or lose, it will affect the Indigo League's reputation too."

"Don't put so much pressure on yourself." Sabrina patted his back, offering comfort she rarely showed so openly. "Forget the spectators. Give it everything you have, and you'll have nothing to regret."

Ethan looked at her in surprise. He had not expected Sabrina to be the one reassuring him. She had changed so much since they met.

When they reached Carl, he barely glanced at the disguised Ethan. Only when he noticed Sabrina did he look again. "Mr. Vale? Is that you? Why are you dressed like that?"

Ethan glanced toward the reporters. "I didn't want to be recognized on the way here and get held up before the match. It's only a little disguise."

At his name, the reporters surged forward.

"Mr. Vale! I'm from Orange Islands TV. Could I ask you a few questions?"

"Mr. Vale! Metropolitan TV here. What made you cross the sea to challenge the Orange League?"

"Did you come because you recognize the Orange League's strength and chose it as your next goal?"

The questions tumbled over one another. Microphones nearly touched Ethan's face. He did not answer. He simply stepped beside Carl.

Carl felt a headache coming on. He had arranged for the reporters to be there, hoping their questions would generate excitement and publicity. He had assumed Ethan would welcome the attention. It was a chance to become more famous. He had misjudged him.

With Ethan refusing the interview, Carl caught the eye of the young man beside him and signaled for him to hold the reporters back. Carl then led Ethan and Sabrina through a staff entrance into the stadium.

They passed along a long corridor and reached an inner hall, where several other Orange League officials waited. Carl introduced them one by one. Ethan shook hands with the well-fed executives and exchanged a few composed words with each before Carl escorted him to the competitors' lounge.

"You really are impressive, Mr. Vale," Carl said as he opened the door. "I don't often see someone your age speak so easily with senior officials, without a hint of nerves."

"It's nothing," Ethan said, waving off the compliment. "I've been through a few similar occasions. I've had some practice, that's all."

Chapter 265: Friends Tune In

In the competitors' lounge, Carl explained the remaining procedures to Ethan: which entrance to use, what to do after a victory, where to leave in defeat, and the other rules around the match.

When he finished, he looked out the window. "To be honest, the Winner's Cup always draws an enthusiastic crowd, but today is exceptional. Every seat is taken, and people without tickets are still waiting outside the stadium. In all my years working here, I've never seen anything like it."

"Really?" Ethan smiled. "Then the Winner's Cup and the Orange League must be growing more popular."

"No." Carl gave him a long look. "Attendance has usually stayed much the same from match to match. This is different. I think we have you to thank, Mr. Vale."

"Me? I haven't done anything. You're the ones who publicized it so heavily."

Carl sighed. He knew that Ethan's fame was the real draw, but did not press the point. He checked the time and moved toward the door. "There's still over an hour before the match. We've set out food and water. Please rest here. I'll leave you in peace."

After Carl left, only Ethan and Sabrina remained. Ethan closed the door and looked around. White walls and a polished floor made the room clean and orderly. It had a large screen, a tactics board, and a tablet computer, along with a massage chair, refrigerator, and air conditioning. The equipment made it a serious preparation space; the comforts softened it.

Even through the thick walls, he could hear the low roar of tens of thousands of spectators. Soon he would walk into their sight and compete in one of the world's most popular and exciting sports. He had experienced big matches before, but the thought still stirred his blood.

Unexpectedly, arriving here had also quieted his nerves. Distractions fell away, leaving him focused. Ethan smiled at himself. Perhaps he was the sort of Trainer who came alive at a major event.

He released several Pokemon from their Poke Balls so they could adjust to the room and the tension before the match. Then he reclined on the sofa and shut his eyes.

He had gone to sleep late and risen early. Both his energy and stamina were low. He needed to rest before the fight. Sabrina did not disturb him. She propped her cheek on one hand and watched him quietly as the minutes passed.

Meanwhile, on Shamouti Island in the central Orange Islands, Melody sat with Carol, the town mayor, and other islanders in a banquet hall. Their television showed the live broadcast from Pummelo Stadium. The same two presenters from the previous day were giving their final preview, with the bustling stadium behind them.

Melody sat cross-legged on her chair. As the start time drew closer, she called toward the kitchen. "Carol, hurry! It's almost starting!"

"Coming!" Carol emerged carrying several platters of fruit. She set one before her sister and smiled fondly. "Look at the clock, Melody. There's still plenty of time."

Melody grinned, plucked a grape, and popped it into her mouth. "Who do you think will win?" she asked around it.

"You mean, who do you want to win?" Carol asked, giving her a knowing look.

"Oh, stop teasing me." Melody was unusually flustered. "Of course I hope Ethan wins. He saved the world and helped us so much."

"Is that the only reason? It couldn't be because you like him?" Carol said. Then she grew thoughtful. "I don't know much about Pokemon battles, but I've heard Drake is formidable. He's beaten dozens of challengers since becoming Supreme Gym Leader."

Melody knew little about battling herself and had never paid much attention to tournaments. That did nothing to shake her faith in Ethan.

"So what?" She lifted her chin. "I know Ethan can beat him, whoever he is."

The mayor shook his head. "It won't be easy. Drake is called the strongest Trainer in the Orange League. Some say he could win a championship if he challenged a major League. Ethan faces a serious opponent."

Melody's expression fell. Realizing what he had done, the mayor hastily added, "But..."

"But what?" Melody perked up.

"Ethan is strong too. He stopped Lugia and won the Indigo League. Drake won't defeat him easily."

"Exactly!" Melody brightened. "If he sends out the three legendary birds, no one could beat him!"

"I doubt he'll use them," Carol said. "I haven't known Ethan long, but I don't think he'd reveal those three in a match like this. They'd draw too much attention and bring trouble."

Melody thought for a moment, then sighed. "You're probably right."

At the same time, another group was gathering around a television in Vermilion City, far away in Kanto. At Cerise Laboratory, Professor Cerise and his family sat with his assistants, Ren and Parker, to watch the broadcast.

Chloe had learned about Ethan's match the night before and told her parents at once. The professor's workload happened to be light, so he gave his assistants half a day off and invited them to watch with the family.

Elsewhere, Ethan's most devoted supporters, the biker gang, had gathered in a newly opened clubhouse. They were following the match too.

Chapter 266: The Match Begins

The sky was clear. Warm sunlight fell across Pummelo Stadium. At ten o'clock, the announcer's excited voice rang through the arena.

"Ladies and gentlemen, thank you for waiting! Welcome to the Orange League Winner's Cup! I'm your announcer for today's match. I know many of you arrived early, just as eager as I am to see this contest. The moment we've all been waiting for is finally here!"

As at the Indigo League, the announcer knew how to warm up a crowd. A few more remarks sent the spectators' excitement soaring. Then he turned to the entrances.

"Now let's welcome the pride of the Orange League, our undefeated Supreme Gym LeaderDrake!"

A man in a black vest walked slowly out of the competitors' tunnel, his arms bare and his expression resolute. The stands erupted. The cheers swelled until they seemed to strike Ethan even through the stone walls.

When they finally subsided a little, the announcer raised his voice again. "And now, please welcome the Indigo League championEthan Vale!"

In the tunnel, Ethan heard his name. He took a deep breath and rose. "It's our turn."

Haunter poked its head out of his shadow and floated beside him with a grin. "I've been waiting for this! They keep saying how strong that man is. Let's see which of us has the last laugh."

Trainer and Pokemon walked out through the stone-lined tunnel and into the stadium. A great cheer greeted Ethan too. It was not even half as loud as Drake's welcome, but the Orange Islanders were showing their respect to the Indigo League champion.

One spectator reacted differently. On the giant screen, a man past fifty saw Ethan's face and gaped. It was the fruit vendor who had wagered with him the day before.

"How can it be him?" The man rubbed his eyes. He had never imagined that the young customer would turn out to be Drake's challenger. His thoughts tangled together as he tried to make sense of it.

Following the match procedure, Ethan and Drake met at the center of the field and shook hands. Each studied the other. Drake was about Ethan's height, though broader and more solidly built. Years beneath the Orange Islands sun had given him a healthy bronze complexion. The determined set of his face added to his commanding presence.

Ethan noticed a necklace made from a Poke Ball hanging at Drake's throat. He guessed it held Dragonite, the man's ace.

Drake spoke first. "Welcome to the Orange Islands. Ever since I heard you were challenging the Winner's Cup, I've looked forward to facing you."

"I've looked forward to it too," Ethan said.

The announcer could not hear their words, but the sight of them together was enough. "What a meeting! The Orange League's strongest Trainer stands face-to-face with the Indigo League champion. Which of them will win? We're about to find out!"

Drake's fans waved signs and shouted his name. They expected a close battle, but were certain he would win. Some local women in the crowd, seeing Ethan for the first time, found themselves less certain about whom to cheer for. They had come to support Drake, yet the visiting champion was strikingly handsome. Perhaps they could give him a little encouragement too.

After their brief exchange, the Trainers returned to their command areas. Drake, as the defending Trainer, took the red side. Ethan stood on the green side.

The plain field split apart beneath them, revealing a rocky battlefield with a round pool at its center and smooth stones scattered around it.

The referee raised a flag to start the match. Drake immediately threw a Poke Ball.

"Go, Ditto!"

Blue light flashed, and a pale-purple Pokemon appeared before Ethan. Its small eyes peered out of a soft, elastic body that looked like a puddle of putty.

"There it is!" the announcer shouted. "Drake's Ditto has defeated many challengers over the years. How will Ethan Vale handle it?"

Ditto, just as expected. Drake had kept to his usual opening. Ethan had spent the night thinking through a response.

Ditto could copy an opponent's cellular structure in an instant, rearranging its own body to take on the same appearance and abilities. Battling one meant facing a version of your own Pokemon.

That would trouble most Trainers, but Ethan had two Pokemon whose imitation did not worry him. The first was Haunter. Even if Ditto became a Haunter with matching types and abilities, Ethan's partner had lived for more than two thousand years. Its battle experience and other powers would give it an easy advantage. Ditto could never reproduce the strange wind Haunter wielded, because it was not a Pokemon move.

As for the other...

Ethan threw a Poke Ball. "The first match is yours. Go, Gyarados!"

"Rrrroar!"

A flash of blue light revealed the enormous Gyarados. Its arrival showed the spectators in the stadium and watching on television what sheer presence looked like. Its roar drowned out the cheers of more than twenty thousand people.

Chapter 267: A Copy Is Still a Copy

The roar of Ethan's Gyarados stunned the Orange Islands spectators into silence. Many clapped their hands over their ears and stared at the fierce Pokemon.

What kind of Gyarados is that?

"Whoa!" Even the announcer was taken aback. His professional instincts returned quickly, and he leafed through his notes while he spoke. "According to our records, Ethan Vale's Gyarados showed astonishing strength at the Indigo League. It defeated many formidable opponents and left quite an impression. What will it show us today?"

In front of a television elsewhere, Masha, his older sister Misa, and the biker gang watched Gyarados's entrance with wide eyes.

"Oh, wow!" Masha shouted. "Sis, look! The boss's Gyarados is even fiercer than the last time we saw it!"

Misa studied the scars of different sizes across its body and nodded. "It must have been through a serious fight. It looks stronger. Is it just me, or has it grown larger too?"

In the stadium, even spectators safely beyond the battlefield felt a trace of pressure from Gyarados. Ditto, standing directly before it, felt far more. Under that cold, merciless stare, fear gripped it before the match had even begun.

Drake frowned. "Ditto, don't back down! Use Transform!"

Ditto nodded and forced itself to concentrate. Its soft body shifted and swelled, changing until it had Gyarados's form. The original's blood-red eyes still unnerved it, but at least it no longer stood frozen in fear.

The two Gyarados looked almost identical, down to their size and scars. Ditto had copied the outer form and abilities, but its eyes lacked the original's particular ferocity.

High on one side of the stadium, the scoreboard displayed the first Pokemon chosen by each Trainer and the result slots beneath their pictures. Seeing both sides ready, the referee stepped forward.

"Trainers, prepare yourselves!"

Ethan and Drake nodded. The referee brought down the flag, officially starting the battle. Neither Pokemon waited for a command. They charged toward the pool at the field's center, bent their bodies, and dove in.

Water-type Pokemon could make better use of their strength in water. Now that Ditto had become Gyarados, it understood that the pool gave its borrowed form an advantage too.

They surfaced almost together. Ethan struck first. "Gyarados, use Hydro Pump!"

He had skipped any cautious opening and chosen a powerful attack. Drake stayed calm. "Ditto, use the same move!"

Both Pokemon opened their mouths. Pressure built, then two huge jets of water shot toward each other. They collided in front of Ditto with a crash, burst apart, and rained down over the field.

Ethan had not expected that attack to decide anything. It was a distraction. Before the spray cleared, he called, "Gyarados, close in and use Aqua Tail!"

Gyarados sped across the pool. The first exchange seemed to have convinced Drake he could match Ethan move for move. He ordered Ditto to approach and use Aqua Tail as well.

Yet a question nagged at him. Ethan knew Ditto now shared Gyarados's Water type. Why keep choosing Water-type attacks? Even a clean hit would do reduced damage. An Indigo League champion should not make such a simple mistake.

He had no time to think it through. Both Pokemon twisted and swung their enormous, water-wrapped tails. The tails crossed in the air, forming a great X. Their impact boomed through the stadium. Water sprayed from them like a sudden downpour.

"What an attack!" the announcer cried. "When those tails struck, it looked as though rain fell over the field. Beautiful! So far, the two Pokemon appear evenly matched. Neither has gained an advantage!"

Privately, he wondered if Ethan's Gyarados was all show. It had looked fearsome, but Drake's Ditto was holding its own.

Ethan heard the commentary. Evenly matched? A slight smile touched his mouth. Ditto had stepped into his trap. There was no reason to hold back now.

"Gyarados, show them your strength! Use Thrash!"

Gyarados's eyes widened. It lifted its head and roared, letting fury drive out reason. Only one thought remained: tear apart the opponent before it.

Across the field, Drake's frown deepened. Thrash? Why choose a move that made Gyarados lose control? It might be powerful, but its wild attacks would be hard to aim and could leave openings. Worst of all, the move would confuse Gyarados afterward. If Ditto survived, Ethan would have handed Drake a helpless target.

Chapter 268: The First Win

While Drake weighed the risks of Thrash, Ethan's Gyarados opened its massive jaws. Saliva still clung to its sharp teeth as it lunged for Ditto's neck.

Drake had no time to dwell on his doubts. "Ditto, dodge!"

Ditto was already trying to evade. At his shout, it twisted aside just in time. Gyarados's jaws snapped shut inches from its neck.

But missing the bite did not put any distance between them. They continued struggling in the pool, churning water against the surrounding rocks and throwing up spray.

Then Gyarados lunged again and drove its head into Ditto. This time Ditto could not get away. It rocked under the blow and let out a pained grunt.

Drake saw the problem. Their Aqua Tails had brought them too close together. Ditto's copied body was too large to slip quickly out of Gyarados's reach the way a small Pokemon might. If it could not escape, it would have to fight at close range. Ditto had become a Gyarados too; Drake believed it could match the original's ferocity.

"Ditto, use Bite! Hold it in place!"

Ditto opened its jaws and bit into Gyarados's neck. With its mind lost in Thrash, Gyarados made no attempt to dodge. It roared and writhed as the teeth sank in.

"Good!" Drake's tense expression eased. For an instant, he thought Ditto had secured the advantage.

He and Ditto had underestimated Ethan's Gyarados. Driven by pain, it did not retreat. It twisted sharply even with its neck caught in Ditto's mouth and bit back into Ditto's neck. The two huge Pokemon coiled around one another like pythons. Each clamped down harder; neither would release its grip.

Ditto had copied Gyarados's body, but it had spent its life fighting orderly Trainer battles. It had never endured this kind of raw struggle, with an opponent willing to take injury to deal it. After only a few seconds, the pain became too much. Ditto opened its mouth.

"No!" Drake saw his Pokemon falling behind after just a few exchanges. "Ditto, use Thrash too!"

If Gyarados became so dangerous when it fought without restraint, perhaps Ditto had to do the same to stand against it. Ditto obeyed. Its eyes reddened, and it abandoned reason, striking wildly.

To everyone's surprise, it still could not move Gyarados. Ethan's Pokemon pinned it beneath its body. Ditto's struggles achieved nothing and brought on still harsher bites. Sharp teeth tore through the scales of its transformed body, and blood poured into the pool, turning a wide patch of water red.

Isn't this too much? Many spectators stared, horrified.

"Why? Why? Why?" The announcer stood and braced his hands on the desk, his voice almost a shriek. "They both look like Gyarados and they're using the same moves! Why can't Drake's Ditto fight back against Ethan's?"

The crowd asked the same question. From appearances, this was a contest between identical Pokemon, yet Ditto was plainly losing the close fight. The answer, perhaps, was clear only to Ethan.

His Gyarados was not winning with clever tactics or elegant moves. It was displaying the fury inside it and the brutal power it had learned to use. Ditto could copy its form, but not that experience. Ethan's Gyarados had fought through blood and pain. It was nothing like an ordinary Gyarados.

Ditto finally lost consciousness under the assault. Its borrowed form gave way, leaving its small purple body in Gyarados's grasp. Even then, Gyarados kept attacking.

"Enough! Stop!" Drake shouted.

Ethan looked at him. "You know Gyarados has lost control. It won't listen to anyone right now, not even me."

Drake knew he was right. He raised Ditto's Poke Ball, waited for an opening, and recalled it from across the field.

With the outcome decided, the referee raised the green flag to award the first round to Ethan.

"I can hardly believe it," the announcer said. "Ditto has defended Drake's opening position against so many challengers, yet it has fallen in this first round! Ethan's Gyarados was unstoppable. Congratulations to Ethan Vale on taking the lead!" There was a trace of reluctance in his voice.

Sparse applause came from the stands. Most spectators were still shaken by what they had seen. The broadcast carried the same images to Kanto, where the reaction was quite different.

At their clubhouse, the biker gang erupted in cheers. "He won! The boss is amazing!" "That Gyarados is incredible!" "So much for the Orange League's Supreme Gym Leader!"

There was laughter and celebration at Cerise Laboratory too.

Ethan recalled his still-raging Gyarados, giving it time in its Poke Ball to rest and recover from the effects of Thrash.

Across the field, Drake regarded him seriously. "You deserve that Indigo League title. Your Gyarados is the most... formidable one I've ever seen."

Chapter 269: Venusaur vs. Onix

Drake did not seem especially surprised that Ditto had lost. If Ethan had failed to defeat it, that might have surprised him more. A Trainer with such a powerful Gyarados deserved his championship title and had met Drake's expectations. The speed and brutality of Ditto's defeat, though, had caught him off guard.

Ethan answered his compliment politely. "Your Ditto is strong. Against most Trainers, it would be difficult to handle. It's unfortunate that it faced my Gyarados. It never had the chance to make full use of its abilities."

Drake fell silent. Ethan sounded sincere, but the words struck him as arrogant. Unfortunate that it faced your Gyarados? The man did not seem to know how to be modest.

Drake smiled faintly. Perhaps that was for the best. A strong, confident opponent rekindled the excitement he had not felt in a long time. After waiting so long for someone who might beat him, he had every reason to give this match his all.

He took another Poke Ball from inside his vest. "Then this is my next Pokemon!"

He threw the ball. Blue light flashed, and Onix appeared on the field.

"Oh! Drake's Onix!" the announcer cried. "It's given outstanding performances in past matches. Drake must be counting on it to even the score. Let's see if it can do it!"

The sight of a fresh Pokemon revived Drake's supporters. They shook off the disappointment of Ditto's loss and cheered for him again. Applause rolled through the stadium.

Whatever the announcer said about Onix, Ethan's path had become simpler after winning the first round. Drake had to send his next Pokemon out first. Ethan could then choose a favorable type. Against Onix, the decision was easy.

He threw another Poke Ball. "Venusaur, this one's yours!"

Venusaur landed with a heavy thud and fixed its gaze on Onix. Its broad body held its own against such a large opponent. Grass-type attacks would strike Onix, a Rock- and Ground-type, with four times their usual effectiveness. The matchup was straightforward.

"My, my," the announcer drawled. "Venusaur! It looks like Ethan intends to claim a second win with a type advantage. How convenient for him."

There was a pointed edge to his voice. As a Drake supporter, he seemed to think a champion from a major League should choose an even matchup instead of exploiting a weakness.

The speakers picked up the sound of pages turning at his desk. A few seconds later, he gave a small laugh. "According to these records, Ethan Vale made good use of type advantages throughout the Indigo League. He must have liked the results so much that he's brought the same style to the Orange Islands."

Ethan heard every word and remained calm. Going first or second, using a type advantage or fighting with the same typethese were ordinary parts of Pokemon battling and legitimate tactical choices. He would not complain if someone used them against him. The announcer was merely venting after Drake lost the first round.

Rather than fault Ethan, perhaps he ought to consider why Ditto had lost.

Ditto's picture dimmed on the scoreboard. Now Onix faced Venusaur. Ethan looked across at Drake, whose expression had not changed. Most of the spectators understood type matchups too and paid little attention to the announcer's complaint.

The referee saw that both Trainers were ready and waved the flag for the second round.

"Venusaur, use Vine Whip!" Ethan attacked first again.

Two vines rose from the center of Venusaur's flower and swept toward Onix.

"Onix, use Dig!" Drake ordered at once. He knew the matchup was unfavorable and did not intend to take a direct hit.

Whoosh! The vines cut through the air like green streaks. Before they could land, Onix broke through the ground and slipped down into a tunnel like an eel. It vanished so quickly that Ethan frowned. Pokemon that knew Dig were often fast underground, but this Onix had clearly practiced it. It disappeared before most eyes could follow.

Smack! Smack! The vines struck bare ground, kicking up dust and gravel without touching Onix's tail.

With his opponent out of sight, Ethan studied the battlefield again. Its rocky terrain suited Onix. It could travel beneath the ground and use the rocks, along with its similar coloring, to conceal its position. Venusaur would have trouble telling where it was.

Ethan had the type advantage; Onix had the terrain. For now he would have to wait for it to surface.

"Venusaur, don't stand still. Move toward the pool and watch the ground beneath you!"

Chapter 270: A Creative Opponent

Venusaur stood alert. The ground beneath it began to tremble, at first faintly, then hard enough to shake the entire field.

Bang!

Earth and broken stone burst upward as Onix erupted from below and struck Venusaur in the belly. The blow hurled it five or six meters into the air. Venusaur's size and weight slowed its ascent after another meter or two, but then it began to fall.

"Venusaur, steady yourself!" Ethan shouted.

Two vines shot from Venusaur's flower and braced against the ground as it came down. It landed on its feet rather than crashing onto its side.

Onix was still aboveground. "Now! Use Razor Leaf!"

Venusaur began to attack, but Onix slipped straight back into the hole it had made and vanished before the leaves could fly. Ethan could only marvel at its speed underground. Drake's Onix had left him almost no opening.

"Well done!" Drake smiled. "Keep it up. Let's do that again!"

The announcer sounded delighted by Venusaur's apparent trouble. "What a predicament! Ethan chose Venusaur for its type advantage, but what good is that if it can't hit Onix? A few more attacks like this and Venusaur will be worn down!"

The crowd cheered. Ethan shook his head with a rueful smile. The announcer did not have to make his preference quite so obvious.

Venusaur might seem unable to reach Onix, but Ethan had a plan. Why else had he sent it toward the pool? He glanced at the water behind his Pokemon and called, "Venusaur, channel the pool water into the hole Onix made!"

"What?" Drake stared. His gaze moved to the pool, and his face changed. He had battled with Onix in Pummelo Stadium countless times. Other opponents had always tried to fight it conventionally. Ethan was the first to use the battlefield's water against its Dig tactic.

Venusaur swung its vines like heavy hammers, striking the ground again and again. Soon it had carved a shallow channel between the pool and Onix's tunnel. Water ran through the channel and poured underground.

Gurgle, gurgle.

The pool looked small from above, but it was deep enough for Water-type Pokemon to battle in. Its volume was substantial, and Onix's tunnel was not long. The water quickly filled it, bubbling as it went.

Underground, Onix recoiled instinctively from the water. It dug deeper, but the water followed. With nowhere left to retreat, it had to surface.

Ethan had been waiting for that. "Venusaur, finish it now!"

The vines lashed out again. Onix had only just emerged and could not dive back underground. Venusaur caught it and wrapped the vines tightly around its body.

Smack! Creak!

The vines struck and tightened. Vine Whip was a basic Grass-type move, but against Rock- and Ground-type Onix, it still dealt heavy damage. Onix strained to tear the vines apart. Venusaur was strong too, and it dug its four feet into the soil like posts.

For a moment, neither Pokemon could pull the other loose. Ethan used the opening. "Venusaur, use Razor Leaf!"

More than a dozen sharp, curved leaves flew from Venusaur's flower. Onix could not move. Every leaf struck home. Grass was strong against both of its types, and the fourfold-effective attack knocked it to the ground.

The fall of Onix's huge body left the announcer gaping. The reversal had come almost too quickly to follow. Venusaur had appeared helpless; a moment later, it had beaten Onix.

"Onix is down!" he cried, dismayed. "Ethan Vale used the pool to force it out! What quick thinking and adaptability!"

The stands erupted. The audience had expected Onix's tough body to endure far more than two hits, even after seeing Ethan choose Venusaur. Now Drake, their undefeated Supreme Gym Leader, was unmistakably on the back foot. Ethan had won both rounds.

Many Orange Islanders began to worry about where the match was heading. Drake's face had turned grave as well. Another loss. He recalled the unconscious Onix and looked at Ethan with mixed feelings.

He had expected an exhilarating Winner's Cup battle, not to fall behind so decisively. Was this the strength of an Indigo League champion? Still, the score was only two to zero. He had four Pokemon left. The real battle was just beginning.

Chapter 271: Haunter's Triumph and Evolution

Drake recalled Onix and threw his third Poke Ball. Blue light flashed, and a deep-purple Gengar appeared, hovering above the field.

The announcer took a long breath and recovered a little from his disappointment. "Just as in his previous matches, Drake's third choice is Gengar! This is a powerful and dependable Pokemon. How will Ethan respond? Will he keep Venusaur in while it's still in good condition, or choose someone else?"

Even the announcer failed to notice that his voice no longer held the confidence it had at the start of the match.

In the Trainer's area, Ethan saw Gengar and recalled Venusaur. He had several Pokemon he could send against it, but he had made a plan the night before. There was no need to hesitate. He turned to the companion beside him.

Haunter turned toward him at the same moment. Their eyes met.

"Heh, heh, heh! Let me take this one!" Haunter laughed before Ethan could speak.

"That's what I had in mind." Ethan smiled and nodded. "I'm counting on you."

"Don't worry. I won't lose!"

Haunter drifted slowly out onto the field under thousands of watching eyes. The announcer sounded incredulous.

"What? Ethan is sending a Haunter against Gengar?"

The spectators were just as confused. Haunter was Gengar's unevolved form. Surely a Pokemon that had not evolved would be weaker than its fully evolved opponent in both ability and strength. Why choose it when Ethan had a formidable Gyarados and a Venusaur fresh from victory? Had two wins made him overconfident, or did he trust this Haunter that much?

Whatever the answer, they thought it was good news for Drake. At last he should win a round.

Drake was not so sure. The two Ghost Pokemon were pulling faces at each other, laughing, and chasing each other about as though they were old friends. Yet his research on Ethan told him that this was not a Trainer who would give away a match. Haunter must have something special for Ethan to choose it now.

When the referee restarted the battle, Haunter showed everyone what that something was.

Who said an unevolved Pokemon could not defeat its evolved form? A born prankster with power far beyond its opponent's, Haunter toyed with Drake's Gengar throughout the match.

They traded Shadow Ball, Dark Pulse, and Hex, but whatever Gengar used, Haunter answered with greater force and broke through it with ease. When Gengar grew tired and used Destiny Bond, Haunter saw through the attempt. It simply waited for the move's effect to end, then finished Gengar with a grin.

Haunter had made it look effortless. Drake's Gengar had not touched it once.

The referee raised the green flag and announced the result. The spectators stared at one another in silence. It took the announcer a long moment to find his voice.

"That... that was far too easy. No, the difference in strength was too great!"

The score was three to zero. Was this really the Drake the Orange Islands knew? Before the match, League officials had promised a fierce, evenly matched contest between two powerful Trainers. What the crowd had seen was nothing like that.

Ditto, Onix, and Gengar were all Pokemon whose strength the spectators had witnessed before. Today, against Ethan, they seemed to be fighting on an entirely different level.

Across the field, Drake looked at his unconscious Gengar and shook his head. The defeat had been absolute. Most troubling of all, Ethan had not given a single command. Haunter had fought freely and won through its own power and experience.

Drake quietly recalled Gengar.

"What a powerful Haunter," the announcer said. A question still bothered him. "But with strength like that, why hasn't it evolved? If it became Gengar, surely it would be even stronger!"

Haunter glanced toward the commentary booth.

Evolve? That's easy. What's so difficult about it?

It would show these people who thought they knew everything.

"Heh, heh, heh!" Haunter grinned widely. White light suddenly shone from its body. When the glow faded, it had taken on an entirely new form before the astonished eyes of Drake and the audience.

"It... it evolved?!" the announcer cried. He sounded baffled. Haunter had not appeared to gain enough experience during that short battle. How had it evolved now?

Ethan reacted differently. He covered his face with a hand. He understood Haunter's reason all too well. The show-off had chosen this moment just to see everyone's shocked expressions.

In its new body, the Pokemon strutted back to him. "Well? I didn't embarrass you, did I?"

It was Gengar now. Round and plump, it still looked mischievous but somehow a little cuter too. It did not seem used to its body yet, stretching its feet and turning its hands over to examine them.

Ethan studied its new shape. "You did well. Just don't do that again."

Gengar blinked and scratched its head. "What? I didn't make a big enough impression?"

"No." Ethan smiled ruefully. "You made too big an impression. Now there's no mystery left to my last trump card."

Chapter 272: Victory in Sight

"I'm your trump card?" Gengar eyed Ethan suspiciously. "I thought you always said those three foolish birds were your trump cards. Since when was it me?"

Gengar had just won him a match, and Ethan was eager to praise it. He pulled the newly evolved Pokemon close and smiled. "Nonsense. They're only the obvious choice. None of them are as thoughtful or dependable as you. You're my real trump card. No one else comes close."

Gengar had not yet learned how easily its Trainer could flatter someone. It grinned with pleasure. Behind its back, the three Great Balls on Ethan's belt shook furiously in protest. Ethan quietly laid a hand over them, trying to soothe the birds without Gengar noticing.

On the field, the referee crossed the red and green flags before his chest. "We will now change the battlefield!"

Under Orange League rules, the field changed after one side lost three Pokemon. The new terrain added variety for the audience and gave the trailing Trainer a chance to alter the course of the match. Both Trainers could take a brief rest while it was prepared.

With a lighter heart, Ethan went to a chair beneath a sunshade. He was closer to the stands now. The nearby spectators watched him with a mixture of hostility, curiosity, and awe. He could hear some of their unguarded comments, but paid them little attention. He drank water, leaned back, and closed his eyes to rest while he considered the battles still ahead.

Three more wins would earn him the Winner's Cup, the second trophy of his career.

The announcer continued filling the break. "It has been so long since we've seen the field change that I can't remember the last time. Ethan Vale has pushed Drake to this point. The Indigo League champion truly is a top-class Trainer!"

After Drake's three losses, the announcer was beginning to fear how the match might end. Praising Ethan now might prepare the crowd for the possibility of their champion's defeat.

Clack, clack...

Gears and machinery broke Ethan's train of thought. He opened his eyes. A lift lowered the rocky field, panels shifted, and a new grass field rose into place at Pummelo Stadium.

A small hill of piled rocks stood at its center. The rest was mostly level, but thick grass grew more than a meter high across it. The field looked as though it had not been used in some time.

"A grass field!" the announcer said. "It hasn't been tended lately, and look how tall the grass has grown. Usually this field is straightforward, but now smaller Pokemon can hide in the growth and strike from cover." He saw the referee signal. "The change is complete! Both Trainers have rested and had time to plan. Let's turn our attention back to the match!"

The audience looked away from the new field. Ethan and Drake were rising from their seats and returning to their command areas.

...

Elsewhere in Pummelo Town, three figures ran through streets still crowded with spectators, heading for the stadium.

"Misty! Tracey! Hurry up! I can hear the cheering!"

It was Ash, with Misty and Tracey struggling to keep up. Misty watched the boy in the cap ahead of her while she ran, breathing hard.

"Ash, wait for us! You're going too fast!"

"Misty's right!" Tracey called from behind. "And listenthe match isn't over. We can still make it!"

Ash looked at the stadium, now close by. He was desperate to get inside, but he slowed down a little.

Since parting from Ethan, the three had hurried to the Kumquat Gym. Ash had won there and earned his fourth badge. Then they had traveled day and night to Pummelo Island. Ash wanted to catch up with Ethan's progress, but more than that, he wanted to watch his match in person.

They entered the stadium and pushed into stands so full that spectators occupied the aisles. Just as they reached a place where they could see, Ethan's Scyther crossed paths with Drake's Venusaur and brought it down with a clean X-Scissor.

"Venusaur is down! It's four to zero!" the announcer cried. "Drake chose Venusaur for this grassy field, but even so, it couldn't withstand Ethan's Bug-type Scyther!"

Four to zero? Ash looked at the scoreboard. Four icons on Drake's red side were dark. Not one of Ethan's Pokemon had been defeated. Ethan was as strong as ever.

"That's wonderful! Ethan's going to win!" Misty's eyes lit up. She fixed them on Ethan, waved both hands, and called out her encouragement.

Her voice could not carry far through the noisy stadium, but some Orange Islanders nearby turned to look. They were surprised to see a girl in her early teens cheering so hard for the visiting champion.

Misty did not care. Even if Ethan could not hear her, she wanted to support him here, far from home, and cheer for the person she liked most.

Chapter 273: The Ace Takes the Field

The match had reached its fifth round. Drake stood on the brink of defeat and sent out Electabuzz. Ethan answered by releasing his Crystal Onix.

Sunlight struck Onix's strange body, and it gleamed like cut crystal. Drake, the announcer, League staff, and spectators in the stadium and at home were all astonished by its beauty. Cameras turned toward it at once. Flashes snapped as photographers tried to capture every angle.

"Is that an Onix? What a beautiful color!"

"Could it be a Shiny Pokemon?"

"A Shiny Onix doesn't look like that!"

People all around Ash exclaimed over the sight. He, Misty, and Tracey had glimpsed the Crystal Onix during the battle with the Gyarados at Rind Island's Lake of Birth and Evolution. They had been too busy then to study it. Now, seeing it clearly, they understood why everyone was staring.

"Incredible..." The announcer swallowed. "Shiny Pokemon are rare, but this isn't one. Its body must have changed in a unique way. What a marvelous sight! Where did Ethan Vale catch it?"

He hesitated, remembering something. "I've heard a story from Sunburst Island. People there searched for years for a legendary Onix with a body that shone like crystal. Could this be the very Crystal Onix they were looking for?"

The name gave the audience an answer. Among those watching the broadcast, the glassworkers of Sunburst Island were particularly excited. They had dreamed of finding that Pokemon. They had thought it might only be a story, and now they had seen it with their own eyes. Ethan had found what they had sought for so long.

As the first surprise passed and the audience focused on the match again, they saw another difficult round ahead for Drake. Crystal Onix was a Ground- and Rock-type. Its Ground attacks would deal double damage to Electabuzz, and Electric-type moves would not affect it at all.

Many spectators already feared a fifth defeat. Their feelings had swung from absolute confidence in Drake to doubt, and now to expecting the worst.

Once the battle began, Electabuzz struggled exactly as they feared. Without its Electric-type moves, it relied on Normal-type attacks, which barely troubled Onix's sturdy defense. Electabuzz had to stay alert and dodge each of Onix's blows.

It did have Low Kick, a Fighting-type move that could hurt Crystal Onix and keep it from being helpless. But relying on one useful attack became harder once Ethan recognized it and guarded against it.

In the end, Crystal Onix used Rock Tomb to drive Electabuzz into a corner. With nowhere left to dodge, it took a Rock Throw and fell. The result had seemed inevitable, but the fifth win still brought a fresh wave of disbelief.

Five battles, five defeats for Drake. Would Ethan repeat his undefeated run at the Indigo League without losing even one Pokemon here?

Having seen his earlier championship, many Orange Islanders no longer felt that Drake's loss would disgrace them. Ethan had done this to strong opponents before. If he won, it would show how powerful he was, not prove the Orange League weak.

With Electabuzz down, everyone knew Drake had only one Pokemon left: his ace, Dragonite.

Drake recalled Electabuzz and looked across the field at Ethan. "You're remarkably strong. Of all the challengers I've facedand even among the Trainers I knowyou're the strongest."

Ethan rubbed his nose and shook his head. "I wouldn't call myself the strongest. I know plenty of people more capable than me. My Pokemon are the ones who carried me through these rounds."

After several exchanges with Drake, Ethan had a rough sense of his level. He judged the Orange League's strongest Trainer capable of reaching at least the final four at the Indigo League. Drake was no lightweight.

Drake took no comfort from the compliment. He reached to the Poke Ball at his chest and detached it from its chain.

"Dragonite, it's your turn."

The ball stirred twice in his hand, as though answering him. The bond between them was plain.

"Go, Dragonite!"

Drake hurled the ball skyward. Blue light flashed, and Dragonite appeared above the field.

"At last," the announcer said, looking up. There was little joy in his voice now. "It's been a very long time since I've seen Dragonite battle. Is it as strong as ever? Let's see whether it can bring Drake a chance to win."

Ethan took his eyes off Dragonite and recalled Crystal Onix. He drew the last Poke Ball he planned to use from his belt. He had sent out five different Pokemon in five rounds. None was badly tired, but he did not intend to use any of them again.

Drake had chosen a winged dragon. Ethan would answer with one of his own.

He enlarged the ball in his hand, weighed it briefly, and pressed its button. Fixing his gaze on Drake, he called, "This battle is yours. Go, Charizard!"

Chapter 274: Dragonite vs. Charizard

Two dragon-shaped Pokemon, one yellow and one red, faced each other in the air above Pummelo Stadium, wings beating steadily.

The yellow one was Drake's Dragonite. Its round belly and broad body gave it an almost gentle look. Dragonite were rare, and the people of Pummelo Island revered one as their guardian. Spectators of every age loved the species.

The red Pokemon, though it looked more like the dragons of legend, was not a Dragon-type at all. Ethan's Charizard was leaner than Dragonite, with a fierce face, sharp claws and teeth, and a blazing tail. Each watched the other, eager to fight.

Beneath them, Ethan and Drake stood in their command areas with equally grave expressions. Neither could afford a lapse in attention. The stands had quieted too. Drake's supporters and the smaller number cheering for Ethan all knew how much this round meant.

If Ethan won, he would claim the Winner's Cup without losing a Pokemon and bring down the Orange League's undefeated champion. If Drake won, he would still have to defeat five of Ethan's Pokemon in good condition. The road ahead would be daunting, but a comeback would earn him extraordinary honor and give Orange Islanders something to boast about before Indigo League fans.

Some spectators who knew type matchups noticed that Charizard appeared to be at a disadvantage.

"Dragonite is a Dragon- and Flying-type," the announcer said, puzzled. "Charizard is Fire- and Flying-type. Dragon doesn't have an advantage over Fire, but Dragonite resists Fire-type attacks. This is the first time Ethan Vale has deliberately chosen an unfavorable matchup. Does he have a special plan?"

Casual spectators who rarely watched battles understood at last why the choice seemed odd. Given Ethan's earlier decisions and his calm expression, they assumed he must be thinking several steps ahead.

In fact, Ethan had no elaborate plan. He had a small team and did not want to send out a Pokemon he had already used. Dragonite could fly, so Charizard's ability to meet it in the air mattered more to him than the type disadvantage. Everyone was reading far too much into a simple choice.

Drake himself kept trying to guess Ethan's intention. When the referee declared the battle open and he still had no answer, Drake set the question aside and attacked first.

"Dragonite, use Water Gun!"

Dragonite could learn attacks of many types: Dragon, Flying, Normal, Water, Fire, Ice, Electric, even Psychic. That range had caught plenty of unfamiliar opponents off guard. Ethan knew enough to be ready.

"Charizard, dodge!"

Charizard rolled sideways in the air, avoiding the stream of water with ease. Dragonite used the moment to close in. Electricity crackled around one of its claws as it swung at Charizard.

Thunder Punch. So he wants a close fight?

"Charizard, answer with Dragon Claw!"

Blue light shone around Charizard's claws. The two Pokemon collided in the air, trading punch for claw. They struck dozens of times in only a few seconds, their limbs blurring and the wind whistling around them. The crowd roared its approval. At last Dragonite was giving them the kind of contest they had hoped for.

Both Pokemon took blows. Neither gained a clear advantage. Thunder Punch was doubly effective against Charizard, but Dragon Claw struck Dragonite's Dragon type hard in return.

Bang!

Their heavy tails collided, and the two separated, breathing hard.

Drake intended to keep up the pressure. Before Ethan could speak, he called, "Dragonite, use Thunderbolt!"

Bright electricity erupted from Dragonite's long antennae and spread outward, threatening to engulf Charizard. It was a powerful, wide-ranging attack. Charizard had no useful way to meet it head-on.

"Charizard, get down to the ground!"

Charizard plunged toward the grassy field and slipped beneath the spreading current. Yellow arcs hissed through the air and faded without touching it.

The thick grass, rooted deep in the ground, would not readily carry electricity. Ethan judged that standing there would reduce the reach and force of Dragonite's Electric-type attacks. Charizard would not be entirely safe, but that weapon was less threatening near the ground.

What a difficult opponent. Ethan and Drake reached much the same conclusion. They had traded attacks without either of them gaining the upper hand.

Looking at Dragonite, Ethan remembered what it had done in the anime. It could use more than a dozen moves across different types. It had beaten three of Ash's Pokemon in succession, only losing to Pikachu when its stamina finally ran out.

Drake's other Pokemon might be strong enough to reach the Indigo League semifinals, Ethan thought. This Dragonite could be strong enough to win the whole tournament.

Chapter 275: Pressing the Attack

"Charizard, our turn! Use Dragon Rage!"

Ethan had spent most of this battle reacting to Drake's attacks. With the two Pokemon separated, a brief opening appeared, and he seized it.

Charizard opened its mouth and fired a blue-green beam toward Dragonite. Hitting a moving target in the air was difficult, though. Dragonite beat its wings lightly and slipped aside before the attack reached it. The energy skimmed past and shot into the sky, piercing a low cloud before breaking into particles and fading away.

Drake saw Charizard's attack end. "Dragonite, use Skull Bash!"

Dragonite gathered speed and dived like a meteor, carrying all its weight toward Charizard. Charizard had landed only moments ago. It could not take off quickly enough to evade the charge.

"Don't dodge," Ethan called. "Take it!"

Dragonite came down with a rush of wind. At the instant of impact, Charizard turned sideways and caught one of its arms in both claws. Dragonite's momentum continued to drive them downward. Charizard's muscles strained as it swung Dragonite around and around like a windmill. After more than a dozen turns, Charizard staggered, but it had bled off the force of the dive. It let go and flung Dragonite across the ground.

Dragonite rolled through the grass and scrambled back to its feet. The thick growth had cushioned the landing, leaving it unhurt apart from a little dizziness and fresh grass juice smeared across its body.

Now! Ethan saw it struggling to clear its head. "Use Dragon Claw!"

Charizard rushed in, blue light gathering around both claws as it raised them.

"Dragonite, dodge!" Drake warned. Dragonite shook its head, but the moment of dizziness had cost it too much. By the time it looked up, Charizard's claws were already before its face. They struck its belly several times. Dragonite grunted and stumbled back.

Ethan knew better than to let it recover. "Use Air Slash!"

Dragonite spread its wings to take off. Charizard cut the air with its claws before it could rise. A crescent-shaped wave struck Dragonite in the abdomen.

Bang! Dragonite flew backward. The two successive attacks had drained a great deal of its strength.

Charizard breathed out, relieved to have finally gained an edge. It relaxed for a heartbeat. Dragonite rose from where it had fallen and fired Water Gun without warning. The stream struck Charizard squarely and drove it back a few steps.

Even after being knocked down, Dragonite's strength and quick reactions let it answer immediately. The balance shifted a little toward Drake again.

"Evenly matched!" the announcer said. "Both Pokemon are landing attacks. I thought Charizard had taken control and might end the battle, but it looks as though the fight will continue!"

Privately, he was disheartened. Even if Dragonite beat Charizard, how would it face Ethan's other five Pokemon after exhausting itself here?

On the field, Charizard and Dragonite steadied themselves and paused on opposite sides. Both were breathing harder now, but neither took its eyes off the other.

Drake looked pale after seeing Dragonite thrown to the ground. His hand trembled inside his sleeve. Am I going to lose? How long had it been since he last felt this helpless before a possible defeat? A year? Two? Even before becoming Supreme Gym Leader, he had rarely been pushed this far.

He looked toward Ethan. The Indigo League champion's expression remained calm. Drake could only acknowledge his strength.

A breeze moved across the field and stirred their clothes. It seemed to signal a new exchange. The Pokemon moved before either Trainer spoke.

But Drake's approach had changed. Dragonite's recent setback seemed to have shaken his confidence. He was no longer pressing in with relentless attacks. Instead, he kept Dragonite at a distance and tried to wear Charizard down with ranged moves.

"Dragonite, use Water Gun!"

Charizard dodged the stream. When it tried to close in, Dragonite retreated on Drake's command, always keeping a safe gap.

A change of tactics? A battle of attrition? Ethan raised an eyebrow. Drake was fighting more cautiously with every exchange.

Ethan directed several attempts to get close. Each time, Drake attacked while withdrawing and denied Charizard an opening. Drake might be content to fight that way; Ethan had no wish to prolong the match.

He scanned the grassy field and found an idea. If you want to hide from me, I'll leave you nowhere to hide.

"Charizard, use Flamethrower!"

"Fire?" the announcer wondered aloud when he saw Charizard preparing the attack. "Ethan knows Dragonite resists Fire-type moves. Why waste an opening on that?"

Ethan gave no answer. Charizard sent a stream of flame across the field. Dragonite did not even spend energy countering it; a beat of its wings carried it safely aside.

Whoosh!

Dragonite had escaped the direct attack, but stray sparks landed in the tall grass nearby. Flames spread, climbing higher by the second. The wind fed them, and the thick growth carried them onward. With a rising crackle, patches of fire joined until half the grass field was burning.

Chapter 276: The Finish

"The field's on fire?" The announcer still thought the blaze was an accident. He had not understood Ethan's purpose.

Ethan pointed toward the center. "Charizard, get onto that pile of rocks!"

Charizard beat its wings and landed atop the little hill made from a dozen or so boulders. It was the highest point on the field and the only place not covered in grass.

Crackle, crackle!

Fire spread quickly. Soon it threatened the entire field. Spectators in the nearest seats leaned away from the heat.

"It's so hot my face feels roasted!"

"The air's wavering. I can hardly see the battle!"

People fanned themselves, trying to push away the hot air.

Dragonite suffered too. It tried to climb into the sky, but smoke obscured its way. The air above the field was hotter still, stinging its eyes and making it choke. It had to descend again.

Drake frowned. The fire was too large for Dragonite's Water Gun to put out now. He had first assumed it was accidental. Ethan's composure told him otherwise.

So that's it. He's set the field ablaze to force a final confrontation. Dragonite might resist Fire-type attacks, but it could not remain in a spreading blaze indefinitely.

"Rrrroar!" Charizard called down from the rocky height. Dragonite had no attention to spare for it. Uneasy, it edged toward the few patches not yet burning.

The two Pokemon were accustomed to different environments. Charizard, like others of its line, was at home with volcanic heat and would feel this fire less. Dragonite lived around lakes and islands, its skin accustomed to damp sea breezes. The heat was harder for it to bear.

"Dragonite has nowhere safe to stay!" the announcer said, surveying the field. "Even if Charizard doesn't attack, it could soon be burned by the fire and heat!"

Drake knew it too. He had to finish the match before Dragonite was trapped. There was no point drawing it out any longer.

"Dragonite, stop running! While you still have room, settle this with one attack! Use Hyper Beam!"

Dragonite opened its mouth and gathered energy. Ethan saw that his plan had worked.

"Charizard, use Inferno!"

The fire cast both Pokemon in red light. Even their eyes seemed to hold flames. A few seconds later, they fired together. One beam was red, the other blue, and both surged across the field.

The announcer sprang to his feet. "This is it! Dragonite has used the immensely powerful Normal-type move Hyper Beam, and Charizard is answering with the advanced Fire-type move Inferno!"

He was so excited that he sprayed the microphone as he spoke and knocked over his water cup with an elbow without noticing.

The attacks raced past the midpoint. They did not collide. Instead, they passed side by side and struck the opposing Pokemon almost simultaneously.

Boom! Boom!

Dragonite fell backward through firelight and smoke. Charizard toppled from the rocks. Both hit the ground and lay still.

"Both attacks connected!" the announcer cried, his voice cracking. "With power like that, who can possibly stand up and claim victory?"

Several seconds passed without movement. Keeping clear of the flames, the referee stepped forward, ready to call a draw. Then Charizard stirred. It struggled, trembling, to its feet.
