"Charizard!" the announcer shouted. "Its fighting spirit burns as fiercely as its fire. It's the first to rise!"

Ethan let out a quiet breath. Hyper Beam was devastating, but Charizard had entered the exchange with enough stamina. It had survived with a little strength left, just as he had hoped.

The crowd turned to Dragonite. On the giant screen, they could see that its eyes were open. It had not fainted, but the aftereffects of Hyper Beam held it on the ground, unable to move.

Drake's face changed. He shouted encouragement, urging Dragonite to get up, but nothing worked. He could only watch Charizard stagger toward it.

Charizard stopped over the Pokemon it had battled so fiercely. A glimmer of respect entered its eyes.

"It's time," Ethan called. "Charizard, finish it with Dragon Claw!"

Charizard raised both claws and struck the defenseless Dragonite without hesitation. The super-effective blow knocked it unconscious.

"Dragonite is down!" the announcer's voice carried a sob across Pummelo Stadium. He had feared this result, but seeing Drake lose made it no easier to accept. Orange League supporters around him felt the same.

Their champion had lost to the Indigo League champion. To them, it felt as though Ethan had swept aside the pride of the Orange Islands.

In Kanto, the response was the opposite. Cheers erupted at Cerise Laboratory and at the biker gang's clubhouse.

"He won!"

Chapter 277: A Second Trophy

It was over. Drake had lost.

The referee raised the green flag for Ethan. On the scoreboard above them, every one of Drake's Pokemon icons went dark.

The announcer sighed, then spoke with genuine warmth. "Congratulations to Ethan Vale! He has defeated Drake and won the Winner's Cup. All six of his Pokemon gave outstanding performances today. He has earned the title of Honorable Trainer in every sense. Let's give him the applause he deserves!"

After seeing the six rounds, the announcer could no longer doubt Ethan's ability. The visiting champion had not won by luck. He had been plainly stronger, and even his easy smile made him hard to dislike. The crowd rose to applaud both Trainers for the match. Many who had watched today would become Ethan's fans.

On the field, Drake recalled Dragonite before the spreading fire reached it. Ethan recalled Charizard too. Gyarados had grown through its fight at the Lake of Birth and Evolution; this battle had brought Charizard growth of its own.

Staff members hurried onto the field and released seven or eight Squirtle. Their Water Guns soon put out the blaze. When the flames and smoke cleared, the grassy field was a muddy wreck. Its former shape was scarcely recognizable. Beneath the blackened remains, a few embers still glowed faintly red.

Drake approached Ethan and offered his hand, as he had before the match. "That was exhilarating. Congratulations on becoming an Orange League Honorable Trainer. Your place in the Palace of Victory is waiting."

Ethan shook his hand under the spectators' gaze. "Thank you. And... I'm sorry my Pokemon were too rough in a few rounds."

"Don't worry. It was a competition. We both fought to win." Drake hesitated, then added, "May I ask you something?"

"Of course. What is it?"

"All your Pokemon are so strong. How do you train them?"

Ethan scratched his head. "Mostly in the ordinary way. I don't have a special method." Seeing Drake's disappointment, he continued, "But I do have a suggestion. If you want your Pokemon to grow stronger, don't stay in one place. The world is wide. Travel, see more of it, and battle strong Trainers from elsewhere. That experience can help them improve quickly."

"Travel more..." Drake shook his head. He had once done exactly that. When he felt his strength had stopped growing, he returned to the Orange Islands and became Supreme Gym Leader. Ordinary travel no longer seemed enough to change him.

Ethan read his expression. "Speaking of strong Trainers, have you heard of Lorelei?"

"The Elite Four member from Kanto and Johto? Of course. Around these islands, everyone knows her name, from small children to the elderly."

"She's coming to the Orange Islands soon. If you want a real challenge, try battling her."

"Challenge Lorelei?"

"Yes. She's powerful. You'll learn plenty from that fight. I know her a little, so mention my name when you meet. I'm sure she'll give you especially thorough attention." Ethan grinned.

Drake thought it over, then nodded. "I will challenge her."

"Before you leave, though, don't go right away," Ethan added. "I think a boy may come to challenge you in a few days. I'd like you to beat him first."

"A boy?" Drake could not see why a child merited special attention.

"About ten years old. Don't underestimate him because of that. He's strong."

"Is he your friend? How will I recognize him?"

"He wears a cap, and a Pikachu follows him everywhere. He can look a little foolish, but he's more capable than you'd expect."

Drake took the warning seriously. "Then I'll do everything I can to defeat him. And when we next meet, I'll be stronger too. I'll be the one challenging you."

Ethan had heard promises like that many times. "I'll look forward to it."

They exchanged contact details in a friendly spirit. Carl approached with the trophy. Drake took it from him, held it carefully in both hands, and presented it to Ethan.

"Congratulations again. You earned this."

Ethan accepted the trophy with a smile and raised it high. Streamers burst around him, and camera flashes caught the moment.

The Orange League did not give him another champion's title, but the trophy mattered to Ethan all the same. Whatever the level of competition, it was the second trophy of his career.

Carl applauded gently. "Congratulations on becoming our newest Honorable Trainer."

For Carl, the day brought both good news and bad. His publicity efforts had given this Winner's Cup its highest television audience ever. Viewers across many regions had learned more about the Orange League.

But the Orange League had lost. Its strongest Trainer had been beaten six to zero by the champion of a major League. To outsiders, it confirmed the Orange League's reputation as a small competition beneath the others. Carl feared that image would be difficult to shake for a long time.

Chapter 278: Celebrations and Interviews

After Drake and Carl left, a staff member carrying a camera came over to Ethan.

"Mr. Vale, the League needs a photograph of you with the Pokemon you used today. Could you bring them all out?"

"Of course." Ethan thought over the six rounds. None of his Pokemon had been badly injured; they could all move freely and did not need urgent treatment. They had enough energy for a picture.

He released the other five. With Gengar already beside him, all six were present. At the photographer's direction, Gyarados and Crystal Onix took positions in the back. Their height gave the picture a commanding background. Charizard and Scyther stood to Ethan's left, Venusaur and Gengar to his right.

The Pokemon held their heads high around him. Ethan raised the Winner's Cup trophy in the middle, smiling as broadly as his partners.

Click!

The staff member took the photograph. "Once it's developed, we'll display one copy in the Palace of Victory and give another to you. One last thing: which of your Pokemon would you like us to make a statue of?"

Ethan looked around. All six had performed well, making the choice difficult. After thinking it over, he picked Charizard. Defeating Drake's strongest Pokemon meant something special, and their battle had been both dramatic and memorable. Charizard was the right choice for the statue.

They discussed the details. The staff member brought out a clay slab that had not yet dried and took impressions from Ethan and each of his Pokemon. Once that was finished, Ethan was free to leave the field. He went through the competitors' tunnel toward the lounge.

He sighed. The victories might have looked easy, but the match had worn him out. Now he could finally relax.

Pop! Pop! Pop!

As he opened the lounge door, three streamers shot out from behind it and draped over him.

"Ethan, congratulations!"

He looked up to find Ash, Misty, and Tracey laughing at him, spent party poppers in their hands.

"Ash? When did you three get here?"

Ash rubbed his nose. "During the fourth round. We wanted to arrive earlier, but we were too late."

Misty looked at Ethan warmly. "You beat the Supreme Gym Leader. You're amazing!"

In high spirits, Ethan indulged in a rare boast. "It was nothing. I could have faced them with my hands in my pockets and still won!"

Show-off, Sabrina thought, giving him an amused glance.

Ethan entered and handed the trophy to Sabrina. Looking down at the streamers all over his clothes, he felt a warmth in his chest. His friends had thought to prepare this little celebration for him.

Ash and the others gathered around Sabrina to inspect the cup. It stood about twenty centimeters high and was coated in gold paint. A golden Poke Ball with two small wings crowned it. The whole thing looked heavy and gleaming.

Ash stared at it, hands clenched with longing. He did not need to envy Ethan for long; his own chance to challenge for this trophy was next.

While the others talked excitedly, Ethan took out his phone. The screen was crowded with missed calls and congratulatory messages sent as soon as his friends had seen him win. Professor Cerise, Chloe, Ren, Parker, Misa, Masha, Melody, Carol, Lorelei, Professor Ivy, and many more had reached out.

He sat on the sofa and began answering them one by one. Then Carl returned and knocked on the door.

"Sorry to interrupt the celebration," he said when he entered. "Reporters are waiting outside. Would you be willing to speak with them?"

A post-match interview was customary, but Carl remembered the awkward scene at the entrance. This time he had decided to ask Ethan before arranging it.

Ethan could hardly refuse now. Having won and drawn so much attention, he ought to say a few words. He followed Carl to the interview area. Cameras turned toward him, and flashes began to pop again.

He greeted the reporters politely, composed rather than gloating. One asked at once, "Mr. Vale, how do you feel about winning the Winner's Cup without losing a single Pokemon?"

Ethan considered his words. "An undefeated result wasn't something I set out to achieve. I just played each round as it came, and this was the outcome."

It was an answer that gave the reporters little to pick apart. They asked many more questions, and he answered each carefully. Then one asked, "What do you think of Drake?"

Ethan knew a careless answer could anger Orange League fans. He thought for a moment. "Drake is very strong, one of the best opponents I've faced. If he competed in the Indigo League, I think he could at least finish as runner-up."

"If you rate him that highly, why only runner-up?"

Ethan meant to stay modest, but a smile slipped through. "Because however hard he fights, the champion would still be me."

The reporters stirred. That was a bold claim from someone who had seemed so restrained. Yet Ethan had won both the Indigo League and the Orange League's Winner's Cup, and he had just beaten Drake. They could hardly say he lacked the results to support his confidence.

Chapter 279: Setting Out for the Next Region

After leaving Pummelo Stadium and walking down the hill, Ethan took Ash and the others to the guesthouse where he was staying.

It was already past four in the afternoon. The spectators were gradually dispersing, but plenty still lingered in the streets, reliving and discussing the match. To avoid being recognized, Ethan pulled on a hat and glasses and covered himself up again.

That was the price of attracting so much attention. If he wanted to be left alone, he had little choice.

Once the group entered the guesthouse courtyard, Ethan spent generously on a table full of food and drinks from the landlady.

The most important thing after winning a championship was to celebrate, and what better reward could there be than a lavish meal?

By evening, it was dark outside. Ethan and the others let the larger Pokemon out into the courtyard and prepared food for them separately. The smaller Pokemon joined their Trainers around the low table on the tatami mats.

Under the bright lights, the room was packed and wonderfully lively.

The landlady had evidently seen Ethan win on television. Delighted to have such a famous guest staying with her, she brought out some complimentary sushi and a few bottles of sake.

Dish after dish covered the table, each giving off a tempting aroma. With everything ready, it was time to eat and drink to their hearts' content.

"Cheers, everyone!"

They clinked their cups and drank. Apart from Ethan and Sabrina, everyone stuck out their tongues afterward. Ash and Misty looked especially uncomfortable, and even Tracey's brow creased. None of them cared for the taste of alcohol.

"Cough... It's so strong!" Ash clutched his stomach, looking miserable. It felt as if every blood vessel in his body were on fire.

"Honestly, you three..." Ethan laughed at their faces. "This sake is already pretty weak. If you aren't used to it, why not just drink juice? You insisted on trying it, and now none of you can handle it!"

"What's wrong with that?" Misty said, ignoring his teasing tone. "We wanted to try it because we're not used to it. Besides, today is worth a drink!"

Everyone knew one another well, so the celebration was in full swing as soon as dinner began.

Ash ate as he always did, wolfing food down as if he had not had a meal in years and giving no thought to appearances. Beside him, Sabrina took small bites and chewed slowly, every bit the lady.

Gengar pulled faces and put on a show to entertain them. Tracey told funny little stories in a low voice from the other side of the table, while the Pokemon laughed and played. The sake even emboldened Ethan to tease Misty a few times, making her blush. For a while, laughter filled every corner of the room.

Halfway through dinner, with food in their stomachs, everyone finally slowed down.

"Oh, Ash," Ethan said, "if you want to challenge for the Winner's Cup too, don't forget to register tomorrow. The rules here say you can't challenge Drake without arranging it in advance."

Ash gnawed on a chicken leg. "I know," he said through a mouthful. "I'll go tomorrow!"

A mischievous look came into Ethan's eyes. "I've already spoken to the Supreme Gym Leader about you. I made sure he knows who you are, so he'll give you a proper welcome. All you have to do is enjoy the battle!"

"Oh, really?" Ash had no idea Ethan had set another trap for the sake of his growth. He thanked him with an innocent smile. "Thanks, Ethan! I'll work twice as hard!"

From across the table, Misty asked, "Ethan, what are you going to do next?"

"Head for the next region, of course," Ethan answered without hesitation.

"The next region?" Ash's eyes lit up. "Where are you going?"

"Probably Johto, to compete in the Silver Conference. It's the nearest one."

"Huh? You're not staying to watch my match?"

"I don't think so." Ethan shook his head. "I believe you can win. Besides, you're catching up to me too quickly. You're improving so fast that I need to leave ahead of you if I want to keep my lead!"

The unexpected vote of confidence lit a fire in Ash's heart. He was deeply touched.

After dinner, Ethan borrowed a karaoke machine from the landlady. Sabrina went off to enjoy the hot spring on her own, while the others sang enthusiastically until late.

They kept singing past eleven. Even after Ethan washed up, he was still too excited to settle down. He spent the rest of the night with Sabrina, keeping them both awake until the early hours.

The next morning, Ash had to leave early to register for his challenge.

Before going, he said his final goodbye to Ethan. "After I finish the Winner's Cup, I'm going back to Pallet Town to give the GS Ball to Professor Oak. Once that's done, I'll come find you in Johto!"

"Then good luck with your challenge. See you next time." Ethan rubbed his eyes, still half asleep. "And say hello to your mom and Professor Oak for me!"

Ash and Tracey left. Misty, as usual, kept turning back, reluctant to go.

Ethan watched until they disappeared, then returned to his room for another nap. By the time the sun was high, he and Sabrina had packed, checked out, and left the guesthouse.

Before setting out for the next region, however, Ethan had one last errand: he needed to visit the fruit stall where he had made that bet.

They followed the streets to the beach they had visited before. When they reached the stall, they found that the vendor had already packed up all his oranges.

The man looked distinctly awkward at the sight of Ethan. After all, he had said that Drake could never be beaten. Now the young man standing in front of him had eliminated Drake six to zero. How could he not feel uneasy?

Ethan broke the silence first. "Hello there! Remember me? I've come for my oranges, just as we agreed!"

"Of course, of course. I have them ready."

The vendor gestured to one side, where more than a dozen boxes of oranges were packed and waiting.

"Ha! I'm only joking. How could I possibly eat all that?" Ethan waved his hands. "Three boxes will be enough. Keep the rest. Besides, I don't want a headline saying, 'Shocking! Indigo League Champion Hides His Identity to Swindle an Old Man Out of His Oranges!'"

"Well..."

The vendor knew Ethan was joking, but he hesitated. He had lost their wager fair and square and had prepared to pay up. He had not expected Ethan to change his mind.

Seeing this, Ethan smiled. "It's fine. Any more than that would just go to waste. Let's do it my way."

"All right." The vendor nodded, then ventured another request. "Um... could I ask for your autograph? My grandson is a big fan of yours!"

"Of course."

Ethan took the pen and quickly signed the autograph board. Then he asked, "What's your grandson's name?"

"Achi."

Without lifting his pen, Ethan added: To Achi: May you become an outstanding Trainer!

The vendor accepted the board with both hands and put it away carefully. Then he lifted three boxes of oranges and offered them to Ethan.

Ethan had no need to carry them himself. A red Pokemon descended from the sky, and Charizard easily gathered all three boxes into its arms.

After saying goodbye to the vendor, Ethan soon found a shipping company. He handed the oranges over and paid to send one box to Cerise Laboratory for everyone to share. He sent his Winner's Cup trophy and the photograph of himself with his Pokemon along with it.

Since he had no permanent home, his room at Cerise Laboratory had become the place where he kept his trophies. Some of his personal belongings and his Indigo League trophy were piled up there too. With Chloe looking after them, he had no fear of anything going missing.

Ethan sent a second box of oranges to Erika. In their earlier chats, she had mentioned how much she loved plants. These oranges tasted so good that he wondered whether she might be able to grow them in her greenhouse.

Once that was done, Ethan and Sabrina climbed onto Charizard and headed for the Orange Islands' big city. From there, they could take a cruise ship directly to Johto and avoid a long detour.

With that, Ethan's adventures through the Sevii Islands and the Orange Islands officially came to an end. The journey had given him wonderful memories and a wealth of rewards.

Chapter 280: Registering for the Silver Conference

Hooooonk!

With a blast of its horn, a small passenger ship slowly pulled into the harbor at Cherrygrove City in the Johto region.

Cherrygrove was known as the city where the scent of flowers drifted through the air. Despite its favorable seaside location, it was small and its economy had never developed much. It had only a modest dock, with just a few regular boats coming and going each day.

Fortunately, a direct route connected it to the Orange Islands' big city. Ethan had been able to make the journey without changing ships.

He and Sabrina disembarked. The moment his feet touched land, Ethan stretched his whole body.

"Ahh! More than thirty hours on a boat. I'm exhausted!"

Sabrina shot him a look. "Exhausted? Other than a few battles with the Trainers aboard, didn't you spend most of the trip lying in bed? How can you be tired?"

Ethan worked the soreness out of his back and grinned. "That's why I'm tired. A little ship like that rocks with every wave. You can't get a decent night's sleep. It only leaves you feeling worse."

Gengar poked its head out of Ethan's shadow. It looked around and sniffed the air. "So this is Johto? It's my first time here, but the air and the buildings don't seem any different from Kanto's!"

"They don't look very different," Ethan agreed. "The two regions are on the same landmass. The Indigo Plateau sits between them, so they're treated as separate regions. Even the Johto League is based on the Indigo Plateau, just like Kanto's."

Gengar shook its head. "Then why not put them together? The Indigo League, the Silver League... all these names are such a bother."

Ethan patted its head, amused. "Come on. They're still two regions. Pay attention and you'll notice plenty of differences. Johto's air is clearly damper than Kanto's, and it has a more rural feel. Buddhism and Shinto are also very prominent here. The local customs aren't the same at all."

"Huh?" Gengar gave him a strange look. "How do you know all that?"

Ethan spread his hands. "I wasn't only sleeping on the boat. I leafed through a travel guide when I got bored. That's where I learned it."

There was more he had not said. He vaguely remembered that when the Pokemon games' maps were designed, Kanto was modeled on Japan's real Kanto region, while Johto drew on Kansai and part of Shikoku. Their roots really did lie close together.

But that knowledge touched on his secret of coming from another world. He could hardly reveal it casually. Sabrina was his girlfriend, and Gengar was his Pokemon; both were utterly loyal and probably would not mind knowing. Still, the fewer people who knew, the better.

As they talked, they walked into Cherrygrove itself.

It was an ordinary city in southern Johto with no Gym of its own. True to its name, its greatest charms were the scent of flowers and the sea breeze. Flower beds stood throughout the city, filled with lovely blooms. Most were shades of red or pink. Ethan knew too little about plants to name many of them.

Erika would recognize them all if she were here, wouldn't she?

He and Sabrina followed the streets past a few quiet high-rise buildings toward the Pokemon Center. The residents seemed to take life slowly, and hardly any vehicles passed them.

Now that Ethan had reached Johto, his first order of business was to register for its League challenge. Until his information was entered, he could not challenge the Gyms here or compete in the Silver Conference.

They crossed at a zebra crossing and entered the Pokemon Center. At the sound of the door, Nurse Joy looked up from the papers she was sorting at the front desk. She stood with a smile.

"Welcome! How may I help you?"

Ethan had long since given up wondering why the Joys and Officer Jennys could be found everywhere. Wherever he went, there they were.

"Hello, Nurse Joy. I'd like to register for the Silver League challenge."

"Certainly. May I have your Pokedex?" She held out her hand.

Ethan gave it to her and watched her connect it to the computer. While he waited, he looked around the Pokemon Center.

Compared with those in Kanto, it had nothing unusual about it. The decor and facilities were much the same, and even Nurse Joy's uniform looked identical. Apart from Ethan and Sabrina, only one other Trainer was there, eating at a table. This quiet little city really was out of the way.

At the computer, Nurse Joy saw his records and covered her mouth in surprise. "Oh! You're the Indigo League champion? I didn't expect you to come challenge our Silver League! Let me see... You just won the Orange League trophy too? Two trophies in such a short time! That's amazing!"

"You're too kind. My partners did the hard work," Ethan said modestly. "It wasn't really down to me."

"Now you're being too modest." Nurse Joy laughed behind her hand. "How could a Pokemon's growth have nothing to do with its Trainer?"

When the registration was complete, she returned his Pokedex and gave him a new case for collecting badges.

"Welcome to the Silver League! Do your best. I'm rooting for you, and I hope you win the Silver Conference too!"

Even Nurse Joy thought so highly of him. Ethan smiled and shook his head as he left the Center.

His second order of business was to contact Looker. He remembered Looker's earlier call, asking him to get in touch as soon as he reached Johto so he could help deal with Team Rocket there. So much time had passed that Ethan hoped he had not delayed anything important.

He and Sabrina found a restaurant. Over lunch, Ethan called Looker. The phone rang for a long time before the call was answered.

Before Ethan could speak, Looker asked, "Hello? Have you reached Johto?"

"Just arrived. I called you as soon as I could."

"Excellent!" Looker sounded delighted. "We've just finished clearing out Team Rocket in Kanto. We're moving our people into Johto now and should have everyone in place within a day or two. I was about to ask for your help!"

Ethan's eyebrow twitched. "What exactly do you need me to do?"

"Take down the Team Rocket bases with the heaviest defenses, of course." Hearing Ethan's tone, Looker laughed. Then he lowered his voice conspiratorially. "I'm in New Bark Town on a special assignment. If you have time, come meet me here first."

"New Bark Town?"

Ethan froze for a moment. Wasn't that right near Cherrygrove City? What a coincidence!

"All right. Wait for me there. I should be able to reach you by tonight!"

Chapter 281: The Girl with the Baseball Bat

According to the map, New Bark Town lay east of Cherrygrove City. It was not very far in a straight line, but a large forest stood between them. The dense growth and the many wild Pokemon living there made the road difficult to travel.

For new Trainers, though, the forest was a paradise. After receiving their first Pokemon, they could test their skills by catching weaker wild Pokemon there and building up their teams.

Ethan and Sabrina left Cherrygrove and followed a country path eastward.

What kind of assignment had brought Looker all the way to New Bark Town? It was only a little town on the outskirts. Apart from Professor Elm's laboratory, there was nothing there that Team Rocket would want to target.

Had someone raided Professor Elm's lab?

As Ethan wondered about that, he and Sabrina enjoyed the scenery and spotted many wild Pokemon along the way. Johto was so close to Kanto that Pokemon moved freely between the two regions. Pidgey and Raticate still poked their heads out of the woods here. There were also plenty of Johto Pokemon: Sentret, Hoothoot, Ledyba, and Spinarak.

After they had walked for some time and entered the area around New Bark Town, Ethan noticed a girl at the edge of the forest. She was trying to catch a Sentret.

As they approached, he saw that she was pretty. She wore a yellow-and-black striped jacket, a white baseball cap, and shorts that reached high on her thighs, leaving her pale legs bare. Her hair was tied into two long, low ponytails. She held a baseball bat and was shouting with all her might. His first impression was that she must be an ardent fan of some baseball team.

A Chikorita stood in front of her, pawing at the ground with its forefeet. It seemed ready to charge and deliver a final blow to the Sentret.

Chikorita's body was pale green, with a dark green leaf on its head and a ring of buds around its neck. Its head was large in proportion to the rest of its body, and it had red eyes, four short legs, a single nailed toe on each foot, and a small tail.

It was the first Chikorita Ethan had seen in person. Curious, he took out his Pokedex and pointed it toward the Pokemon.

"Beep! Chikorita, the Leaf Pokemon. A Grass type. Gentle by nature, Chikorita loves basking in sunlight. It uses the leaf on its head to sense the temperature and humidity around it and find a warm place. Though it may wave its leaf to threaten an opponent, the sweet scent it releases calms them both down."

As one of Johto's three starter Pokemon, Chikorita was certainly popular. Ethan, however, had little interest in raising one.

The Sentret had already taken several hits and was running low on strength. It was searching for a way to escape when it caught sight of Ethan and Sabrina. Its eyes brightened, and it ran straight toward them without hesitation.

"Chikorita, don't let it get away! Use Tackle!" the girl shouted.

Chikorita prepared to charge, but Sentret had already darted behind Ethan and Sabrina. The little Pokemon hesitated and stopped.

That moment was enough. Using the two travelers for cover, Sentret plunged into the forest beside them and vanished.

"Ah! My second Pokemon!" The girl dropped to the ground like a deflated balloon, watching her nearly captured Sentret get away.

Ethan glanced back toward the place it had disappeared. What a clever Sentret. It had even known how to use them as a shield.

He shook his head and started to leave. He had taken only a few steps when the girl shouted behind him, "Hey! You two, don't go!"

She had scrambled back to her feet and came trotting over. Ethan turned to face her.

"What is it?"

"You have the nerve to ask?" she said angrily. "If you hadn't suddenly shown up, that Sentret never would have escaped!"

Her voice was clear, with a strong Kansai accent.

"That's my fault?" Ethan shrugged. "You're the one who couldn't catch it. If you'd knocked it out sooner, it wouldn't have had a chance to run away."

"I don't care! You got in my way!" She waved her baseball bat around, fierce in a way that was more cute than threatening.

Sabrina frowned at the girl's unreasonable behavior and was about to teach her a lesson. Ethan hurriedly caught her hand before the young Trainer could get hurt.

After a second look, he had recognized the girl from her clothes. She had appeared several times in the animated series, and he remembered her fairly well. That was one reason he was not angry. He was a grown man; there was no point in quarreling with a little girl.

He considered her for a moment, then smiled. "What would you like us to do about it?"

His smile made the girl's cheeks turn red instead.

Wow, he's handsome. And he looks even better when he smiles!

She had not meant any harm. She was frustrated that a Pokemon she had almost caught had escaped, and seeing Ethan simply walk away had annoyed her. She had rushed over without thinking. Now that he was speaking to her so politely, she felt rather embarrassed.

Seeing her stumble over her words, Ethan said, "You look like a new Trainer. Maybe that Sentret just wasn't meant to be yours. If you still want it, you could try your luck in the forest and see whether you find it again. Or you could look for a different Pokemon to catch."

"I... I'm sorry. I got carried away."

She lowered her head and fiddled with her baseball bat as though she intended to examine every inch of it.

"It's all right." Ethan waved it off. When she remained silent, he took that as the end of it. "Good luck with your catching. See you!"

He and Sabrina turned and walked away without looking back.

"Oh? G-goodbye..."

The girl answered automatically. She watched Ethan go until he disappeared. Only then did it occur to her what she had missed, and she smacked herself on the head.

"You big idiot!"

At her feet, Chikorita looked up in curiosity, its little head full of confusion. Before it could work out what its Trainer meant, the girl sighed again.

"I didn't even ask his name. Oh well. We'll probably meet again. I'll ask him next time!"

Chapter 282: Silver

New Bark Town: the town where the winds of a new beginning blow.

If Kanto's starting town, Pallet Town, was a cozy little place, New Bark Town was practically a small city by comparison. In the games it had been a remote town with few residents. Now that Ethan had set foot here, he found it far larger and more prosperous than Pallet Town.

It had tall buildings as well as shopping centers, schools, a hospital, and even an amusement park. The Johto Pokemon League had built a Pokemon Center here especially for new Trainers. Yet, like Cherrygrove City, New Bark Town was quiet. Few people were out on the streets, and those who were moved at an unhurried pace.

As Ethan and Sabrina walked toward the town center, it was clear that she was still thinking about the girl they had just met.

She glanced at him. "I've noticed you're always especially friendly to cute girls. Misty, Chloe at Cerise Laboratory, Melody in the Orange Islands, and now that girl. You don't have some peculiar preference, do you?"

The hairs on Ethan's arms stood up.

Danger. Danger. Danger!

"Hey, don't say things like that!" His heart hammered as he hurried to deny it. "They're my friends, or like little sisters to me. I've never had any improper thoughts about them. And didn't you see how well I treated Ash? I like helping younger Trainers. How does that give me a peculiar preference?"

Sabrina studied him for a long moment. "Really? I was only making an observation. Why do you look so nervous?"

"I'm not nervous!" He wiped the cold sweat from his forehead, trying to look unconcerned. "It's just a dangerous question. If someone overheard you, they might think I was a bad person!"

Fortunately, Sabrina did not press him. Ethan breathed a little easier.

Hadn't she asked something similar before? Had his behavior lately been too brazen? Had Sabrina noticed something again? A psychic girl's sixth sense was a frightening thing.

To keep her from asking more, Ethan quickly took out his phone and contacted Looker again. They agreed to meet at a coffee shop, which Ethan found easily with the map on his phone.

It was a small shop on a street corner. Evening was approaching, but it was not yet dinnertime, and hardly anyone was inside. As soon as Ethan entered, he spotted Looker skulking in a corner.

Looker wore his usual trench coat, but he had added sunglasses and a wide-brimmed hat. If he had not been the only customer, and if his build had not been so familiar, Ethan might not have recognized him.

Ethan dropped into the seat opposite him. "Long time no see. What is this? Is someone following you?"

"Keep your voice down!" Looker removed his glasses, revealing a face so haggard that he looked as though he'd been overindulging for weeks. He glanced toward the server and put a finger to his lips. "No one's following me. I'm following someone else."

"Following someone?" Ethan looked him up and down. "Come on. Dressed like that, you'd stand out in any crowd. They'll notice you at once!"

"I don't have much choice." Looker spread his hands. "Team Rocket has circulated my picture everywhere. Practically all of them know my face. Without a disguise, they'd recognize me even faster."

Looker noticed Sabrina and nodded in greeting. Before Ethan could say anything else, he raised his eyebrows at him with a leering expression.

Well done, kid. You really did win Sabrina over!

Ethan had no trouble reading him. He glanced at Sabrina, then answered with a look and a lift of his own eyebrows.

What can I say? I'm irresistible.

Oh, please. When did you get so shameless?

What would you know, you lonely old bachelor?

It was the kind of wordless understanding men shared. Sabrina could not possibly follow their exchange. She stared at them, puzzled by all the eyebrow waggling. Both looked rather indecent, and she was sure they were discussing something offensive.

Her expression grew more suspicious, and her gaze more dangerous. Ethan hastily cleared his throat and turned to Looker with a serious face.

"Back to business. Who exactly are you following here?"

Looker pointed through the window. "Look over there."

Across the street, inside a restaurant, a man was eating dinner alone with his back to the coffee shop. He wore a tight black top and dark jeans. Ethan could not identify him from behind, but one thing stood out: his medium-length red hair.

"Who is he?"

"Giovanni's son," Looker said quietly.

"Giovanni's son?"

Sabrina froze. She and Giovanni had been Gym Leaders for years, yet this was the first she had heard that he had a son. Ethan frowned too. Did Looker mean Silver?

So that was why Looker had been so secretive, and why he had traveled all the way to New Bark Town: he was tailing Silver.

"Surprised?" Looker asked when he saw their faces. "We only found out recently that Giovanni has a son. The sly old man hid him well."

Ethan tried a question. "So you're following him in the hope that he'll lead you to Team Rocket?"

Looker nodded. "His name is Silver. I intend to use him to find Giovanni's hiding place. And I suspect he has a scheme of his own for coming here."

Ethan sighed softly. Looker was bound to be disappointed. Silver did not get along well with Giovanni. Though he was Giovanni's son, he disapproved of his father's withdrawal and hated what Team Rocket did. He wanted to make his own way, had almost nothing to do with the organization, and generally acted alone.

Ethan could hardly tell Looker any of that. Besides, he wanted to see what Silver was doing in New Bark Town himself.

Chapter 283: Following Silver

Ethan doubted Silver would leave the restaurant immediately, so he waved over the server and ordered a quick meal for himself and Sabrina, with two coffees.

The server soon brought several sandwiches with different fillings, along with vegetable salad, fries, and chicken nuggets.

While they ate, Looker gave them a general account of the operation the International Police were about to carry out.

He spoke openly, apparently unworried that Ethan or Sabrina might leak his plans. The two had been a great help in Kanto, after all. The International Police could not clear out Team Rocket with their own forces alone. They needed the Pokemon League and Trainers like Ethan to help them.

It took four sandwiches to fill Ethan up. Just as he reached for a fifth, he saw Silver rise from his table across the street and leave the restaurant.

To Looker's amazement, Ethan demonstrated the rare skill of eating a sandwich in three bites. He stuffed the rest into his mouth, hurried to pay with his cheeks bulging, and left the coffee shop with Sabrina and Looker.

The evening air was cool at this time of year. A breeze passed over them, raising goose bumps on their skin. The streetlights were already on, but there were fewer and fewer pedestrians.

Silver walked northwest through town at an easy pace, seemingly unaware that anyone was following him.

As Looker made to slip after him, Ethan caught his arm.

"No. It's dark, but the streets are nearly empty. If we stay close enough to follow him, he'll spot us."

"I know," Looker said with a helpless shrug. "But if we wait, he'll be out of sight. I came in a hurry, and there are no other International Police officers nearby to work with me. I'll have to take the risk."

In Ethan's old world, they would probably use some piece of surveillance technology. Here, though...

"We have another option." Ethan smiled and looked down at his shadow. "This is exactly when you ask a Pokemon for help. Gengar, you heard us, right? I'm counting on you."

Gengar's head rose out of the shadow. It gave a wicked chuckle. "Of course. Shall I abduct him, or wait until no one's around and kill him?"

Ethan stared at it.

"Please..." He sighed, exhausted already. "Just follow him quietly and see where he's going and what he's doing. Do you have to make it sound so horrifying?"

Ethan could have sent Charizard to watch from above, or asked Sabrina to keep track of Silver with her psychic powers. After considering his options, he had decided Gengar was the best choice.

"Ha! I know," Gengar said with a laugh. "I was only joking to lighten the mood!"

"Who feels like joking now?" Ethan shook his head. "Leave markers along the way so we can find you. Above all, don't let him notice you, and don't play any pranks on your own initiative."

Gengar patted its chest. "Leave it to me!"

Before their eyes, its body slowly turned transparent, as though it were vanishing into thin air. Soon they could not see it at all. A light breeze brushed past them, and they knew Gengar had silently gone after Silver.

Looker watched the direction they had gone with envy. "You have so many powerful, dependable Pokemon. That's impressive."

"Why don't you catch a few more yourself?" Ethan asked. "I think you only have a Growlithe and a Croagunk. Isn't that dangerous for an International Police officer?"

"I can't manage it," Looker said with a smile. "My assignments keep me busy every day. I don't have time to catch more Pokemon, much less raise and care for them. I may have only two, but both are partners I can count on. They're enough for me."

The three waited until Silver was out of sight, then slowly followed the route he had taken. They saw neither Silver nor Gengar along the way, but every few dozen meters a clear arrow appeared in a conspicuous place, keeping them on course.

The sky darkened as they crossed most of the town. At last, they reached a large walled property.

Inside stood an imposing three-story building with a round tower. It looked like some kind of research facility.

Looker brightened, as though his suspicions had been confirmed. "Professor Elm's laboratory! I knew it. This is Team Rocket's target!"

He scanned the area, but no matter how carefully he looked, he could not find any other Team Rocket members.

Professor Elm... Ethan rubbed his chin, trying to recall what he knew. Elm was Johto's Pokemon professor and provided starter Pokemon to new Trainers. He also seemed to have studied under Professor Oak. Beyond that, Ethan remembered little.

Gengar's head appeared above the courtyard wall. It beckoned to Ethan and whispered, "Over here! That Silver fellow went into the grounds!"

With no one else nearby, Ethan asked Sabrina to stay outside and watch their backs. Then he and Looker climbed over the wall.

As soon as they landed, they saw Silver crouched among some distant bushes, staring fixedly at the three-story building. They hurried behind a large tree before he could spot them.

Silver clearly had no idea that someone was watching the watcher. He would never have guessed that his every move was under observation.

The grounds remained quiet. After watching for some time, Looker finally voiced his confusion. "How strange. Why is Silver alone? Where are the other Team Rocket members?"

Ethan was silent for a moment. "Perhaps there aren't any. I think he's acting on his own this time."

"On his own?"

Looker's face fell. If that was true, he had spent all this effort for nothing.

Chapter 284: The Arrest

Silver had clearly studied the grounds and Professor Elm's laboratory before coming here.

From a distance, Ethan and Looker watched him check both sides to make sure no one was around. He hurried to the building, forced open a window, and climbed inside. The whole maneuver was so smooth and efficient that Ethan could not help admiring it.

He exchanged a glance with Looker. They crept to the window and peered in, showing only the tops of their heads. Inside a research room full of delicate instruments, Silver took one of three Poke Balls lined up on a machine and slipped it into a pouch at his waist. Then he carefully returned the way he had come.

Ethan and Looker hurried back behind the tree before he emerged.

Silver did not bother hiding his tracks. He left the window open and walked straight out of Professor Elm's grounds without a pause.

Ethan turned to Looker and whispered, "What do you want to do? Should I stop him?"

Looker had frowned through the entire theft. Lost in thought, he did not answer for a long while.

Was that really it? How disappointing!

Given Team Rocket's usual methods, and the fact that Silver was Giovanni's son, Looker had assumed he must be the organization's second most important figure. He had imagined Silver arriving with Team Rocket members to carry out a spectacular raid. At the very least, he had expected them to strip the whole laboratory of Pokemon and research material.

Instead, after all the effort of following Silver here, he had witnessed a simple theft. Just one Poke Ball! When had Team Rocket become so unambitious?

You're the son of Giovanni, the Earth Gym Leader. Where are your standards? Your pride? How is this any different from the work of a petty thief?

"Let's wait," Looker said at last. He still could not let go of his theory. "I feel there's more to this. Follow him and see where he goes."

Ethan signaled for Gengar to keep tracking Silver. He and Looker went back over the wall and met Sabrina outside.

Silver walked directly out of New Bark Town toward Cherrygrove City. They followed for a long time. The moon climbed high, and stars filled the sky. When they were nearing the forest where Ethan had met the girl with the baseball bat, Looker finally ran out of patience.

"We can't let him keep going," he said with a sigh. "I read too much into it. He didn't come here to lead some major Team Rocket operation. He came to steal a Pokemon from Professor Elm, plain and simple."

Ethan looked at him. "So what now?"

Looker thought it over. "We have two choices. We can leave him alone and keep following him. Perhaps he'll eventually lead us to Team Rocket's hideout. But that could take a great deal of time and effort, and if he has other things to do, we'll be following him forever."

"The odds of that working are slim," Ethan agreed.

"Then we take the second option. Arrest him and question him. That should tell us everything we need to know."

Ethan weighed the choices and nodded. "All right. We have a Team Rocket member in front of us. No reason to let him walk away. Let's move."

They whispered over a plan. Ethan hurried ahead through the forest and caught up with Silver beside a lake, blocking his path.

"Who are you?" Silver demanded with a frown.

Before Ethan could answer, footsteps sounded behind him. Silver turned and saw a man and a woman approaching from either side. The three had him surrounded. They had plainly come for him, and their intentions were anything but friendly.

Looker held up his identification. "Silver, I'm with the International Police. You're under arrest. Come with us quietly. Don't resist and get yourself hurt."

"The International Police?" Silver did not look frightened. He sneered. "What crime have I committed that calls for you to arrest me?"

"If we're watching you, you can assume we know where you come from," Looker said. "You just left Professor Elm's laboratory. Must I tell you what you did inside?"

Damn it. When did they notice me?

They knew exactly what he had done at the laboratory. Silver realized they must have been following him for some time. If he wanted to get away, he would probably have to fight.

He took out the Poke Ball he had stolen and spun it around one finger.

"Fine. The Poke Ball is right here. If you want to take me in, let's see whether you can manage it!"

He had scarcely finished speaking when the Poke Ball lifted itself from his finger. It shot toward Sabrina, who caught it neatly in her hand.

"What?"

Silver stared at her in shock. Then recognition seemed to strike him. His pupils contracted as he looked her over.

"It's you! The woman with psychic powers!" His face paled, and he turned to Ethan. "Then you must be Ethan Vale. People in the organization keep talking about a man and a woman causing trouble for us everywhere. I never thought I'd run into you!"

A few ordinary International Police officers would not have worried him. This pair was different. They had made a fearsome reputation within Team Rocket. Elite units and even the four Executives had fallen to them. For ordinary members, merely hearing their names was enough to make their skin crawl.

What rotten luck. Why did I have to meet them?

Silver dropped any thought of underestimating his opponents. He took several Poke Balls from his belt. Three flashes of blue light followed, and three Pokemon appeared before them.

Ethan looked from one to the next: Sneasel, Zubat, and Gastly.

They were already good Pokemon. Why had Silver bothered stealing a starter from Professor Elm's laboratory?

Chapter 285: Suicune

"Just as I thought." Looker raised a hand as Silver refused to surrender. He released Growlithe and Croagunk. "I knew you wouldn't come quietly. Don't blame us when we use force to arrest you!"

Ethan was only helping Looker, not leading the arrest. As the battle began, he sent out Gardevoir to join Gengar, which was already beside him. The two guarded his side in case Silver tried to escape past him.

On the opposite side, Sabrina released Alakazam and the Slowking she had caught in the Orange Islands.

"Growlithe, Flamethrower! Croagunk, Sludge Bomb!"

Silver shouted his own orders. "Sneasel, Icy Wind! Zubat, Air Cutter! Gastly, Shadow Ball!"

Five Pokemon clashed. Attacks flew through the air, and cries of anger and pain echoed over the forest. Wild Pokemon that had been peering out from cover scattered in fright.

Silver had three Pokemon to Looker's two, giving him a slight numerical advantage. But with Ethan's and Sabrina's Pokemon guarding the sides, Looker did not have to hold back. He pressed Silver from the start.

Boom! Boom!

Explosions followed one another. Thick trees toppled under the continuing attacks.

Ethan watched for a while. When Zubat had just finished an attack, he told Gardevoir to use Confusion. Caught off guard, Zubat lost control of its flight and slammed into a rock.

With one opponent disrupted, Looker had less to contend with. His Pokemon drove Silver's back step by step.

Tch. Silver grew increasingly irritated. The International Police officer was difficult enough on his own. The other two were watching every escape route.

He could not win by staying in this fight. It was time to get away.

Gritting his teeth, Silver ordered all three Pokemon to attack together. They forced Looker back for a moment, then turned and charged toward a gap beside Ethan.

Ethan saw what he intended. He waved a hand. Gengar and Gardevoir struck together, and the difference in power sent Silver's Pokemon back.

"Damn it!"

Silver changed direction and tried Sabrina's side. Team Rocket's stories said she was formidable, but to him she was still a woman. Even with psychic abilities, surely she would be easier to overcome than a Psychic-type Pokemon.

He had chosen the most dangerous route of all.

Sabrina did not need either of her Pokemon to stop him. Her hair lifted as she released her own psychic power. An overwhelming force swept into Silver and his Pokemon and knocked them all to the ground.

Bang!

Silver's head swam. He was hurt and pale, but what shook him most was the power he had just felt. How could this woman be so strong? It seemed beyond anything a human should be able to do. Escape now looked impossible.

"Stop struggling," Looker said. He brought Growlithe and Croagunk closer. "Those two are far beyond what you can handle. Give up and come quietly."

A cornered beast might still fight, but Silver had not yet grown into the dangerous Team Rocket figure Looker had imagined. He could not break through the cage they had made around him. After the short, intense battle, Sneasel, Zubat, and Gastly were covered in injuries and had little strength left.

Looker took out handcuffs and stepped forward to arrest him.

At that moment, a brilliant light flared above the lake beside them. Everyone turned.

As the glare eased, Ethan narrowed his eyes. A Pokemon stood at its center, on a rock rising from the water. The remaining light hid its features, but he could make out a four-legged shape, flowing hair, and two long ribbon-like tails. It looked graceful even in silhouette.

"What Pokemon is that?" Looker called, shielding his eyes.

The light faded. Its shape became clear, and the sight startled them even more. There was no mistaking it.

"Suicune!" Looker cried.

The Pokemon's body was blue-green with white markings. A crest like a blue jewel adorned its head, and two pale streamers trailed behind it.

According to legend, Suicune had once been one of three unknown Pokemon that died in the fire at the Burned Tower. Ho-Oh restored them to life, and they became Suicune, Entei, and Raikou. Because of Ho-Oh, they were also known together as its guardians.

Looker still seemed unable to believe what he was seeing. "Why would the legendary Suicune appear here?"

No one answered. Ethan had seen and even caught several Legendary Pokemon, yet the sight of Suicune still thrilled him. He quickly took out his Pokedex and pointed it at the Pokemon.

"Beep. Suicune, the Aurora Pokemon. A Water type and one of the Legendary Pokemon. Gentle as a spring, it seeks places where clear water flows. It races over the land with gliding movements and can instantly purify dirty water. The north wind is said to rise when Suicune appears, and it runs away with that wind. It is called the embodiment of the north wind."

Suicune regarded them with calm eyes. When their gazes met, they understood why it had come. This powerful Pokemon valued peace. It had heard the fight and believed Ethan's group was disturbing and damaging the forest, so it had appeared to stop them.

Chapter 286: Professor Elm

Suicune opened its mouth. Thick white mist rolled in from every side and covered them all.

It came so quickly and was so dense that Ethan could see less than a meter ahead. The others vanished from view. Only the faint outlines of Gardevoir and Gengar beside him kept him from panicking.

Both Pokemon drew close and took up protective positions, watching the fog for danger.

"Sabrina! Are you all right?" Ethan called toward where she had been standing.

"I'm fine," she answered steadily.

Her voice came from the expected direction. The mist had blocked their sight, but it had not confused their sense of where the others were.

Looker called from the other side. "I'm fine too, but I can't see Silver. There were a few flashes of red light just now!"

Red light? That usually meant someone was recalling Pokemon into their Poke Balls. Was Silver using the fog to escape?

Ethan nodded to Gengar. "Blow this mist away, quickly!"

"Got it!"

Gengar drew a deep breath and blew upward. A strange wind swept across the clearing, scattering the white mist.

As soon as they could see again, they looked toward the place where Silver had fallen. He was gone.

"Damn it! He escaped while we were blinded!" Looker's face fell. He searched the surrounding trees, but the forest was too dense to show which way Silver had gone.

For Looker, losing Silver meant his operation had failed. After all the effort of tracking him, they would leave empty-handed. The disappointment hit hard.

When they turned back to the lake, Suicune had disappeared as well.

"Why would Suicune help Silver?" Looker said bitterly, punching a tree.

Ethan shook his head. "I don't think it meant to help him. It wanted to stop the fight. It would probably have done the same no matter who was involved."

He addressed the Pokemon around them. "Don't just stand there. Spread out and search. Find Silver or Suicuneeither one."

Gardevoir and Gengar went in different directions. Sabrina's and Looker's Pokemon joined the search. More than half an hour later, they returned without finding a trace of either one.

"Forget it," Looker said with a sigh. "They're long gone. This operation was a complete failure."

"Not entirely." Ethan glanced at Sabrina. She still held the Poke Ball she had taken from Silver. "At least we recovered Professor Elm's Pokemon."

Looker paused, then nodded. That made him feel a little better.

The three recalled their Pokemon and retraced their way through the forest to New Bark Town. It was past eleven at night. The town was silent, its streets empty.

Professor Elm's laboratory looked peaceful as ever. Apparently no one inside had discovered the break-in.

Looker yawned. "The professor must be asleep by now. There's no rush. We can return the Pokemon in the morning. Let's find somewhere to rest."

Ethan agreed. They chose the Pokemon Center near the laboratory instead of walking farther to a hotel.

Nurse Joy had been about to sleep. She came out to greet them in pajamas, with a face mask on.

"Sorry to bother you so late," Ethan said. "Were you already in bed?"

Seeing her dressed like that, he wished they had gone on to a hotel.

"It's all right," Nurse Joy said with a wave. "Pokemon Centers are here for Trainers. Plenty arrive late at night. I'm used to it."

She arranged two dormitory rooms for them. Staying at a Pokemon Center was convenient and free, but the rooms were separated by gender. Ethan had no choice but to share with Looker while Sabrina slept elsewhere.

Since Ethan and Sabrina had begun sleeping together, they had not spent a night apart. He had grown used to her presence beside him. Lying alone now felt strange.

One hour apart from Sabrina. I miss her.

One hour and five minutes apart. I still miss her.

After the lights went out, Looker talked with him for a long while. They moved from Pokemon to work, then from work to battles. Looker shared stories from his time with the International Police. Ethan learned things he had never heard before, and the conversation brought them closer.

He still slept poorly. The space beside him felt empty all night. When morning finally came and he saw Sabrina again, the hollow feeling vanished.

The three ate breakfast at the Pokemon Center, then visited Professor Elm.

By daylight, the laboratory was much livelier. Researchers moved busily through its halls. They found Elm in a workroom.

He looked about twenty-seven or twenty-eight. He wore a white lab coat and semicircular glasses, and his unusual hairstyle stuck out like a sea urchin.

They introduced themselves. Only after they explained what had happened did Elm realize that a Pokemon had been stolen from his own laboratory.

Ethan handed him the Poke Ball. Elm looked at it and sighed. "Totodile's Poke Ball. What kind of professor am I? I didn't even know a Pokemon was missing."

"Don't be too hard on yourself," Looker said with a smile. "You've been working so hard that you hardly stop to eat or rest. New Bark Town is usually safe, and this thief had clearly studied the laboratory and your routines. You had no reason to be on guard."

Despite the reassurance, Elm still looked troubled. "It was my responsibility, no matter how it happened. Thank you for stepping in. If you hadn't, the children starting their journeys from here in the next few days would have been disappointed."

Chapter 287: Turning Down the Offer

Professor Elm turned his attention to Ethan.

"You said your name was Ethan Vale?" He adjusted his glasses, sounding excited. "I know that name!"

He knows my name? Ethan was not surprised. Pokemon competitions were enormously popular. Television, short videos, and websites had carried stories about him more than once. People even talked about his unbeaten record in schools, workplaces, and shopping centers.

Kanto and Johto shared a landmass and kept in close contact. The excitement over the Indigo League had not entirely faded. Ethan might not be known in every household here, but most people had probably heard of him.

Then Elm said, "You're the young man who published a paper with Professor Oak and Professor Ivy recently, aren't you? I read it. Your theory about the butterfly effect was especially good!"

Oh. That's how he knows me.

Of course a Pokemon professor would follow academic work more closely than tournaments. Ethan knew his own level of expertise. He smiled awkwardly and shook his head.

"I'm embarrassed to take credit. I have no research experience or qualifications. I only benefited from working with two professors. I made an offhand comment, and I never expected them to put it in the paper."

"You're too modest," Elm said with a laugh. "Two leading professors wouldn't both value the opinion of just anyone. Your idea really did offer a fresh perspective."

Grateful that they had recovered the Poke Ball, Elm warmly invited them into a reception room for tea.

As they talked, Ethan learned that Elm had studied at Celadon University and had been one of Professor Oak's outstanding students. He regarded Oak as both a great teacher and his most respected rival.

Elm set down his cup. "I also heard that you're an assistant to Professor Cerise?"

"I'm not really an assistant." Ethan gave another rueful smile. "I'm a field investigator at the lab. I mostly run errands. Do you know Professor Cerise too?"

"Of course!" Elm's expression grew nostalgic. "Our field is large in some ways and small in others. We work in different regions, but I've had many academic exchanges with Professor Cerise and Professor Ivy."

Ethan nodded. Come to think of it, Oak, Cerise, Ivy, and Elm all had names linked to trees or plants, despite working in different regions.

He sipped his tea. He knew little about tea leaves and could only tell that it was some kind of green tea.

Looker asked, "Professor Elm, what is your field of research?"

Elm straightened in his chair. His eyes lit up. "Pokemon Eggs!"

"Pokemon Eggs?"

What was there to study about them? Before anyone could voice the question, Elm continued.

"That's right. From what I've observed, all Pokemon hatch from Eggs! The shells usually have distinctive patterns, and those patterns can help you guess what kind of Pokemon is inside."

"All of them hatch from Eggs?" Looker was startled. He had never heard that before. He had learned something new already.

"Surprising, isn't it?" Elm had clearly expected that reaction. "Some Pokemon look like mammals, but they hatch from Eggs too. And here's something remarkable: a Pokemon inside its Egg can already see the world outside before it hatches!"

Elm grew more animated with every word. Professors seemed unable to stop talking once they reached their own specialties.

"Oh, that reminds me!" He turned to Ethan, his eyes brightening again. "Since you recovered my Pokemon, would you like to choose a Johto starter? Unlike Kanto's, ours are the Grass type Chikorita, Water type Totodile, and Fire type Cyndaquil. They're all excellent Pokemon!"

Ethan declined politely without hesitation. "Thank you, but please save that chance for a new Trainer. I already have Kanto's three starters, and their roles would overlap. With too many Pokemon, I couldn't give each one the care it deserves."

"The three starters. That's an interesting way to put it."

Elm did not mind the refusal. Ethan's willingness to pass up a free Pokemon showed him that this Trainer was different from those who only wanted to catch more and more.

They stayed a little longer. Assistants kept coming in to report to Elm, and it was clear he was busy, so they said their goodbyes. Elm insisted on walking them all the way to the entrance before returning to his work.

Watching him go, Looker said to Ethan, "You made a good impression. I could tell he liked you even more after you turned down the starter."

"Did I?" Ethan rubbed his nose. "I didn't notice."

Looker patted his shoulder and leaned close. "Being on good terms with him could benefit you. Someone in our organization told me Professor Elm may have a Master Ball. That must be precious to a Trainer."

Professor Elm has a Master Ball too? Ethan's eyes widened. He had not expected the scholarly professor to own an item Trainers dreamed of having.

He wanted one himself, but he barely knew Elm and had no real hope of obtaining it. With a wistful sigh, he shook his head.

"A Master Ball can catch a Pokemon without fail. It doesn't just tempt Trainers. Anyone would want one."

They chatted a little more. Then Looker checked the time and said goodbye.

"I'd better go. Silver was lucky to get away this time, thanks to Suicune. Team Rocket won't be so lucky next time. In ten days, we plan to attack their base in Mahogany Town. I hope you and Sabrina will join us."

Chapter 288: Casey, a Bundle of Energy

"Mahogany Town in ten days..." Ethan made an okay sign. "You can count on us!"

"Then I'll see you there."

Looker waved goodbye and walked away, disappearing alone around the street corner. Once again, only Ethan and Sabrina remained.

Ethan stretched and said lazily, "Well, that's settled for now. I can finally start challenging Johto's Gyms!"

"Which one will you challenge first?" Sabrina asked.

Ethan took out his phone and opened the map. "As the crow flies, the closest Gyms are Violet Gym in Violet City and Blackthorn Gym in Blackthorn City. The road to Blackthorn is harder to travel, but it's close to Mahogany Town. If we go there, we won't have to double back much to meet Looker."

Sabrina nodded. "So you're going to Blackthorn City?"

"No. I think I'll go to Violet City and challenge Violet Gym first."

Sabrina stared at him. What was wrong with this man's head? Why explain the convenient route to Mahogany if he intended to go the other way?

Amused by her expression, Ethan brushed her cheek and laughed. "That's exactly why I don't want to go there first. It's too close to Mahogany. Once I finish the Gym battle, we'd have nothing else to do. Violet City is in central Johto, with roads leading in every direction. It makes a convenient starting point. We can go to Blackthorn later."

His leap in reasoning had almost thrown Sabrina off balance, but she had no objection to the plan. In truth, even if Ethan decided to settle down here for the rest of his life, she would stay beside him.

It was nearly noon. After eating lunch nearby, they left New Bark Town and headed west, planning to pass through Cherrygrove City again on their way to Violet City.

They were in no hurry. They walked at an easy pace, talking and enjoying the scenery.

As they passed a forest divided by a river, Ethan read an online description of the area. Thousands of Heracross and Pinsir lived there. It was an excellent place to catch Bug-type Pokemon. Ethan had little interest in either species, though, and already had Scyther. He did not go into the forest.

At its edge, they spotted the girl they had met the day before.

She was trying to catch a Pidgey. Strangely, as she battled, she delivered her own play-by-play commentary and cheered herself on.

"Chikorita, this is the deciding inning! We absolutely can't lose! Go, go, go!"

Urged on by her cheers, Chikorita swayed the leaf on its head and fixed Pidgey with a serious stare. More than a dozen leaves, sharp as blades, gathered above it and shot toward the bird.

Whoosh! Whoosh!

The glowing green Razor Leaves struck Pidgey again and again. It had started dodging, but most of the leaves still hit. Unable to bear the pain, Pidgey wobbled, lost its balance, and fell onto the grass.

"Nice work!" With victory in sight, the girl held her baseball bat to her mouth like a microphone. "Can our starting pitcher Chikorita make the wild Pidgey swing and miss for a strikeout? Let's follow up and settle it with a fastball! Chikorita, use Tackle!"

Ethan stopped to watch. He knew nothing about baseball, its terms, or what a strikeout meant. But he knew she was a fan, and she had turned the Pokemon battle into a baseball game in her mind.

At her command, Chikorita charged. Just as it reached Pidgey, the bird recovered, flapped its wings, and lifted lightly out of the way of the Tackle.

"Aw!" The girl sighed, then rallied at once. "Too bad, but that's all right! The first pitch was a ball, and we've still got a second one. Chikorita, Razor Leaf again!"

Chikorita sent another volley of leaves into Pidgey. This time the bird ran out of strength. It dropped from the air and lay unconscious.

"We did it!"

The girl cheered, pulled out a Poke Ball, and threw it at Pidgey with a proper baseball pitch.

Tap! Red light flashed as Pidgey went into the ball. It shook only a few times before the capture was complete.

"Yes! I caught Pidgey!"

She bounced in place, full of energy. Perhaps she was too happy to contain herself. She began swinging her arms in rhythm and burst into song.

"Let's sing a victory song to celebrate! Here we go: Riding the winds of Mt. Mortar, here it comes with Quick Attack! Light Screen, Thunder Punch! Leer, leer, Electabuzz! Go, go, Electabuzz!"

Ethan had no idea what to make of it. He knew Mt. Mortar was one of Johto's mountains, but he did not think it was known for Electric-type Pokemon.

Like Trainer, like Pokemon. Chikorita swayed beside her in time with the song, keeping the beat. Did they really have to break into an awkward song just because they were happy?

Ethan encouraged his own Pokemon, but he could never throw himself into it quite like this girl. He certainly could not talk to himself and work up such excitement all on his own.

Still, her behavior told him plenty about her personality. She was warm, lively, and brimming with energy. And after he listened a little longer, he had to admit the song was unexpectedly catchy.

When she finished, the girl turned around. Only then did she notice Ethan.

"Oh! It's you!"

Her eyes brightened, and she hurried toward him.

"What a coincidence! We met again!" Slightly out of breath, she tipped her face up and gave Ethan a brilliant smile. "I'm sorry about what happened last time. I forgot to introduce myself. I'm Casey, and I just set out from New Bark Town as a new Trainer!"

Chapter 289: Casey's First Trainer Battle

Just as Ethan had thought, the girl was Casey. What surprised him was how little ground she had covered since they parted the previous afternoon.

It was probably her first time traveling alone. She must have wanted to catch every Pokemon she encountered and spent most of her time trying.

"I'm Ethan Vale, and I'm a Trainer too," he said. "This is Sabrina, my traveling companion."

Sabrina folded her arms and lifted her head a little haughtily. Her long, straight black hair stirred in the breeze, but she made no move to greet Casey.

"Ethan Vale?" Casey frowned. The name sounded familiar. Studying his face, she felt sure she had seen him somewhere before. Then she cried out, "I remember! You're the Indigo League champion! I watched a replay of your final match!"

Her voice was so loud that Ethan had to cover one ear.

"There's no need to be that surprised." He shook his head. "Every League crowns a champion each year. Add all the regions together, and there must be at least a dozen new champions annually. This year I happened to be one of them."

Inside, he was rather pleased with himself. Now that's how a champion stays humble. Even when someone recognizes you, you keep your modesty!

"You're nothing like the others!" Casey's eyes shone. "You won the whole tournament without losing a single match! My dad, my mom, my grandpa, my grandma, and our other relatives all watched you battle. They like you too!"

That reminded her of something. "This is such a great chance. Please battle me!"

"Battle you?" Ethan looked her over. She knew he had won a championship and still wanted to challenge him. Casey was so new to traveling that she had little sense of other Trainers' strength and no idea what it meant to be afraid.

Then again, if Ash encountered a champion he had seen on television while out in the wild, he would probably be unable to resist challenging them too.

But Casey had only just set out, and Ethan's Pokemon...

He considered his options. Charizard, Gengar, Gardevoir, and Gyarados would be far too much for her. Even against Jolteon, Crystal Onix, Scyther, or Venusaur, she had no real chance.

Looking into her hopeful eyes, he could not bring himself to refuse.

"All right. Let's have a battle."

"Yes!"

Delighted, Casey ran back to make plenty of room between them. She bowed politely. "This is my first battle against another Trainer. I hope you'll teach me a lot!"

"It's just a friendly match, not a tournament. You don't have to be so formal." Ethan smiled helplessly, still wondering which Pokemon would give her a reasonably even contest without leaving her discouraged.

At last, he took out Dratini's Poke Ball.

Casey spoke to the Chikorita by her feet. "Go, Chikorita! Our opponent is strong, so let's open with a fastball!"

Chikorita nodded and trotted into the middle of the clearing.

"Dratini, I'm counting on you!"

Ethan opened the Poke Ball. Blue light flashed, and a small, snake-like Pokemon appeared opposite Chikorita.

"Wow, a Dratini! I've never seen one before. It's so cute!"

Casey stared at it in disbelief. New Trainer or not, she knew how rare and precious a Dratini was. She had never imagined Ethan would have one. She pulled out her Pokedex and pointed it at Dratini.

"Beep! Dratini, the Dragon Pokemon. A Dragon type. This serpentine Pokemon is covered in pale blue scales. It has purple eyes, white fins on either side of its head, and a large, round white nose. Dratini usually live near swift-flowing water, such as waterfalls, and grow by repeatedly shedding their skin."

When Casey put her Pokedex away, Ethan smiled and explained the rules. "One Pokemon each. No special restrictions. The first Trainer to make the other Pokemon unable to battle wins."

"Got it!" Casey nodded eagerly.

Her innocent expression made Ethan sigh inwardly. She was almost certain to lose. Dragon-type attacks would do ordinary damage to a Grass type, while Chikorita's Grass-type attacks would deal only half damage to Dratini. The matchup was against her from the start.

Ordinarily, Ethan would not exploit a type advantage against a novice. But Dratini was the only relatively weak Pokemon on his team. Sending it out was the best option he had, and Casey did not yet have enough experience to recognize the disadvantage.

Sabrina stepped to the side to act as referee. Once both Pokemon were ready, she said flatly, "Begin."

Casey snapped into action, swinging her baseball bat. "Chikorita, use Razor Leaf!"

Even at half strength, Ethan had no intention of taking the attack head-on. "Dratini, fly up!"

Without any visible effort, Dratini rose into the air and avoided the sharp leaves. The pearl-like growth on its head let it fly as freely as a bird despite having no wings.

"Use Twister!"

Dratini opened its mouth. A great whirlwind sprang up in the clearing, growing larger as it swept toward Chikorita.

"Quick, dodge!" Casey shouted.

Chikorita was not especially fast, and the Twister moved too quickly. In a blink, the little Pokemon was caught inside. It spun around and around like a coin trapped in a washing machine. When the wind died, Chikorita fell hard to the ground with a thud, stars circling over its head.

Chapter 290: Arrival in Violet City

"Chikorita! Are you all right?"

Casey's face changed when she saw how hard Chikorita had been hit. She felt a pang of worry. They had traveled together for only two or three days, but they had already formed a close bond. They had battled wild Pokemon all along the way and won each time without much trouble. Chikorita had never taken a blow like this.

"Hang in there! Use Growl!"

Chikorita shook off its dizziness and let out a cute cry. Growl was a basic move many Pokemon could use. It did no damage, but it weakened the opponent's attacks, which could be useful in its own way.

The sound reached Dratini's ears. There was no way for it to shut out the cry completely. Dratini looked unchanged, but Ethan knew its Attack had fallen.

His expression did not shift. From what he had seen, Chikorita was still inexperienced and knew few moves. It kept returning to Tackle and Razor Leaf. Dratini was stronger and had a wider range of attacks.

"Lower Attack or not, we can keep going. Dratini, Dragon Rage!"

Energy gathered inside Dratini. A moment later, a shock wave burst from its mouth and struck Chikorita before it had a chance to dodge. The little Pokemon was thrown to the ground.

After taking two powerful hits in succession, Chikorita had no strength left to struggle. It was unable to battle.

It was Ethan's first battle against another Trainer with Dratini, but it had spent plenty of time at his side and had trained well. Both Pokemon were still at an early stage. As Ethan had expected, Dratini's superior strength had been enough to defeat Casey's Chikorita easily.

Sabrina glanced at the fallen Pokemon and announced, "Chikorita is unable to battle. Dratini wins."

"Chikorita!"

Casey ran forward and scooped it into her arms. Thankfully, it had only fainted and was not badly hurt. She let out a relieved breath, then looked up at Ethan.

"I lost..." Her voice was full of disappointment.

After watching Ethan compete in the Indigo League, she knew he had many formidable Pokemon. Sending out Dratini had already been a special consideration for her. She still could not win. The gap between them was enormous.

"You did well," Ethan told her. "You lost this time, but Chikorita has a lot of room to grow. Before long, I'm sure it can become a strong Pokemon that holds its own in battle."

Casey was naturally cheerful. His encouragement helped her recover quickly.

"You're right! Chikorita will get stronger!" She recalled it to its Poke Ball and looked enviously at Dratini. "Your Dratini is amazing, though. My Chikorita couldn't keep up at all!"

Ethan stepped over and stroked Dratini's forehead in reward. "Don't rush. Your journey has only just begun. I'm sure you'll catch plenty of rare partners yourself."

Casey hesitated. "Maybe. But I really like Pokemon with yellow markings, like Pikachu, Beedrill, and Electabuzz. I've always dreamed of making a whole team of them!"

Sabrina finally asked, "Why are you so set on Pokemon with yellow markings?"

Casey rubbed her nose and grinned proudly. "Because my whole family supports the Electabuzz baseball team! Electabuzz is yellow, so I want lots of Pokemon with yellow markings!"

That was her reason? Ethan looked at the yellow stripes covering Casey's clothes and shook his head. She was a truly devoted fan.

"Oh, right," Casey said, looking up at him again. "You came to Johto to challenge the Silver League, didn't you?"

"That's right. I need to collect eight badges before I can enter."

Her eyes brightened and she clenched her fist. "Then I'm going to enter the Silver Conference too!"

"You too? That's great." Ethan nodded. "You said you watched my matches, didn't you? Going from spectator to competitor is a fine change."

"Heh." Her eyes seemed to shine. "I'll start challenging Gyms, one at a time. By the time we meet in the tournament, I'll be much stronger than I am today. I'll surprise you!"

...

Violet City was a place with the charm of an older age.

Built on a hill at a bend in the river, it was a sizable city, yet forests covered much of it. Greenery was everywhere, giving the whole place a close connection to nature.

After parting from Casey, Ethan and Sabrina passed through the Mysterious Forest and Donphan Valley. They finally reached Violet City at noon the next day.

In the city, they occasionally glimpsed wild Pokemon among the dense trees. It was plainly another place where people and Pokemon lived together in harmony.
