Unfamiliar with the streets, Ethan opened the map on his phone again. Its description told him that Violet City had a Trainer School, where children learned about Pokemon in hopes of becoming excellent Trainers.

At the northern end of the city stood a great swaying tower, with many monks practicing inside. Sprout Tower was one of Violet City's best-known sights, and visitors invariably went to take a look.

The city's most famous destination, though, was Violet Gym. It was an official Pokemon League Gym specializing in Flying types. Its leader was Falkner, and Trainers who defeated him earned the Zephyr Badge.

Following the directions, Ethan and Sabrina crossed the city to the Gym. When Ethan reached the end of the street, a strange building came into view.

Violet Gym rose as a ten-story, pale purple tower. Windows covered its walls, which spiraled upward. Fitting for a Flying-type Gym, the battlefield appeared to be on the roof.

Chapter 291: Falkner

"Here we are. This is Violet Gym."

Ethan and Sabrina went inside, but when he told a staff member why they had come, he learned that Falkner was not there.

"I'm sorry," said a young man in a white martial arts uniform. "Gym Leader Falkner went out this morning. I don't know when he'll be back. If you'd like to challenge him today, please wait in the seating area."

Unfortunate timing. Still, there was nothing for Ethan and Sabrina to do but sit and wait.

Ethan settled into a soft sofa and looked around to pass the time. He saw several other young men dressed like the one who had greeted them.

Are they all apprentices here? It seemed strange to him. This was a Gym specializing in bird Pokemon. Did every one of these young men aspire to be a master of Flying types?

They could learn from Ash or Lance instead. Everyone knew those two were the true masters of Flying-type Pokemon, birds included!

He glanced at Sabrina and asked quietly, "Do you know Falkner?"

She shook her head. "Kanto and Johto may be governed by the same Pokemon League, but their Gym Leaders belong to different regions. I've never met him, and we've never had any contact."

"I see."

Ethan thought back over what he knew. Understanding an opponent was half the battle, but he knew little about Falkner. He had heard that Falkner was famous for looking cool and handsome, though his father Walker outshone him.

Walker was traveling at present, which was why he had handed the Gym Leader's position to his son. Many of the Pokemon Falkner used had come from his father as well. It was a straightforward case of a son taking over the family profession.

Then again, that was hardly unusual. In Kanto alone, Brock had followed his father, Sabrina had followed hers, and Misty's family had passed down their Gym too.

They waited a while longer. As the minutes slipped by, Ethan grew restless.

"We can't sit here all day. Should we find a place to stay, or go take a look at Sprout Tower?"

Sabrina gave a soft sound of agreement. They had reached the Gym entrance on their way out when they met a young man coming in.

He looked about Ethan's age, though a head shorter. His martial arts uniform resembled the staff members' but was more elaborate. Thick, medium-length hair fell over part of his face, yet his fine features were still visible.

That must be Falkner.

He did look cool, with a composed air about him. Handsome, though? Ethan gave a private scoff. No one had ever dared call himself handsome in front of Ethan Vale.

Before Ethan could speak, the young man said, "I'm Falkner. Are you here to challenge the Gym?"

So it was him.

"Yes," Ethan said. "I'm the challenger. The woman with me is here to accompany me."

Falkner looked Ethan over once more and nodded. "I understand. Follow me."

He walked past them toward the main hall. Ethan noticed a Hoothoot perched on one foot on Falkner's shoulder.

Hoothoot resembled an owl, only rounder, with tiny wings and a small beak. It was a Normal- and Flying-type Pokemon. Its red eyes could look downright frightening at night.

Ethan remembered that an organ inside Hoothoot could sense the Earth's rotation. It called at the same time every day, and its internal clock kept remarkably accurate time. People in the past had valued it as a divine messenger that announced the hours. In some countries, it was even regarded as a god of wisdom that understood everything in the world.

Judging by how comfortable Falkner and Hoothoot were together, Falkner liked to keep a Pokemon outside its Poke Ball, just as Ash did with Pikachu.

Hoothoot tilted its head when it noticed Ethan looking at it. It hooted, fluttered its wings, and greeted him with surprising awareness. Ethan smiled back.

Falkner led them to the elevator in the center of the hall. The three rode it to the top floor.

That was where the Gym's battlefield stood. Ethan discovered that it had no roof. It lay open to the sun, enclosed by stepped spectator seating on every side. In the middle was an ordinary regulation battle court.

Ethan did not like battling on rooftops, ship decks, or raised platforms. They always felt too thin beneath him. He would hesitate to use a terrain-breaking move like Dig for fear of damaging the structure. He preferred solid ground, where he could battle freely without that worry.

Falkner walked to one Trainer's position and spoke formally. "Let me introduce myself properly. I'm Falkner, the leader of Violet Gym, which specializes in Flying-type Pokemon. I welcome your challenge."

"I'm Ethan Vale."

Falkner showed no surprise. "I know. You're the Indigo League champion. I thought you'd come to challenge the Silver League as soon as that tournament ended. I didn't expect you to take this long."

He recognized me already. Ethan said, "I went to the Orange Islands along the way and stayed there for a while."

They did not know each other well, so Falkner asked no more. Once Ethan had taken the opposite Trainer's position, he explained the rules.

"Each of us may use three Pokemon. The first Trainer whose entire team is unable to battle loses."

It was the usual sort of Gym match: two-on-two or three-on-three, with little difference in the basic rules.

Just as Ethan got ready, the elevator opened again. More than a dozen young men stepped out. They stood silently behind Falkner to watch, while one of the older students took the referee's position at the center of the field.

Chapter 292: Raising the Difficulty

"Both sides, send out your Pokemon!" the referee called.

As Gym Leader, Falkner went first. His opening choice was the Hoothoot that had been standing on one foot on his shoulder.

"Hoothoot, I'll leave the first match to you!"

"Hoothoot!"

It flapped to the center of the field. Falkner's apprentices took seats in the stands and fixed their eyes on the court. Their serious expressions made the atmosphere tense.

Sabrina gave them a glance and walked alone to the seats on the other side. She sat down gracefully, entirely at ease.

Ethan studied Hoothoot and considered his own team. His fingers passed over the Poke Balls on his belt and stopped at Gardevoir's.

"Come out, Gardevoir!"

He threw the ball. Blue light flashed, and Gardevoir appeared before Hoothoot.

"Gardevoir," Falkner said, folding his arms as he looked it over. "It's from another region. We don't often get to see one here."

"That's how regional differences work," Ethan said with a shrug. "Kanto and Johto Pokemon are uncommon in other regions too."

Neither Pokemon had a clear type advantage over the other. Hoothoot was Normal- and Flying-type, but it could use several Psychic-type moves. Ethan had chosen Gardevoir with that in mind.

"Begin!"

The two Pokemon started battling. After only a few exchanges, Ethan's brow slowly furrowed.

Gardevoir was not doing badly. Quite the opposite: it was doing too well. It controlled the match from the start and gave Hoothoot no chance to fight back. Whether Hoothoot tried the Flying-type Peck or the Psychic-type Confusion, Hypnosis, or Reflect, Gardevoir calmly dealt with it and counterattacked.

Falkner had raised Hoothoot well, but it was still an unevolved Pokemon. It could not match the fully evolved Gardevoir.

That made Ethan realize the problem. Winning was far too easy.

After the Indigo League tournament and the Orange League, bringing his championship team against the ordinary Pokemon used in Gym challenges was almost unfair. Gym Leaders owned strong partners, but they usually sent out less powerful ones against new Trainers. Otherwise, few beginners would ever earn a badge.

Those matches might still challenge other Trainers. For Ethan, they offered little resistance. A one-sided victory taught him almost nothing and did not help his Pokemon grow.

He had two choices. He could challenge Gyms with his newer Pokemon, or find some way to make the battles harder.

Hoothoot finally fell. It had barely inflicted any real damage on Gardevoir. Falkner's apprentices stirred in their seats, murmuring to one another. They had seen Ethan compete before, but only now, watching their teacher face him, did they realize they had underestimated the champion.

Falkner shook his head. "Your Gardevoir is strong. The difference between them was plain. Hoothoot had little chance."

Ethan showed no pleasure in the win. He sighed. "That was my mistake. I wasted a chance to get stronger through a proper battle."

Falkner raised an eyebrow. "What do you mean?"

Ethan met his gaze. "I'd like a more demanding match. Falkner, would you use your strongest Pokemon?"

"So you think little of the team I chose?" Falkner gave a rueful laugh. "Are you sure? That goes against the League's purpose in setting Gym challenges."

Ethan did not change his mind. Falkner considered him and nodded.

"Fair enough. This is too easy for you. If you're asking for my best, then I'll show you what I can really do."

He recalled Hoothoot and put the three Poke Balls he had chosen back into his pouch. Then he took out two different balls. They looked ordinary, but Falkner's expression suggested they were special to him.

"To be honest, I want a real contest with you too," Falkner said. "I want to see what an Indigo League champion can do. Now I'll show you how powerful a bird Pokemon can be when it dances through the sky!"

He threw the next Poke Ball upward.

"Come out, Staraptor!"

In a bright flash of blue, a fierce-looking bird Pokemon appeared overhead.

Staraptor. Ethan looked up. It was a large, powerful bird, the final evolved form of Starly and Staravia. Its sharp eyes, claws, and beak gave it a formidable air. Its tail feathers spread like a fan, and the dramatic crest on its head made it look especially striking.

This was Ethan's first time seeing one in person. He raised his Pokedex.

"Beep. Staraptor, the Predator Pokemon. Normal- and Flying-type. It leaves its flock to live on its own and takes great care over the shape of its crest. Its strong muscles and wings let it fly while carrying small Pokemon. Fierce by nature, it will challenge opponents larger than itself and keep attacking even when badly hurt."

Ethan felt a very different presence from Staraptor than he had from Hoothoot. This might truly be one of Falkner's strongest partners.

He called Gardevoir back to rest and threw his second Poke Ball.

"Your turn, Jolteon!"

To Ethan, Falkner's switch felt like skipping the first playthrough and going straight into a harder second one. He had asked for the greater challenge, so he would not underestimate it. He needed to seize the initiative from the start.

Chapter 293: Taking On the Ace

Jolteon's Electric type gave it an advantage against Staraptor's Flying type. It could deal extra damage with its Electric attacks, while taking only half damage from Flying-type moves. On offense and defense alike, the matchup favored Jolteon.

Falkner understood that perfectly well, but he did not let it trouble him. Since becoming Gym Leader, he had met plenty of challengers who studied his team beforehand. Most brought Pokemon strong against Flying types, expecting an easy Zephyr Badge. Many had miscalculated and received a harsh lesson from his well-trained partners.

What irritated him most was the claim that a Flying-type Pokemon was bound to lose to an Electric type. Nothing in the Pokemon world was that certain.

"Staraptor, Wing Attack!"

At Falkner's command, Staraptor soared upward and circled several times. Then it spread its wings and dived at Jolteon, moving fast enough to make the air whistle.

Ethan did not panic at an attack that brought Staraptor straight to him. "Jolteon, Discharge!"

Brilliant electricity burst from Jolteon's body in every direction. Staraptor could be as fast as it liked; to reach Jolteon, it would have to pass through the current.

A moment later, Staraptor did exactly that. Golden light crackled over its body as the electricity struck. It endured the pain, kept its speed, and swept a wing across Jolteon like a blade.

Thud! The two Pokemon passed each other. Both had taken damage, but Staraptor was plainly worse off.

Trading blows seemed unfavorable for Falkner, yet he had few alternatives. His confidence in Flying Pokemon could not change the fact that their attacks did half damage to an Electric type. He needed to close the distance and find another way to win.

At least the first exchange had brought Staraptor close enough. "Staraptor, Close Combat!"

It drove both wings at Jolteon like a flurry of fists. Ethan meant to order another Electric attack, but Staraptor was already upon them. Jolteon had no time to gather its power.

He changed tactics. "Don't let it beat you up close! Jolteon, Double Kick!"

Jolteon reared and struck with its front paws in quick succession, meeting Staraptor head-on.

Bam! Bam!

Wings and paws collided. Both Pokemon stumbled back a step, each hurt by the exchange. Staraptor, still suffering from the electric shock, had a little less strength left. It faltered before quickly regaining its balance.

That instant was enough for Ethan. "Jolteon, Thunder Fang!"

Electricity filled Jolteon's open jaws. It lunged and clamped its teeth onto one of Staraptor's wings. Staraptor shrieked in pain.

"Shake it off!" Falkner shouted.

Staraptor thrashed its wing, but Jolteon held on. Falkner frowned. "Damn it! Use Endeavor!"

Endeavor was a Normal-type physical move. Jolteon had no resistance to it, and Ethan would not simply watch it take the hit. He whistled. Before Staraptor could strike, Jolteon released the wing and sprang backward.

Staraptor's other wing swept through empty air. It had freed itself, but Jolteon had a mouthful of scorched feathers.

Staraptor panted. Its strength was running out. When it tried to lift the bitten wing, deep tooth marks showed where Jolteon had gripped it. The pain drew a miserable cry from the bird.

Falkner could see that Staraptor had been driven to the brink. He had to take a final risk.

"Staraptor, Brave Bird!"

Brave Bird? Ethan frowned. Among Flying-type moves, its power was second only to Sky Attack and matched Dragon Ascent. For many Flying Pokemon, it was a signature weapon. Falkner must be ready to stake everything on this attack.

Ethan suspected Staraptor had the Reckless Ability, which would increase Brave Bird's power further. But the move also hurt its user. In Staraptor's condition, the recoil might make it collapse even if it struck Jolteon. Falkner seemed willing to trade one Pokemon for the other to win this round.

Fighting through its pain, Staraptor beat its wings and climbed high above the court. A few loose feathers drifted down in the sunlight, giving it a tragic, resolute air.

After circling again, it folded its wings and plunged toward Jolteon with deadly precision.

"Jolteon, dodge!"

Jolteon sensed the danger and sprinted to escape before Staraptor reached the ground. Brave Bird was too fast. In the blink of an eye, Staraptor slammed into it.

Boom!

Jolteon flew seven or eight meters before hitting the ground. Its type resistance had halved the damage. Though it was breathing hard, it managed to stand.

Staraptor was less fortunate. The recoil from that collision had knocked it unconscious.

Seeing Staraptor motionless, the referee reluctantly raised a hand. "Staraptor is unable to battle! Jolteon wins the second round!"

Falkner's apprentices sighed softly, their shoulders slumping. Even his ace had fallen. Sabrina alone leaned back in her seat and gave a gentle round of applause.

Falkner recalled Staraptor, then looked at Ethan with a sigh. "Too bad. It was so close. If I'd worn Jolteon down a little more earlier, we might have knocked each other out."

Chapter 294: Togetic

So close? Ethan shook his head at Falkner's remark and said nothing. There were no guarantees about what might have happened.

Staraptor's ability to fly had kept it beyond Jolteon's reach at times. Without that advantage, it might have lost even faster. Jolteon had plenty of Electric-type moves it had never used. Staraptor's Flying type had put it at a disadvantage, but flying had also spared it some of the punishment it might otherwise have taken.

Still, Staraptor had fought like a Gym Leader's strongest Pokemon. Ethan had to acknowledge its strength. Against one of Ethan's second group of partners, it could probably win four battles out of ten. Fortunately, Jolteon had come out ahead this time.

Falkner composed himself and picked up his last Poke Ball. Ethan watched it closely. What would come out? Unlike his easy match with Hoothoot, this demanding battle had made him eager for the next round.

The answer came quickly. Falkner threw the ball high. "Come out, Togetic!"

Blue light flashed, and a white Pokemon marked with red and blue triangles appeared above the battlefield.

Togetic? Misty had a Togepi, Ethan remembered, and Togetic was Togepi's evolved form. It had a longer neck and legs and a pair of wings on its back. Compared with Togepi, still wrapped in its eggshell, Togetic looked almost like a teenager beside a small child.

It was rare, and Togepi needed a close bond with its Trainer to evolve. Ethan had not expected Falkner to have one. Since he had never seen a Togetic in person, he took out his Pokedex and aimed it upward.

"Beep! Togetic, the Happiness Pokemon. A Fairy and Flying type. No wild Togetic has yet been recorded. It brings good luck. When it flies, it can float steadily through the air with scarcely a beat of its wings. It is said to travel the world searching for kindhearted people to share happiness with. When it finds a gentle person or Pokemon with a pure heart, it appears and scatters glittering feathers known as happiness powder, bringing them good fortune."

Once his surprise faded, Ethan thought about which Pokemon to use. Togetic had come out after Staraptor, so it was likely even stronger. This would not be easy.

Of his Pokemon, Charizard and Gengar seemed best placed to exploit its typing. Both were too powerful for the sort of contest he wanted, though. After considering it, Ethan chose Crystal Onix.

He had several reasons. Crystal Onix's Ground-type moves could not hit a Flying Pokemon, but its Rock-type moves would be twice as effective against Togetic, making up for that weakness. He also needed to train his less experienced partners. His strongest Pokemon were pulling farther ahead, and he did not want the gap between them and the rest of his team to grow too wide.

Jolteon had gained experience in its hard fight with Staraptor. Crystal Onix deserved the same chance.

Ethan recalled Jolteon and threw his third Poke Ball. "You're up, Crystal Onix!"

Blue light flashed. A huge Pokemon landed heavily on the court.

Rumble!

Its weight sent a faint tremor through the entire building. Fortunately, Violet Gym's sturdy, unusual design bore the impact easily.

"What kind of Onix is that?" one apprentice whispered.

"Is it a Shiny? But that's not the color of a Shiny Onix!"

The young men behind Falkner could no longer keep quiet. Even with all the Pokemon they had seen, Crystal Onix's beautiful form astonished them.

Falkner studied it for a long moment. "A remarkable Pokemon. It has power and beauty, like a finely carved work of art."

Ethan smiled. "It's a Crystal Onix. I came across it by chance. Without a habitat like the one where it grew up, I doubt there's another like it in the world."

"Crystal Onix. A fitting name."

After a brief moment of admiration, Falkner focused on the match again. The referee gave the signal. Having lost the first two rounds, Falkner attacked first once more.

"Togetic, use Yawn!"

Ethan narrowed his eyes. "Go underground!"

An odd light spread toward Crystal Onix. Just before it reached the great serpent, Onix bored a hole in the court and vanished beneath it with surprising speed.

In his battle against Drake, Ethan had been impressed by how quickly Drake's Onix could dig. He had studied its technique afterward, then spent time training his own Crystal Onix. It was not quite as fast as Drake's, but it had become much quicker than an ordinary Onix using Dig.

Ethan was still nervous about using the move here. They were battling on the top floor. If Crystal Onix dug too deep and broke through the floor, the damage would be disastrous. His Pokemon could fall out of bounds and be eliminated, and Ethan might have to pay for the repairs himself.

Falkner had prepared for battles on this roof, though. The floor was thick enough. Ethan's fear did not come true.

Yawn's light passed over the opening without affecting Crystal Onix. The sight reminded Falkner of a hawk hunting a snake. To catch this one, he would first have to lure it from its hole.

He had Togetic descend toward the ground. As he hoped, Crystal Onix sprang from its tunnel and drove the point of its head toward Togetic.

"Dodge!"

Togetic shifted to the side. The attack missed by a hair.

There! Falkner's eyes lit up. This was the opening he had waited for.

"Togetic, Magical Leaf!"

Chapter 295: Putting the Lesson to Work

Magical Leaf was a Grass-type move, but many Psychic- and Fairy-type Pokemon could learn it. Gardevoir, Cleffa, Flabebe, and Falkner's Togetic were among them.

Grass was strong against both Ground and Rock. If Magical Leaf hit Crystal Onix, it could do four times the usual damage. It was one of Togetic's most straightforward ways to hurt Ethan's Pokemon badly.

Leaves gathered beside Togetic and shot toward Crystal Onix.

Falkner had not counted on how quickly Onix could dig. It had only just landed, but before the leaves crossed half the distance, it bored another hole and vanished underground.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

The leaves skimmed past its tail and lodged in the court. Crystal Onix was nowhere to be seen.

"What?" Falkner's expression changed. "How can it dig that fast?"

Ethan smiled at his surprise. The difficulty Drake's Onix had once caused him was now someone else's problem. Falkner was its first victim. Ethan had put what he learned into practice.

Drake had forced Ethan to use water to flush his Onix out of its tunnel. There was no water source here. Ethan was curious to see how Falkner would handle the same predicament.

Togetic circled the court, looking for a chance to attack. Below it were only three holes and some freshly dug earth. Crystal Onix showed no sign of emerging.

Falkner frowned as he considered his options. Onix's digging speed was far beyond anything he had expected. He could not strike an opponent he could not see, and the feeling of having power he could not use was maddening.

Should he attack again? The result would probably be the same. He would have to lower Togetic farther.

He knew that doing so might leave it no time to avoid an attack from below. But if he wanted to draw Crystal Onix out, he had little choice.

"Togetic, descend a little more! Watch the ground beneath you every moment!"

Falkner had realized that whenever Togetic tried Yawn or Magical Leaf, moves that could disable or seriously injure Onix, it simply stayed underground. From there, Togetic could do nothing to it.

Ethan was the challenger and ought to be seeking a quick win. But with Falkner already down two rounds, he was the one feeling the greater pressure.

Togetic floated lower, a little over two meters above the court. The next instant, Crystal Onix burst from the ground, its gleaming head driving straight toward it.

Falkner's eyelid twitched. "Dodge, then counterattack!"

To a Pokemon Crystal Onix's size, two meters was hardly any height at all. Its head was upon Togetic almost immediately.

Togetic tried to slip aside as it had before, but flying lower had cut its reaction time. Onix struck before it could finish moving.

Bam!

Togetic stood less than a meter tall, smaller than Crystal Onix's head. The blow hit with the force of a truck and sent it crashing to the court like a deflated ball.

It rolled several times, picking up dirt all over its white body.

Ethan seized the opening. "Rock Tomb!"

Crystal Onix raised part of its body and opened its mouth. More than a dozen huge rocks formed in the air and dropped toward Togetic.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

Togetic scrambled up and dodged the first few falling stones by the narrowest margin. It was not especially quick, though. The others struck it, and more rocks came down until its body was buried. The barrage changed the shape of the battlefield.

When Rock Tomb ended, the court fell quiet. Falkner's apprentices leaned forward anxiously, craning their necks toward the pile of stones. They had never imagined seeing their teacher's Pokemon battered like this.

Crystal Onix towered over the court. The heap of boulders before it made its advantage plain.

Ethan knew one hit would not finish Togetic so easily. If it could, it would never have become one of Falkner's strongest partners.

The rocks began to shake. With a crash, a mysterious force sent them flying apart. Togetic reappeared amid the rubble, covered in injuries.

Falkner relaxed a little at the sight of it. "Quick, use Magical Leaf!"

Still trying to attack? Ethan would not give Togetic the chance. "Stone Edge!"

Sharp stones erupted from the ground toward Togetic. Magical Leaves formed overhead at the same time. The attacks collided and canceled each other, but the force of the explosion shattered the rocks into a rain of fragments.

In the confusion, Crystal Onix rushed in before Togetic could take off. Its tail coiled around the smaller Pokemon and held it tight.

Onix squeezed. A chilling creak rose from Togetic's body. It felt as though every bone might break.

"No!" Falkner's face changed. "Use Sweet Kiss!"

Togetic could barely breathe. Gathering its last strength, it opened its mouth to send out a Fairy Wind. Before it could release the move, its world tipped sideways. Ethan had seen what it was trying to do and signaled Crystal Onix to fling it away.

Togetic rolled across the court again, the move trapped in its throat.

"Finish it!" Ethan said. "Iron Tail!"

Before Togetic's despairing eyes, Crystal Onix's huge tail shone brightly. It rose high enough to block the sun, casting a broad shadow over Togetic's face, then came crashing down.

Chapter 296: EvolutionCrystal Steelix

"Togetic is unable to battle! Crystal Onix wins!"

Even after the referee announced the result, Falkner's apprentices could not quite believe what they had seen. They stared at the unconscious Togetic, struggling to accept its defeat.

This was no ordinary Violet Gym defender like Hoothoot or Pidgeotto. Togetic was one of Falkner's best Pokemon, a symbol of his strength and pride as a Trainer and Gym Leader. Yet it had fallen all the same.

Falkner recalled Togetic with a sigh and slowly approached Ethan. He looked up at Crystal Onix towering over him, then shook his head.

"There's nothing more to say. My two strongest Pokemon could hardly fight back against yours. You've earned your champion's title, and I accept this loss." He took a badge from his clothes and offered it to Ethan. "The Zephyr Badge is yours."

"Thank you."

Ethan examined the badge. Its simple shape resembled a pair of slightly distorted wings. The material looked and felt like metal, gleaming brightly in the sun. It was heavy in his hand, perhaps the heaviest badge he had collected so far.

He put it in the new badge case Nurse Joy had given him when he registered for the Silver League, then nodded to Falkner.

"I'm sorry your Pokemon were hurt. Before I go, may I ask you something?"

"Of course. There's no need to be so formal."

"Why do you want to become a master of bird Pokemon?"

Falkner considered the question instead of answering at once. He pointed toward the blue sky. Ethan looked up. A few white clouds moved slowly overhead, but he saw nothing else.

Falkner smiled. "Ever since I was young, I've longed for the open sky. Over time, I came to love the bird Pokemon that fly through it."

"I see."

Ethan said it aloud, though he had doubts. Had Falkner really chosen this path without his father's influence? Even his name seemed to speak of Walker's hopes for a Flying-type Trainer. Ethan kept that thought to himself.

"Now that I've accomplished what I came for, we'd better be going," he said.

"Take care." Falkner shook his hand. "I wish you more success in the Silver League."

Ethan and Sabrina left Violet Gym as evening approached. They went to the city's Pokemon Center rather than looking for a hotel.

After the night they had spent apart, Ethan wanted badly to fall asleep holding Sabrina again. But first he had something more important to do, and he needed a place that was spacious yet reasonably private. The Pokemon Center seemed a good choice.

They ate dinner there, then went to the courtyard behind the building. Crystal Onix had fought well in its recent battles, but Ethan felt it still lacked strength. It had traveled with him for a long time. Perhaps the moment had come for it to evolve.

Orange light from the setting sun covered Ethan and Sabrina. The other Trainers staying at the Center had returned to their rooms. With no one in sight, Ethan released Crystal Onix.

Rumble...

Its arrival made the ground tremble a little. Walls enclosed the courtyard on three sides, and thick trees helped screen it from view, so no one else seemed to notice.

"You did well today," Ethan told Onix. "But tougher battles are waiting for us. I think it's time for you to evolve."

Crystal Onix lowered its head and looked at him. It said nothing, but Ethan understood its gaze.

"You want to get stronger too?"

Once he saw it agreed, Ethan took the piece of Onix metal he had obtained in the Sevii Islands from his pack. He had carried it for a long time. There had been many opportunities to use it earlier, but other considerations had made him wait.

Now he and Crystal Onix had built trust and affection, and Onix had grown stronger. It was time to decide. Holding the metal in his hand, though, Ethan realized he did not know how to use it.

Crystal Onix solved the problem itself. It leaned forward and touched the metal lightly with the horn on its forehead.

At once, the Onix metal seemed to come alive. It spread swiftly over Crystal Onix's body, and brilliant white light wrapped around the huge Pokemon. Ethan's eyes stung, and he stepped back instinctively.

He had seen this light often enough to know what it meant. Crystal Onix was evolving.

When the glow faded, a fiercer Pokemon stood before him, larger, longer, and stronger than Onix had been.

Crystal Onix had finally become Crystal Steelix!

Like before, its body was made of crystal. Its head had grown much larger. The gentle, almost foolish look it once had was gone, replaced by a fierce one. Red eyes made it seem dangerous. Its horn had disappeared, but its tail had become sharply pointed, making it look deadlier still.

Delighted, Ethan walked around it several times. Then he pointed his Pokedex at the evolved Pokemon.

"Beep! Unknown Pokemon. The Trainer has registered a similar Pokemon. Beginning automatic identification... Identifying..."

After a pause, the Pokedex continued. "Identification complete. Beep! Crystal Steelix, the Iron Snake Pokemon. A Steel and Ground type. Steelix lives even deeper underground than Onix. Pressure and heat beneath the earth have tempered its body until it is harder than any metal. Steel particles coat it and reflect a glittering light. It sometimes digs toward the center of the planet. Its eyes can see even in the darkness deep underground."

The longer Ethan looked at its fearsome new shape, the more he liked it. He was about to put away the Pokedex when it spoke again.

"Beep! No corresponding entry in the database. Upload data?"

That would share Crystal Steelix's information on the network. Just as he had done when he first caught Crystal Onix, Ethan selected No without hesitation.

"Beep! Upload canceled."

Crystal Steelix gently twisted its body, getting used to its new form. Gengar emerged from Ethan's shadow and looked it over from head to tail.

"Hey, you look good! You've got a lot more presence than before."

Pleased by the praise, Steelix narrowed its eyes happily. It lowered its enormous head to Ethan's hand and rubbed against him affectionately.

Only up close did Ethan realize just how large its head had become. It was taller than he was, at least two meters across.

Its evolution had changed its Ground and Rock typing to Ground and Steel. Ethan knew fights against Fighting- and Fire-type Pokemon would now be harder for it. Even so, its increased strength had made his team far more powerful.

Chapter 297: Sprout Tower and Gary Again

After spending the night at the Pokemon Center, Ethan and Sabrina did not leave Violet City right away. They crossed a lake and visited Sprout Tower in the north of the city.

The tower stood within a temple on the outskirts. Away from the noise of the city center, with hills and water nearby and forest all around, it was well placed for quiet religious practice.

Admission was free, and the tower was famous, so many tourists came every day. It was only nine in the morning, though, and the monks had just finished their morning observances. Few visitors had arrived yet.

Ethan and Sabrina walked through the temple gate. Standing among the solemn buildings, Ethan almost felt a sense of meditative calm settle around him. He glanced at Sabrina and laughed at himself.

What calm? Go away. He and Sabrina were inseparable these days, and several younger girls liked him too. With all those ties pulling at him, he was hardly destined for a life of Buddhist detachment.

They walked farther inside. The temple's layout was simpler than he expected. The monks lived in buildings around the edge, and a few were sweeping the courtyard. In the center, a three-story wooden tower rose from a stone platform.

That must be Sprout Tower.

Seeing it in person was a little disappointing. When Ethan had first heard the name, he had imagined six or seven stories. Instead, it looked much like an ordinary wooden pagoda, with wooden walls and blue-gray tiles. It was neither shaped like a Bellsprout nor especially tall.

Gengar popped out of his shadow and looked around. It seemed to share his question.

"Why call it Sprout Tower? It doesn't look like a Bellsprout at all!"

Ethan consulted the travel guide he had picked up at the Pokemon Center. "It says there's a huge pillar inside that sways constantly to keep the tower balanced. Its movement resembles a swaying Bellsprout, so that's where the name comes from."

"A swaying pillar?" Gengar asked. "Wouldn't that make the building unstable? Why design it like that?"

"I'm not entirely sure. The guide doesn't explain it. But I've heard flexible structures like that resist earthquakes well. People used them in ancient buildings, and some modern buildings still use the principle. It looks unsteady, but structurally it works."

As they talked, Ethan and Sabrina climbed the steps and entered through the tower's open door.

Creak.

The wooden floor sounded beneath their feet. The first thing they saw inside was an enormous timber moving back and forth like a pendulum. Nearly two meters wide, it hung down through the upper floor and extended below the ground, swaying in a steady rhythm.

"It's... enormous!"

Curious, Gengar passed through the walls to inspect the floor above, then sank underground. When it returned, it was still muttering in amazement.

"I checked. Nothing is pushing it. It keeps swaying entirely because of the clever way it's built. That's incredible!"

"It is," Ethan agreed.

With Gengar still talking, Ethan and Sabrina took the stairs to the second and third floors. Ethan was disappointed again. Apart from the great timber, Sprout Tower looked like any other wooden tower. There was little else to see.

They had arrived with high expectations and found almost nothing to reward them. Still, it was a famous landmark. Ethan took several photographs as souvenirs.

They lingered until about eleven, when they tried the free vegetarian meal provided by the temple. Simple as it was, the food tasted excellent. It might have been the best part of their visit.

While eating, Ethan noticed that seemingly every monk practicing there had a Bellsprout. Perhaps they had chosen the Pokemon to match the temple's name and accompany them in their training.

When they had eaten their fill, Ethan and Sabrina put their hands together to thank the monks, then left the temple and went straight out of Violet City.

They still had several days before their appointment with Looker. Their next plan was to travel northeast through the vast, dense forest to Blackthorn City and challenge its Gym. Afterward, they would turn west to Mahogany Town to meet him.

They had walked along the broad road for half an hour and were nearing the forest when a red sports car appeared ahead.

It sped past Ethan, raising a trail of dust. Before the dust settled, the car reversed and came back. The scene was so familiar that Ethan instinctively looked inside.

There, in the passenger seat, sat an old acquaintance about Ash's age.

"Gary?"

Of course it was Gary Oak. As before, the car was full of young women in revealing outfits. Gary looked just as surprised to see Ethan.

"Ethan? I didn't expect to run into you here!"

"I'm surprised too." Ethan waved, then glanced at the car. "It has been a while, but you haven't changed. Still traveling by sports car?"

"Naturally." Gary folded his arms and lifted his chin. "It's the fastest way to travel. You walkers can't compare!"

Seeing Gary reminded Ethan of Ash. "If you're here, have you seen Ash?"

Gary snorted. "We parted at the laboratory yesterday. He set off in this direction too, but at his pace he's probably still trudging across the Indigo Plateau."

Only as far as the Indigo Plateau? That really was slow.

Ethan turned his attention back to Gary. As he had when they first met, he took a Poke Ball from his pouch and tossed it lightly in his hand.

"Since it's been so long, how about a battle right now?"

Gary shook his head. "Now? No. Our next big match has to wait until the Silver Conference. I won't lose to you again. This time you'll find out what defeat feels like!"

He gave Ethan no chance to reply. At his signal, the young woman driving pressed the accelerator, and the sports car sped away.

Vroom! Dust rose again.

Ethan watched the car shrink into the distance and smiled. Strong rivals were appearing one after another. With them and the opponents he had yet to meet, the Silver Conference promised to be lively indeed.

Chapter 298: Ice Path

Following the map, Ethan and Sabrina finally crossed the vast, dense forest after two days of travel.

They had camped outdoors, made their way through difficult terrain, and defeated plenty of wild Pokemon that had harassed them along the way. The journey had left them tired in body and mind. At least their destination was close now. Once they crossed a few more mountains, they would reach Blackthorn City.

Ethan could have ridden Charizard over the forest with ease, but he had chosen to walk.

To him, the pleasure of traveling lay in overcoming difficulties alongside his Pokemon, taking in the scenery, and strengthening their bond along the way.

If all he wanted was speed and convenience, how would his journey be any different from Gary's hurried travels?

After leaving the forest, the two continued east. They had not gone far when Ethan stopped again.

"What's wrong?" Sabrina asked softly.

Ethan tipped his chin forward. Sabrina followed his gaze and saw that the road split across the open ground ahead. One branch went around the mountains; the other led into them.

Ethan studied the map for a while. "Both roads take us to the same place, but they're very different. The first goes around the mountains. It's longer, but easier to walk. The second passes through a mountain cave called the Ice Path. It's covered in ice and snow and will be bitterly cold, but it'll save us a lot of time and take us straight to Blackthorn City."

Sabrina considered it, then pointed to the road on the right. "Let's take the shortcut. The sooner we reach Blackthorn City, the sooner I can have a hot shower."

Two days without a bath had been hard on her.

"We might not have to wait until Blackthorn for that." Ethan pointed to the Ice Path on the map. "It looks like there's a Pokemon Center inside. We could stay there tonight!"

Sabrina blinked. "There's a Pokemon Center inside a cave?"

"That's right. Apparently, enough people use the cave to reach Blackthorn that the League built a Center there for their safety, so travelers can stop and rest along the way."

With their route decided, they headed into the mountains.

At first, the slopes were still lush and green. As they drew closer to the Ice Path, though, the temperature dropped noticeably, and the trees and other plants gradually withered.

By the time Ethan stood at the cave entrance, a gust of cold air from within made him shiver. Unlike the surrounding mountainside, the entrance was already coated in solid ice.

"It's freezing. We'd better change before we catch a cold."

Sabrina agreed. They took their thick clothes out of their packs. By now, they were so comfortable with each other that neither thought much of changing in front of the other. They simply had their Pokemon stand guard nearby while they put on their winter clothes.

The extra layers warmed them at once. Inside the cave, however, the wind still cut through them. Ethan had to call out Charizard, whose ever-burning tail finally kept the cold at bay.

Although the cave's official name was the Ice Path, local people preferred to call it the Ice Cave.

Like the other frozen caverns Ethan had visited, it had smooth, glittering walls of ice all around. Ice-type Pokemon wandered there too, but at the sight of intruders they fled deeper into the cave.

Creak...

The ice beneath their feet made soft sounds as they stepped on it. Farther in, the ice grew thicker and thicker.

Seeing how carefully Sabrina was walking, Ethan hurried to take her hand. "Watch out. It's slippery here. Don't fall."

He also called out Gengar to help if anything went wrong. By flashlight and firelight, they pressed farther into the cave.

There was only one main passage. Small openings branched off to either side now and then, but they led into tunnels barely half a person's height, too small for a human to enter. Even on his first visit, Ethan had no reason to worry about getting lost. He could also see footprints left by earlier travelers.

Following those prints, they walked for more than two hours before they came beneath a vast dome of ice.

Sunlight filtered down through the dome overhead and reflected softly through the cavern, lighting the space. On the ice ahead stood a three-story building.

Even seeing it with her own eyes, Sabrina was astonished. "I can't believe there's really a Pokemon Center in a place like this!"

What surprised her most was the light shining from its windows. The Center was clearly still open.

"Come on. We'll stay here tonight."

Ethan recalled Charizard, took Sabrina's hand, and walked into the Pokemon Center. The automatic doors slid apart, and warm air washed over them. Ethan felt as though the blood that had frozen in his veins had finally begun to flow again.

"Phew! Hello!"

At the sound of their arrival, a Jynx wearing a nurse's uniform came through a side door.

Huh? They have Jynx helping here instead of Chansey? As far as Ethan knew, the Pokemon assisting at Johto's Pokemon Centers had always been Chansey. He had not expected to find a Jynx here. And he had to admit, the sight of one in a nurse's uniform was rather hard on the eyes.

"Lutu-dulu!"

Jynx said something to them. Unable to understand Pokemon speech, Ethan turned to his Gengar.

"What did it say?"

Gengar, serving as translator, shrugged. "Nothing much. It's just saying hello."

"I see..." Ethan looked around but saw no sign of Nurse Joy. "Hello, Jynx. Is Nurse Joy here?"

No sooner had he asked than Nurse Joy herself came out from behind the door.

"Good afternoon, you two."

Ethan nodded to her. "Hello, Nurse Joy. We'd like to stay the night."

"Of course. We have plenty of rooms." Joy smiled and gestured them inside. "You must have had a tiring journey. Come have something to drink and warm yourselves up."

They followed Nurse Joy into the dining room and found that they were not the only guests. A man and a woman were sitting at a table eating dinner. Hearing Ethan and Sabrina's footsteps, both turned their heads.

Chapter 299: The Week Siblings

Nurse Joy led Ethan and Sabrina to an empty table, but before leaving, she asked, "Are either of you feeling unwell?"

Ethan took his eyes off the other two guests and gave her a puzzled look. "Why would you ask?"

"The temperature changes so sharply between the cave and the outside that people who enter the Ice Path without preparing can easily catch a cold or come down with a fever," Joy explained with a smile. "That's why I ask. If either of you has caught a chill, I have a special ginger soup for you."

Ethan and Sabrina exchanged a glance. They had prepared before entering the cave and neither felt anything wrong.

"We're fine. Two coffees will do, with extra sugar, please."

"Of course. Just a moment!"

As Joy walked away, Ethan's gaze lingered briefly on her short skirt. Then he shook his head.

That's impressive...

Even in a place this cold, Nurse Joy wore the same standard uniform as always, with her arms and legs bare. Women really did seem remarkably resistant to the cold.

And the Pokemon Center was deep inside an icy cavern, where she rarely saw sunlight and had few people to talk to. Living here year after year would take more endurance than most people had.

Before long, Jynx brought their coffees. Ethan thanked it and took a sip while his drink was still hot. The warmth drove the last of the chill from his body.

Just then, the man and woman at the distant table came over.

Both looked about Ethan's age. The woman had long, curly brown hair, while the man's hair was parted to one side. Otherwise, they were so ordinary-looking that neither would have stood out in a crowd. Looking closer, Ethan noticed a resemblance around their eyes and guessed they were brother and sister.

"Hello," the woman said with a smile as she approached Ethan. "You look like Trainers too. Let me introduce us first. We're the Week Siblings. I'm Tuscany, and this is my younger brother, Wesley."

Tuscany? Wesley?

Were their names really that casual? Why not simply call them Tuesday and Wednesday and be done with the day-of-the-week joke?

But wait. The Week Siblings?

Now that he thought about it, the name sounded familiar. He seemed to remember that there were seven of them, each with a name tied to a day of the week.

Ethan gave them a friendly nod. "Is there something you wanted?"

Tuscany raised an eyebrow and came straight to the point. "How about a battle? We're traveling around and challenging the Trainers we meet. If you beat us, we'll give you a badge!"

"A badge?"

Seeing how little interest Ethan showed, Wesley spoke up. "You must not be from around here if you've never heard of us! I don't mean to brag, but the Week Siblings are fairly well known in Johto. We made these badges ourselves. They won't help you challenge the Silver League, but winning one from us proves your strength!"

"I see." Ethan nodded again. "I don't mind battling, but I haven't had dinner yet. It'll have to wait a little."

"That's fine." Tuscany waved a hand. "We have all evening. We can wait here."

Truthfully, Ethan would have liked a hot shower and a good night's sleep. He was tired. But someone had challenged him, and as a Pokemon Trainer, he was not going to back down.

"All right."

With his agreement, Tuscany and Wesley returned to their table.

After they finished their coffee, Nurse Joy assigned Ethan and Sabrina rooms. With so few guests and plenty of space, each of them effectively had a room to themselves. They left their luggage there, then went back to the dining room for dinner.

But as they ate, Ethan kept feeling two pairs of eyes glance his way. Knowing the siblings were waiting on him spoiled his appetite. After only a few hurried bites, he gave Sabrina a rueful smile to show he was done.

Sabrina ate at her usual unhurried pace. When she finished, Ethan saw her to the Pokemon Center's bathing area. Then he found Tuscany and followed the siblings outside to the battlefield behind the building.

Since the Center stood within the Ice Path, the battlefield had been made from the ice already there.

Night had fallen, and the sun no longer lit the dome overhead. With nothing else to do, Nurse Joy and Jynx switched on the floodlights to illuminate the field. They waved from a second-floor window.

Ethan faced Tuscany and Wesley from the opposite side of the field.

"The rules are simple," Tuscany said. "Ordinary one-on-one battles. Each of us sends out one Pokemon, and whoever defeats the opponent wins. If you beat both of us in a row, you'll receive two badges."

She took a Poke Ball from her waist pouch and waited for Ethan.

Unlike a Gym match, a casual battle like this called for both sides to send out their Pokemon at the same time. Neither Trainer would get to choose a counter after seeing the other's Pokemon, which made the contest fairer.

Ethan did not reach for a Poke Ball yet. "A friendly warning: send out your strongest Pokemon. I'd rather this not be too easy. I'm pretty strong, you know!"

Wesley snorted at his audacity. "Nobody has ever spoken to the Week Siblings like that! If you're asking for trouble, I won't hold back. Tuscany, let me go first. I'll teach this arrogant guy a lesson!"

He stepped past his older sister, reached deep into his waist pouch for a Poke Ball, and fixed Ethan with a hostile stare.

Ethan had angered them, but his confidence had not come from an inflated ego. Simple battles were no longer enough for him. He needed demanding opponents to improve, and he wanted as many of his battles as possible to be worth fighting. Asking for their strongest Pokemon made sense to him.

After a moment's thought, he sent out Venusaur.

A faint sneer appeared on Wesley's face when he saw it. Blue light flashed, and his own Pokemon appeared on the field. Ethan looked over: it was a Forretress.

Oh?

Bug was strong against Grass. This time, it seemed, Ethan was the one at a type disadvantage.

Chapter 300: Two Badges

Forretress, the evolved form of Pineco, looked as though it were wrapped in hard armor. It had no limbs. A narrow gap ran along the middle of its red shell, revealing its eyes, while four tube-like projections extended from its sides.

It floated in midair. With such an odd shape and no obvious features of a Bug-type Pokemon, it looked more like a mixture of rock and steel.

Wesley had the type advantage. Venusaur's Grass-type moves, in particular, would do very little to Forretress. That gave him the mistaken impression that victory was already his.

Once the battle began, though, he gradually realized something was wrong.

Under Ethan's direction, Venusaur opened with Tackle and followed it with Sleep Powder. The powder did not put Forretress to sleep, but it made the Pokemon falter and left an opening.

Venusaur seized the chance to use Leech Seed and Growth, strengthening itself while drawing energy from its opponent.

When Ethan saw the right moment, he ordered Double-Edge. Venusaur knocked Forretress down, bound it with Vine Whip, swung it through the air more than a dozen times, and slammed it into the ice. Leech Seed took effect again then, draining what little strength Forretress had left.

Boom!

Forretress punched a great hole in the ice and fell unconscious.

Wesley's eyes widened. "What?!"

To Ethan, it had been an imperfect win against a poor type matchup. The injuries covering Venusaur showed just how much damage Wesley's Forretress had dealt with its advantage. Still, even while at a disadvantage, Ethan had won.

He shrugged. "See? I told you to send out your strongest Pokemon."

It was an unkind thing to say after the fight, and Wesley did not answer. In fact, Forretress was his strongest Pokemon. He had chosen it to teach Ethan a lesson for his rude warning.

What made the loss especially embarrassing was that he had been beaten decisively despite such a favorable type matchup. Ethan was simply the better Trainer. Wesley had no reply.

Seeing that Wesley accepted his defeat, Ethan turned to Tuscany. The Week Siblings might have a big reputation, but perhaps their natural talent had its limits. They were capable Trainers, yet there was still a wide gap between them and a tournament champion like Ethan. Neither sibling seemed to recognize him, and he did not volunteer his identity.

"I'll take this one," Tuscany said.

She patted her brother's shoulder and motioned for him to step aside. Then she chose her own strongest Pokemon.

"Be careful, Tuscany," Wesley warned. "This guy isn't ordinary."

"I know."

Tuscany was more than a year older than Wesley, and stronger as well. Ethan recalled his injured Venusaur and took out a second Poke Ball.

"As you asked, this is my strongest Pokemon," Tuscany said. "I hope you're satisfied. Go, Lombre!"

They threw their Poke Balls together. Two flashes of blue light brought Lombre and Jolteon onto the icy field.

Lombre was a bipedal Water- and Grass-type Pokemon with a resemblance to a kappa. A broad lily pad covered its head. Its body was green, its fingers and toes were red, and it had short, rounded ears and a red mouth shaped like a beak.

This time, the situation was reversed: Ethan had the type advantage.

Jolteon nearly fried Lombre. One electric blast followed another until Lombre collapsed, smoke rising from its body. Even the icy field beneath it cracked apart under the shocks and melted into water from the heat.

Tuscany lost faster than her brother had.

"We lost," she admitted.

The siblings exchanged a look, then quietly came to Ethan. Each took a badge from their clothes and held it out to him.

"Here," Wesley muttered. "This is my Disappointment Badge."

"And this is my Heartbeat Badge," Tuscany said. "Take it, mysterious Trainer. They're proof that you beat us."

Heartbeat and Disappointment? Ethan accepted the two badges and inspected them. One was red and the other blue, but otherwise they were identical. They looked much like the ordinary badges he had seen before coming to this world.

Even so, they marked one more step in his growth. He put both away carefully and thanked the siblings. He had taken only a couple of steps toward the Pokemon Center when Tuscany called after him.

"Mysterious Trainer! Our other brothers and sisters are traveling around Johto. If you meet them, you can challenge them too. I promise you won't be disappointed. I'll tell them what happened tonight, and they'll avenge Wesley and me!"

The Indigo League champion yawned. Without turning around, he waved a hand at them. "Got it. I'll take them on anytime!"

...

After a night's rest in the Ice Path, Ethan and Sabrina set out again, refreshed and full of energy.

To Ethan, the battles with Tuscany and Wesley had been only a small episode in his life. He soon forgot about them.

The second half of the Ice Path was just as easy to follow. As the map had promised, when they emerged from the frozen cave and descended a mountainside, Blackthorn City was close at hand.

Blackthorn was a quiet city nestled among the mountains. Somewhere in those mountains, people said, lay a mysterious Dragon's Den inhabited by many Dragon-type Pokemon. Trainers who came from Blackthorn tended to specialize in Dragon types.

Blackthorn Gym stood in the north of the city. Its Gym Leader and keeper of the Rising Badge was Clair, a master of Dragon-type Pokemon. And speaking of Clair inevitably brought up her cousin, the famous Elite Four member Lance.

Both belonged to the local clan of Dragon Tamers. Clair's accomplishments were hardly insignificant beside Lance's, and the clan had held firm control of Blackthorn Gym since its founding.

Clair had trained in the Dragon's Den from childhood. Ever since becoming a Trainer, she had been supremely confident in her abilities. She even called herself the world's greatest Dragon-type master and dared to take on members of the Elite Four.

Her record gave that confidence some basis. In her years as Gym Leader, she and her powerful Pokemon had defeated many Trainers who came to challenge her. Even among Johto's Gym Leaders, she was one of the very strongest.

Chapter 301: Clair

It was only eleven in the morning. With plenty of time left in the day, Ethan and Sabrina went straight to Blackthorn Gym.

Perhaps because the same clan had governed it for generations, the Gym had an imposing, dignified air. Unlike other Gyms with unusual designs, it resembled a manor. Everywhere Ethan looked, he could sense the weight of its long history.

The courtyard held many statues of Dragon-type Pokemon and Trainers. Ethan did not know who the people were or what deeds had earned them a place there. He guessed they were past Gym Leaders from the Dragon Tamer clan, or powerful Trainers who had begun their journeys here. After their deaths or retirements, people had erected these statues so they could continue to stand watch over the Gym.

He and Sabrina crossed the courtyard and entered the building.

The broad, bright hall was silent. Fine murals and decorations lined it, and an expensive-looking carpet lay underfoot. Yet even after they had stood in the entrance hall for some time, no one came to greet them.

"Is anyone here?" Ethan called softly.

He waited, but still no one appeared. He was about to go farther inside to look for a staff member when a familiar man came down the marble staircase.

The man's spiky red hair looked like flame. He wore dark blue clothes beneath a black cape. Those distinctive features made him impossible to mistake: Lance, one of the Elite Four.

"Oh, Mr. Lance?"

"Ethan!" Lance looked surprised too. "What brings you here? Ah, what am I saying? There's only one reason you'd come. You must be here to challenge Blackthorn Gym, right?"

Ethan smiled and nodded. "That's right. I've come for the Rising Badge. Is the Gym Leader here?"

Lance was about to answer when a woman hurried down the stairs behind him.

She looked about twenty-seven or twenty-eight, with long, pale blue hair tied in a ponytail. Her unusual bangs struck Ethan as a little strange. His first impression was of a Gyarados in human form.

She wore a fitted blue bodysuit and tall boots and was fastening a cape behind her as she came down. Seeing Lance still at the foot of the stairs, she urged him on. "Lance, we don't have time to waste. We need to go!"

Only after passing him did she notice the unfamiliar man and woman talking with him in the entrance hall. She studied them in surprise.

"Who are these two? Friends of yours?"

"These two..." Lance stepped aside with a courtly gesture. "Let me introduce everyone. This handsome young man is Ethan Vale, the Trainer I told you about. Don't let his age fool you; he's very strong. The lady beside him is Sabrina, Gym Leader of Saffron Gym in Kanto. And beside me is Clair, the Leader of this Gym."

Sabrina gave a slight nod in greeting.

Ethan offered his hand. "Hello, Clair. I'm Ethan. I came to challenge Blackthorn Gym."

Clair looked him over carefully, then shook his hand. "So you're the one who won the Indigo League tournament. It's a pleasure to meet you. I'm sorry, though. A Gym battle isn't possible right now. Lance and I have a very difficult problem to handle, and we have to leave immediately."

"That's all right." Ethan waved it off. "I can wait here. Do you know when you'll be back?"

Clair was about to reply, but Lance spoke first, smiling. "Hard to say. Maybe a day or two. Maybe four or five."

"That long?" Ethan stared at him. "Then my Gym battle...?"

"It'll have to wait, of course." Lance paused, then added with a smile, "What we're dealing with is troublesome, and we can't just walk away from it. But if a powerful Indigo League champion helped us, we might get back much sooner."

Oh, so that's what you were leading up to!

The hint was obvious. Before Ethan could respond, Clair frowned at Lance.

"No. The Dragon Holy Land is forbidden ground for our Dragon Tamers. Outsiders aren't allowed in, and you know the rules!"

"It'll be fine." Lance gestured for her to calm down and explained patiently. "Ethan and I fought side by side against Team Rocket in Kanto. I trust both his ability and his character. Most of all, he has a Dragon-type Pokemon himself, so you could even call him a Dragon-type Trainer!"

It was the first time Clair had heard Lance praise someone so highly. She gave Ethan another, surprised look, thought for a moment, and said no more.

Ethan, however, had no wish to get involved in the Dragon Tamers' affairs. Family secrets might be at stake. If he accidentally heard or saw something he should not, he could invite unnecessary trouble.

Still, he said I'm a Dragon-type Trainer? Lance must have meant his Gyarados.

"If it's forbidden ground, I'd better stay out of it."

Before Ethan had finished speaking, Lance's strong hand landed on his shoulder.

"Come on, don't refuse." A smile appeared on Lance's normally composed face. "I've said all those good things about you, and now you're backing out? Besides, don't you want to see the Dragon Holy Land? All right, I'll tell you the truth. We're going there to stop someone. He has a kind of Dark Ball that can steal other people's Pokemon. He's very difficult to deal with, and that's why we need your help."

"A Dark Ball? It can steal someone else's Pokemon?"

For some reason, Ethan suddenly thought of Mewtwo.

"That's right. He's already stolen Pokemon from many innocent Trainers with those Dark Balls. He's a ruthless Pokemon hunter!"

"Aren't there any other Dragon Tamers who could help?"

Lance spread his hands helplessly. "Our clan believes in training a small number of exceptional people. Apart from us, the others are away on their journeys. Only the elderly and the children are here. We can't send them out to fight, can we? We'd be laughed at if word got around!"

"All right..." Ethan sighed and nodded. "Since you've put it that way, I'll help. So who is this person? What kind of opponent needs an Elite Four member and a Gym Leader to face him together?"

"That's the man I knew I could count on!" Lance clapped Ethan's shoulder again and broke into a grin. "We're short on time, though. I'll fill you in on the way!"

Chapter 302: Team Rocket's Top Executive

A chilly mountain wind whistled past.

North of Blackthorn City, three Charizard flew in a single-file line, speeding over the mountains. When the clouds above them parted, sunlight fell across the faces of their riders.

It was Ethan's party, on its way from Blackthorn Gym. Lance led on his Charizard, Clair followed him, and Ethan and Sabrina rode together on Ethan's at the rear.

As Pokemon raised by Dragon Tamers, Lance's and Clair's Charizard were exceptionally strong. All three looked fierce as they flew together, an imposing sight against the sky.

Their destination was the Dragon's Den in these mountains. Beyond the Den lay the Dragon Tamers' sacred ground, the Dragon Holy Land.

While they flew, Lance explained what had happened. The Pokemon hunter wielding the Dark Balls was Vicious, the Iron Masked Marauder, one of Team Rocket's highest-ranking executives.

No one knew why this notorious hunter had set his sights on the Pokemon of the Dragon Holy Land. As soon as Lance received word, he had abandoned his other work and rushed back to Blackthorn to face the dangerous intruder with Clair.

A top executive. Vicious. A Pokemon hunter...

Ethan searched his memory. He remembered the man from the fourth Pokemon movie. Vicious had been its main villain and had dragged Ash into an incident spanning forty years.

As Ethan recalled, Vicious held a high position in Team Rocket, but his ambitions were too great for him to remain under Giovanni. Their ideals differed, and Vicious had long wanted to replace him, take command of Team Rocket, and conquer the world.

He had nearly every trait one could expect in a villain: cruelty, deceit, and total self-interest. He hurt people and Pokemon alike and seized anything he wanted. There was nothing redeeming about him.

But Ethan remembered that Vicious was supposed to be hunting Celebi right now. Why had he come here instead?

Clair glanced back at Ethan. Seeing that his Charizard had kept pace all this time, she gave him an approving look. "Not bad. You can keep up with us. You've raised your Charizard well!"

"Training is only part of it," Ethan replied without a trace of modesty. "My Pokemon has plenty of natural talent too!"

From their brief acquaintance, Ethan had found Clair outwardly tough but softer underneath. She acted decisively and spoke with confidence. Despite her commanding manner, she was easy enough to get along with.

After another ten minutes or so of flight, they landed beside a lake.

The lake was not large, but its current was swift. Ethan traced the water toward its source and found a dark opening in the mountainside beside it. River water poured steadily out of the cave.

From a distance, the rock around the entrance looked like a gaping mouth. Two fang-shaped stones hung above it, with two more rising below. Whether nature or human hands had shaped them, they gave the water cave an eerie appearance.

Charizard folded its wings, and Ethan jumped down. Almost as soon as he landed, a short, silver-haired old man stepped out from behind a rock and hurried over.

"Miss Clair! Master Lance! You're finally here!"

Lance went straight to the point. "Kabu, what's the situation? Where is Vicious?"

The old man had clearly been waiting for some time. He wiped sweat from his forehead. "I've been following him. I saw him enter the Dragon's Den with my own eyes!"

"He's already inside?"

"Damn it!" Clair turned toward the water cave and punched the air. "How did he get here so quickly? And how did he know this was the entrance?"

She noticed Kabu studying Ethan and Sabrina and briefly introduced them.

"This is Kabu. He's served our Dragon Tamer clan as a butler for decades, and he referees battles at Blackthorn Gym. Kabu, these two came to help us. Ethan is the Indigo League tournament champion, and Sabrina is the Leader of Saffron Gym."

Kabu greeted them at once. To Ethan, he looked at least seventy. He wore a well-fitted suit, and though his hair was silver, his face had few wrinkles. He stood straight and alert, with bright eyes.

"So you're Ethan Vale!" Kabu said, delighted. "I've heard about you and what you've done. With your help, we'll have a much better chance of catching that Pokemon hunter!"

By then Lance had recalled his Charizard. He took out another Poke Ball, cutting the conversation short.

"There's no time to waste. Let's enter the Dragon's Den. We have to stop Vicious before he reaches the Holy Land and enslaves the Pokemon there!"

He turned to Ethan. "The next stretch is by water. The currents inside are complicated, so be careful."

"I will."

Lance and Clair released Gyarados at the same time and leaped onto their backs.

They certainly lived up to their reputations as Dragon Tamers. First both had ridden Charizard; now both had brought Gyarados. Their Pokemon choices overlapped a great deal.

Then again, if it was a matter of having the same Pokemon...

Under their gaze, Ethan opened a Poke Ball and released his own Gyarados.

Splash!

Its huge body plunged into the lake, throwing up a spray of water. Gyarados roared toward the sky, then fixed its fierce eyes on the other two. Even against members of its own species, it radiated a more threatening presence than either of theirs. Both backed away a little under its domineering stare.

The difference was plain to see.

Sensing something unusual about this Gyarados, Clair looked at Ethan with surprise. "When Lance said you had a Dragon-type Pokemon, I didn't think much of it. I never expected you to have both a Charizard and a Gyarados. Anyone who saw us together without knowing you would think you came from the Dragon Tamer clan too!"

"What can I say?" Ethan spread his hands and smiled. "Charizard and Gyarados are two of my favorite Pokemon. How could I pass up either of them?"

The answer clearly pleased Clair. She nodded, then noticed the scars covering Gyarados's body. Large and small, they ran across almost every part of it.

People said scars were marks of honor. Clair needed no explanation to see that this Gyarados had survived extraordinary battles. That was how it had become so fearsome.

Chapter 303: Dragon's Den

Lance rode ahead on his Gyarados, leading the way again. Ethan and Sabrina shared a ride as before, while Kabu joined Clair on hers.

The three Gyarados crossed the lake, steered between sharp rocks beneath its surface, and pushed through swirling currents before entering the mouth of the Dragon's Den.

To Ethan, the dark water cave looked less like a dragon's mouth than a snake's.

Kabu was quick to read his expression. Holding tightly to his Gyarados's scales, he offered an explanation. "The Dragon's Den is an intricate cave system formed long ago when a Dragon-type Pokemon ran wild. Among our clan, its formal name is the Dragon Cave. That's a mouthful, though, so over time everyone shortened it to Dragon's Den."

A Dragon-type Pokemon? Ethan could hardly imagine one reshaping the land and opening such a huge water cave unless it was a Legendary or Mythical Pokemon.

"What Pokemon was it? Latias or Latios? Or another Dragon type?"

"I don't know. It happened hundreds of years ago," Kabu said. "But according to legend, when the first head of our clan went to stop it, he obtained a Dragon Fang from the Pokemon. Since then, every three years we have washed the Dragon Fang in the lake's water and prayed for the health and prosperity of every Dragon-type Pokemon in Johto."

Kabu turned to Clair. "That reminds me, miss. Cleansing Day is a little over a month away. Now that you've inherited the Gym, you'll need to conduct the cleansing ceremony yourself."

Clair gave a quiet acknowledgment. "I know."

Kabu seemed fond of conversation, and Ethan was curious. They spoke for a while longer. Perhaps Kabu assumed Ethan was a friend of Lance and Clair, or perhaps he trusted him because he was a champion. Whatever the reason, he answered almost everything Ethan asked.

They talked at length about Blackthorn Gym. Only then did Ethan learn how vast these mountains were. Even the Ice Path they had crossed belonged to the same range as the Dragon's Den.

Water rushed and splashed against the Gyarados without troubling them. While they talked, the group passed through the long entrance and reached the Den's interior.

The farther they went into the water cave, the darker it became. Before long, they could barely see ahead and had to switch on their flashlights to find their way.

A draft swept through the tunnels. Ethan could not shake the feeling that something eerie was watching him from the dark, like a hostile spirit. More likely, wild Pokemon disturbed by the visitors had their eyes fixed on the group.

Side passages of every size branched away through the water cave, leading to unknown places. Kabu said that even after hundreds of years, the Dragon Tamer clan had not explored them all.

Several hundred meters farther on, the water grew colder and faster. At a fork, Lance patted his Gyarados on the neck and brought it to a stop.

There was no sign of which way Vicious had gone.

Lance looked back at Clair and Ethan, frowning. "This is the main fork. The two channels go to different places, and the waterways beyond are complicated. One wrong turn and we'll lose our way. We haven't seen any trace of Vicious, so I can't tell which route he took."

Clair studied the passages. "What do you want to do? Split up and search?"

Lance examined them for a moment, then turned to Ethan. "What do you think, Champion?"

Ethan rubbed his chin. "If Vicious is after the Dragon Holy Land, we should plan for the worst. We can't count on the maze to stop a notorious Pokemon hunter. He must have some way of finding the right route. I say we go straight to the Holy Land."

Clair nodded in agreement. "That's what I think too."

With everyone in agreement, Lance led them onward.

The three Gyarados wound left and right through the water cave for another half hour. At last they plunged over a vast underground waterfall into a cold pool and reached the far side of the cavern.

Ethan knew that if he had been traveling alone, he would never have dared take such a drop. The waterfall was surely the Dragon Holy Land's final barrier.

They left the water cave, and the view suddenly opened into light.

A huge valley spread before Ethan. Sunlight fell on green grass, flowering plants, and trees as far as he could see. In a prominent patch of flowers, two or three Dragonite lay basking in the sun. Several Dragonair and Dratini stayed close beside them.

"We're here." Lance looked at Ethan with a touch of pride. "This is a paradise for Dragon-type Pokemon and the secret sanctuary of our clan: the Dragon Holy Land!"

Ethan gazed around in surprise. "I thought the Holy Land was inside the cave. It's a valley? Why didn't we just fly here?"

"The route we took is the only way in," Clair explained. "A mysterious power protects this place. From above, you would see nothing but bare mountain peaks. You couldn't find the valley at all."

"I see."

Even so, Ethan looked around again. Groups of Butterfree fluttered overhead. A few Rattata chased one another across the grass, while Bellossom and Hoppip played farther away.

"There are plenty of Pokemon besides Dragon types here!"

"Of course," Lance said with a smile. "The mysterious power of the Dragon-type Pokemon keeps the flowers blooming through every season, and that draws many other Pokemon here. A healthy, complete ecosystem needs a variety of species."

Ethan nodded.

As for the Pokemon he thought of as dragons, he could see only Dragonite, Kingdra, Gyarados, and their earlier forms. There did not seem to be any especially rare Dragon-type Pokemon.

For a place with a name as grand as the Dragon Holy Land, it still has the limits of its region...

Chapter 304: The Dragon Elder

Standing in the Dragon Holy Land, Clair scanned the valley carefully. Everything looked as it should, and Vicious was nowhere in sight. She let out a breath of relief.

"Good. We made the right choice. That Pokemon hunter hasn't reached this place yet!"

Lance nodded. "We still can't let our guard down. First, let's go to the shrine and tell the Elder what's happened."

The Elder? Who's that?

Curious, Ethan followed Lance and Clair toward the center of the valley.

Pokemon watched them as they walked. Perhaps they were used to the Dragon Tamers, because unlike many wild Pokemon, they showed no suspicion or hostility toward humans. A few bold ones even greeted the group.

Deep in the valley, beside a pond and beneath a few solitary trees, Ethan saw a shrine.

Lance pointed. "Here we are. This is the Dragon Shrine."

It looked like a small temple covered in vines and flowers. On either side stood a stone pillar carved with inscriptions. Both pillars, like the shrine itself, bore the marks of years of wind and rain. Ethan could not guess how long they had stood there.

What caught his attention most was the flame burning fiercely in a stone basin inside. It had no visible fuel, yet it continued to burn without fading.

Noticing Ethan's gaze, Lance smiled. "After our clan fought that Dragon-type Pokemon, we built this shrine to pray for peace between people and Pokemon. We placed a sacred object here called the Flame of Prayer. Incredibly, it has burned for five hundred years and is still burning today."

A flame that's burned for five hundred years? That was extraordinary power. Ethan could imagine how formidable the Dragon-type Pokemon from the legend must have been.

Inside the shrine, an old man in armor and a helmet sat cross-legged on a cushion, dozing with a dragon-headed staff in his arms.

He was short, scarcely a meter and twenty centimeters tall by Ethan's estimate. Thick, long white eyebrows hid his eyes, and a white beard like a goat's covered his mouth. On his aged face, only his nose and deep wrinkles remained visible. He snored steadily and did not notice the group approaching.

He looked even older than Kabu, perhaps well into his nineties. Ethan took care not to startle him.

The others exchanged glances. Finally, Lance cleared his throat twice, stepped forward cautiously, and spoke in a low voice.

"Elder... Elder..."

He waited, but the old man did not respond. Lance sighed, unwilling to raise his voice. It was hard to imagine an Elite Four member looking so helpless, like a child caught doing something wrong.

Fortunately, footsteps sounded behind the shrine. A Dragonite came around the building.

At a glance, Ethan could tell it was stronger and sturdier than the other Dragonite he had seen.

Clair spoke softly. "It belonged to the Gym Leader from two generations ago. After its Trainer died, Dragonite carried on his wishes and stayed to protect this land. The Pokemon here love and respect it."

Dragonite clearly knew Lance, Clair, and Kabu well. It waved a greeting, then saw that they needed to speak with the Elder and gently shook him awake.

Yawning, the Elder slowly opened his eyes and finally noticed the people beside him.

"Ah, it's you..."

At the sound of his voice, Lance, Clair, and Kabu hurried to bow. Their respect for the Dragon Elder was unmistakable. Ethan followed their example. When he raised his head, he noticed an ornament on the Elder's helmet shaped like Rayquaza.

The likeness was remarkably lifelike. As far as Ethan knew, Rayquaza was a Legendary Pokemon that lived in the ozone layer and had never descended to the ground. Few ordinary people even knew it existed. Yet the Elder knew its appearance well enough to wear it as a totem. Ethan wondered whether the Dragon Tamer clan had once encountered Rayquaza.

Could Rayquaza have been the Pokemon that fought the clan's founder?

Lance respectfully explained the situation.

"A Pokemon hunter?" Despite his age, the Elder's thoughts were clear. He struck the ground firmly with his staff and spoke in a grave, weathered voice. "We cannot let him disturb the peace here. He must be stopped!"
