Then he turned to Ethan. His eyes were bright and wise, and his gaze seemed to look straight through Ethan to his soul.

"Young man, your destiny is intertwined with Dragon-type Pokemon. Now and in the future, you have a strong connection to the path of dragons. Would you like to join our Dragon Tamer clan and become a great master of Dragon-type Pokemon?"

Ethan was not especially moved, but the offer stunned Lance and the others. As far back as any of them remembered, Dragon Tamers had always been raised within the clan. They had never heard of someone being invited to join it. Ethan's acceptance would set a precedent.

"Me?" Ethan blinked, then shook his head. "I'm sorry. Becoming a Dragon Tamer sounds wonderful, but I love other Pokemon too, not only Dragon types."

The Elder did not seem surprised by his refusal. He sighed with a hint of regret and turned his attention to Sabrina.

"As for you, young lady, your destiny cannot be predicted. Psychic power is the source of your strength. It protects you, but it also binds you. It seems the path of dragons is not yours in this life."

The fact that he could discern Sabrina's psychic abilities told Ethan the Elder was no ordinary man. Then again, this world held more than extraordinary Pokemon. Plenty of its people were far from normal too!

Sabrina had no interest in becoming a Dragon Tamer or a master of Dragon-type Pokemon. She was about to say so when a tremendous blast sounded from a distant mountainside.

Everyone turned. Choking smoke billowed from the mountain where an explosion had torn through the rock. As the stone broke apart, a dark opening appeared.

Chapter 305: Splitting Up

To everyone's astonishment, a huge black mechanical spider crawled out of the new opening in the mountain. Four steel clawed arms extended from its body.

The group was too far away to make out the face of the person at its controls, but Ethan had little doubt who it was: Vicious.

So the danger they had come to stop had arrived after all. Unable to find the proper exit, Vicious had simply made a new one. Team Rocket's top executive certainly had his own way of doing things.

Worse, more than a dozen Team Rocket members in black uniforms jumped out of the hole behind the spider. Each carried a specialized gun.

The Elder frowned. "Go. You must protect the peace of the Dragon Holy Land! I'm too old to fight as I once did. I'm counting on you now."

"Don't worry. Leave this to us."

Lance gave the Dragon Elder a solemn nod, then turned to Ethan and Clair. As the oldest, most experienced, and most composed Trainer among them, he naturally took charge. He made his decision without hesitation.

"I didn't expect so many of them. We'll split up. I'll deal with Vicious. You two take care of the Team Rocket members first. We cannot let them reach the heart of the Holy Land."

Clair bristled at being assigned the grunts. "No. I want to fight that Pokemon hunter too!"

"Don't rush in." Lance held up a hand to calm her. "Vicious has Dark Balls that can steal other people's Pokemon. We can't all charge at him without a plan. I'll keep him occupied. Once you've cleared out the Team Rocket members, come join me."

He looked to Kabu. "Stay here with Dragonite and protect the Elder. If any of them slip through, don't let them get past you."

Lance's orders were clear and sensible, so no one objected further.

With no time to lose, Lance summoned his Dragonite, climbed onto its back, and flew straight toward the black spider. Clair released her Charizard and rushed toward the Team Rocket members.

They vanished into the distance in moments. Before following, Ethan turned to Sabrina.

"We should split up too."

"Oh?" She tilted her head. "You don't need my help?"

"They're ordinary grunts. Surely we don't need the Leader of Saffron Gym to deal with them!" Ethan joked. Then he looked in the direction Lance had gone, concern showing on his face. "Vicious is the real danger. If his Poke Balls can take someone else's Pokemon outright, even Lance may have trouble with him. I'd feel better if you went to support him."

"I understand."

Sabrina nodded. Psychic power flowed from her body, and without any visible movement, her feet lifted off the ground.

As she was about to leave, Ethan added, "Be careful. It would be best if you didn't use your Pokemon either. Rely on your psychic powers."

"Don't worry." She gave him a gentle smile, warmth in her eyes. "I know what to do."

Sabrina flew toward the mechanical spider. Ethan mounted his Charizard and headed for the Team Rocket members.

The valley was vast, but a flying Pokemon could cross it in minutes.

When Ethan arrived, Lance had already engaged the spider. Dragonite was attacking its legs, apparently trying to stop it from moving. Wary of Vicious's Dark Balls, Lance had not sent out any other Pokemon, and Dragonite kept a safe distance even while attacking from afar.

Clair, meanwhile, was battling the Team Rocket members. Alongside Charizard, she had released Dragonite, Salamence, and Altaria. The four Pokemon flew overhead, diving now and then to strike at the grunts.

Team Rocket had come prepared. Its members sent out great numbers of Golbat and Weezing, two Pokemon so common among them that they might as well have been their symbols.

In moments, dozens of Pokemon were chasing one another through the air. Moves flashed in every direction, so many that it was hard to follow them all. The wild Pokemon nearby had long since scattered, frightened by the explosion and the battle.

Clair was powerful, but there were too many opponents. Her Pokemon had to evade attacks from the Golbat and Weezing while the grunts' guns repeatedly launched capture nets at them. Dodging took so much effort that they could barely mount an effective offensive.

Ethan narrowed his eyes and jumped down from Charizard. With both hands, he released every Pokemon he had with him except the three Legendary birds, Venusaur, and Scyther.

"Go! Don't hold back. Take them down!"

Blue light flashed. Gyarados, Crystal Steelix, Jolteon, Dratini, and Gardevoir appeared one after another. With Charizard already out, Ethan had six Pokemon in the fight.

Gyarados roared toward the sky. Its deafening voice seemed to still the entire valley. Intimidated, every Pokemon paused for an instant. Before Gyarados could move, however, the impatient Jolteon had already charged into the battle.

Electric light burst again and again. Each hit left several Golbat tingling and numb, sending them tumbling helplessly toward the ground.

Charizard and Crystal Steelix joined the fray behind Jolteon. Even Dratini, relying on its agility, worked around the edge of the fight, finding isolated opponents and attacking them.

Gyarados came last, hauling its massive body over the ground despite how awkwardly it moved on land.

Ethan's fierce Pokemon tore into the enemy ranks. The balance shifted at once. With the pressure on Clair's team suddenly lifted, her Pokemon could finally show their strength.

Gardevoir, though, took a step back. It knew Gengar was protecting Ethan and that he was unlikely to come to harm. Still, as loyal as ever, it remained close beside him, guarding both Ethan and Clair.

Chapter 306: Vicious

Flames burned and the ground shook. A fire dragon wheeled through the air while a water dragon roared below.

The battlefield was chaos. After so many hard-fought battles, though, Ethan had grown. He could divide his attention, spot openings amid the confusion, and give useful orders to several Pokemon in turn.

"Gyarados, bite Golbat's wing! Don't let it get away!"

"Good work, Jolteon! Keep using Discharge and scorch them all!"

"Crystal Steelix, yes! Charge straight through and crush that Weezing!"

Under Ethan's direction, his Pokemon steadily cleared out Team Rocket's forces.

While Ethan and Clair fought the grunts, Lance kept Vicious occupied and drew him farther from the main battle. The two fights drifted apart. Vicious must have understood the tactic and the disadvantage it left his people in, but he did not seem to care. That was his nature: he thought only of himself. To him, the Team Rocket members were expendable, there to be used up.

The battle around Ethan raged on. First a few of Team Rocket's Pokemon lost consciousness and fell from the sky. Then a dozen, and finally dozens more.

Ordinary grunts and their Pokemon were no match for Ethan and Clair. Within minutes, their forces were nearly spent.

Seeing that victory was close, Clair relaxed enough to look properly at Ethan's Pokemon. Before meeting him, she had never watched one of his battles. She knew only that he was the latest Indigo League tournament champion, and she had not had a clear sense of his strength.

Now she could see that his Pokemon were formidable. Judging by their performance, they were even stronger than hers. Lance had good reason to value this champion.

Her gaze rested on Crystal Steelix for a few seconds. She was about to remark on its beauty and imposing size when she noticed a small, snakelike Pokemon weaving nimbly along the edge of the battlefield.

"Oh? You have a Dratini too?"

As Dragonite's first form, Dratini was known to be even rarer than Dragonite itself. People generally encountered it only where Dragonite lived.

"Yes," Ethan said with a nod. "I caught it on Dragonite Island."

"Dragonite Island..."

Clair had heard of the island but had never had the chance to find it. She had not expected Ethan to have been there. She studied him for a long moment.

"You managed to find Dragonite Island. Perhaps the Elder was right. You really do have a strong connection to the path of dragons."

Ethan smiled but said nothing.

By now the grunts had lost all their Pokemon and stood defenseless. They still tried to resist with their capture nets, but humans alone could do little against Ethan's and Clair's Pokemon. They were subdued in short order.

Only when every Team Rocket member had been dealt with did Ethan finally let himself relax. During the fight, he had worried that ordinary grunts might also carry Dark Balls and steal his Pokemon. Evidently, they did not.

It made sense. A Dark Ball could capture a Pokemon and even take one from another Trainer. In that respect, it surpassed a Master Ball. Something so valuable had to be scarce, even for Vicious. Why would he hand them out to his subordinates?

Ethan picked up the capture nets scattered on the ground. He and Clair used them to bind the grunts together, then recalled their defeated Pokemon into Poke Balls.

Some distance away, Vicious heard his followers' cries. He shot Lance a sideways glance and sneered. "This is pointless. Don't think beating a few lowly grunts means you can beat me! Before my Dark Balls, every Pokemon will submit. All your efforts are wasted!"

The mechanical spider swung its huge claws and forced Dragonite back. Then Vicious pulled a black Poke Ball from inside his coat and aimed it at Lance's Pokemon.

Lance's eyes widened. "Dragonite, dodge!"

Black light burst from the Dark Ball. Dragonite was far away and tried to evade it, but the light still spread over its body.

"Dragonite!"

Lance snatched Dragonite's Poke Ball from his waist pouch and tried to recall it. The red recall beam struck the black light and was canceled out. Dragonite struggled with all its strength, but before Lance's horrified eyes, the black light drew it into the Dark Ball within seconds.

Holding the ball, Vicious laughed wildly.

"Ha ha ha! You knew I was coming, so you must know who I am. Then you should have heard about this Dark Ball. It makes any Pokemon it captures evil and draws out its power to the utmost! No Pokemon can break free of it. Now obey me. Come out, evil Dragonite!"

Black light flashed again. Dragonite appeared before Lance, its eyes glowing with darkness.

The instant it emerged, it opened its mouth and fired Hyper Beam directly at him. The attack came without warning. Even Lance had no time to prepare.

Hyper Beam crossed the distance in an instant. Lance could already feel its terrifying heat. Just as it was about to strike him, it stopped in midair as though it had hit an invisible wall.

Boom!

The energy exploded. Thick smoke rolled outward and flames spread in every direction, but Lance was unharmed. He lowered the arm he had raised to shield his face. Hearing movement behind him, he turned and saw that Sabrina had arrived just in time.

She descended beside him without even glancing his way. Her hair moved in the air, and she kept her cold gaze fixed on Vicious.

She could not travel as quickly as a Pokemon, so she had arrived later than the others. That delay had put her in position to save Lance at the crucial moment.

"Thank you..." Lance said. Then he looked back at Dragonite.

Only seconds had passed, yet the Pokemon felt utterly unfamiliar to him, as if its entire nature had changed.

"Dragonite! Come to your senses!"

At the sound of his voice, Dragonite shuddered. For a moment, a trace of resistance appeared in its eyes. Then the darkness swallowed it again.

"Oh?" Vicious had noticed even that fleeting response. He grinned cruelly. "You and Dragonite must have a deep bond. But it only listens to me now!"

Lance refused to give up. "Dragonite! Don't let that dark power consume you! Wake up!"

"Hmph. How stubborn. How many times do I have to tell you?"

Impatience flashed in Vicious's eyes. He waved a hand. "Dragonite, what are you waiting for? Finish them!"

Chapter 307: The Unstoppable Black Light

The controlled Dragonite opened its mouth and attacked again. Sabrina stopped the blast just as she had the first.

Only then did Vicious take the woman in front of him seriously.

"Long black hair, that icy face... You're Sabrina, aren't you?" He looked her up and down and snorted. "I heard you and that Ethan Vale caused Team Rocket a great deal of trouble. You stopped Dragonite's Hyper Beam, so clearly you have some skill. But even with your unusual psychic power, how many times can you do it? You might stop the first attack and the second. Can you stop the tenth? Beside a Pokemon, a human is as fragile as a child!"

He was about to order another attack when footsteps sounded nearby. Ethan and Clair had arrived.

Before approaching, both had recalled their Pokemon to keep them away from the Dark Balls. Only Gardevoir remained visibly at Ethan's side, while Clair kept Charizard with her for protection.

Vicious glanced at them, crossed his arms, and regarded them coldly. He showed no sign of panic.

"Good. All the pests are here. Now I don't have to waste time hunting you down."

His voice was confident and cruel. He did not spare his defeated followers so much as a glance, as if their safety meant nothing to him.

Ethan walked unhurriedly to Sabrina's side and studied Vicious. Unlike ordinary Team Rocket members and executives, the man did not wear their usual uniform. His outfit was entirely his own.

A thick tuft of beard covered his chin, and his hair was tied in a short ponytail. A yellow mask hid most of his face, leaving only his nose and mouth visible. Ethan could not make out his features or judge his age, though his voice suggested a man in at least his thirties or forties.

He wore a black, sheer, stocking-like bodysuit, with a spiked black vest over it resembling something a biker gang member might wear. A red cape marked with an R hung behind him.

Altogether, it was a strange look, especially for a man of his age.

After taking it in, Ethan curled his lip in the sort of smug smile a domineering company president might wear.

What terrible taste. My transformation outfit and Gengar mask are leagues better. His disguise isn't even in the same class as mine!

After that brief moment of self-satisfaction, Ethan turned his mind to the fight.

Dragonite was plainly not itself. The Dark Ball had seized control of its mind, and it obeyed only Vicious. Lance could not bring himself to hurt his own Pokemon, and sending out another would only give Vicious another target to capture. He was caught between two bad choices.

There was one small piece of good news. Lance had trained Dragonite so well that its abilities were already near their peak. The Dark Ball had therefore added little to its strength, leaving its attacks within a range Sabrina could still withstand. The fact that she had blocked them proved as much.

Clair glared at Vicious. "What do you want in the Dragon Holy Land?"

"Isn't it obvious? The Pokemon here, of course!" Vicious pointed at Lance, Ethan, and Sabrina in turn. "Thanks to them and the International Police, most of Team Rocket's bases in Kanto have been destroyed. We've lost people and Pokemon, and we need fresh recruits."

Clair was furious. "So you came here? What a vile organization!"

"This is only one of our targets." Vicious took out several more Dark Balls and rolled them in his hand. "If you have any sense, stop wasting time and hand over your Pokemon."

"You can dream on!"

Vicious shrugged and gave an exaggerated sigh. "Then show me how you'll face the Pokemon that used to be yours. Come on. Struggle like insects for Lord Vicious to watch!"

It was an outrageous boast before an Elite Four member, a tournament champion, and two Gym Leaders. With the Dark Balls in his hands, though, he had reason to be confident.

At his gesture, two Dark Balls flew out together, aimed at Ethan's Gardevoir and Clair's Charizard. Black light surged from them toward both Pokemon.

Clair ordered Charizard to dodge. Ethan tried something different.

At his thought, Gardevoir raised both hands and pushed back with psychic power. A blue wave of energy met the black light.

It had no effect. The psychic force passed straight through the darkness. Neither disturbed the other in the slightest.

That's a problem... Ethan sighed. His idea had failed.

As the black light closed in, Gardevoir used Teleport and reappeared at a safe distance. Fortunately, the light did not track its target, and Gardevoir escaped.

The Dark Ball reminded Ethan of the strange black Poke Balls Mewtwo had used during their confrontation on New Island. The two kinds were similar in color and purpose, but their abilities differed.

Vicious's Dark Balls forcibly captured Pokemon and turned them evil. Mewtwo's black Poke Balls did not strengthen the Pokemon they caught, but they could track a target and even take a Pokemon directly from someone else's Poke Ball.

Each had an advantage. Mewtwo had escaped from Team Rocket, so Ethan suspected it had taken the technology behind its balls from them and modified it. If so, the two devices shared an origin. That would explain both their resemblance and how Vicious had come to possess Dark Balls.

Understanding that much did not give Ethan a way to stop Vicious. For now, he could guarantee only one thing: whatever happened, he would not bring out the three Legendary birds and turn this battle into a nightmare.

While Ethan and the others faced Vicious, Lance narrowed his eyes and thought rapidly. At last he addressed his companions.

"We can't keep dodging forever. Let's attack together and focus on the Dark Ball in his hand. If we break it, Dragonite should come back to its senses!"

Chapter 308: All or Nothing

At Lance's proposal, the others exchanged glances and nodded. They had no better plan left. They would have to put their hopes in breaking the Dark Ball.

Clair attacked first. "Charizard, aim at the Poke Ball in his hand! Flamethrower!"

Charizard had retreated to the edge of the battlefield to avoid the black light. At Clair's command, it turned back, fire gathering in its mouth as it dived toward Vicious.

At the same time, Lance released an Aerodactyl and directed it to circle around from the side.

"You go too," Ethan told Gardevoir with a nod. A moment later, it used Teleport to appear behind the mechanical spider.

The three Pokemon slipped around the controlled Dragonite and converged on Vicious.

"You think numbers will win this? How naive!"

Without hurrying, Vicious took three more Dark Balls from his clothing. He opened them, and three Pokemon wreathed in black light appeared before Ethan and the others.

"My evil Pokemon, destroy the enemies in front of you!"

Ethan watched Vicious closely as he brought out the balls. From a distance, he had not seen how the man handled them. Now he realized that the Dark Balls containing Pokemon had been fixed to Vicious's red vest.

Before being enlarged, they hung across its front like buttons, almost as if they were decoration. Whenever Vicious needed one, he plucked it free and expanded it in his hand. He had carried at least six filled Dark Balls in plain sight. After this release, only two of those button-like balls remained.

But Ethan glanced at the pouch on Vicious's waist. A man like him surely carried spare, empty Dark Balls beyond those six.

By then the black light had faded. The new Pokemon were a Houndoom, an Ursaring, and a Beedrill.

Ethan had seen Beedrill plenty of times, so he paid closer attention to the other two.

Houndoom resembled a lean black dog with two curved horns. It had sharp claws and fangs, bony ridges along its body, and a thin, long tail like those depicted on little devils and succubi. Ethan had heard that its designers drew on hellhounds and the black dogs of European legend.

Ursaring looked much like a large brown bear. Its defining feature was a yellow ring on its belly. Tufts of longer fur at its shoulders resembled armor, and its tail was somewhat larger than an ordinary bear's. Otherwise, it was not so different from the brown bears Ethan had seen back on Earth.

All three were ordinary species that could often be found in Johto's wilds. Now, though, black light shone in their eyes. The Dark Balls had clearly seized their minds and greatly increased their strength. The moment they appeared, they blocked the attacking Pokemon.

Seven Pokemon clashed, moves exploding in every direction. For a time, neither side could get the upper hand. Enhanced by the Dark Balls, Vicious's Pokemon were much harder to fight than their species suggested. With Dragonite among them, his four held off Ethan's, Clair's, and Lance's three before any could reach him.

As the fight continued, Ethan noticed something else. Perhaps Vicious relied too heavily on the Dark Balls and let his controlled Pokemon fight on instinct. He did not seem skilled at directing them. He acted more like their owner than their Trainer.

He did not switch their opponents to take advantage of type matchups or arrange any tactics. He merely let them attack at will, showing off their destructive power.

With the battle deadlocked, Clair looked at Lance. "What now? Do we press harder?"

Lance sighed and considered it briefly. Then his expression hardened. "At this point, we have to. Look at his clothes. He has very few Dark Balls left. He can't have many more tricks to play."

He threw two more Poke Balls to the ground, his gaze fixed on Vicious.

I'll defeat him. I'll take Dragonite back, and I'll protect the Dragon Holy Land myself!

Clair saw her cousin's resolve. Protecting the Holy Land and helping Lance recover Dragonite mattered equally to her. Without hesitation, she took out two Poke Balls of her own.

Blue light flashed, and four more Pokemon joined the fight. The balance began to turn in their favor.

Vicious's smile faded. For once, he grew serious. Lance was right: he had few Dark Balls left. Along with the six filled ones on his vest, he had only two or three empty spares in his pouch.

But if he could turn a few more of his opponents' best Pokemon against them, victory would be easy. Their trained Pokemon would be much stronger prizes than any wild Pokemon he could catch and control.

Fools. I'll beat you, and your Pokemon will help me realize my dream!

Watching both sides commit everything, Ethan knew neither would back down now. Whatever had brought them to this point, they were all betting on the outcome of this fight.

He signaled Sabrina to wait, then took out two Poke Balls. "Venusaur, Scyther, go help them!"

Blue light flashed as the two Pokemon joined the battle. Ethan's group surged forward and surrounded Vicious's controlled Pokemon. No one held back. The fighting shook the entire valley.

Vicious removed the last two balls from his vest and released the rest of his Pokemon. Their arrival helped him recover some of the ground he had lost.

Then attacks collided in a violent explosion, wrapping Ethan and the others in thick smoke. Vicious's eyes sharpened. He pointed at Clair.

"Dragonite, attack that woman!"

The controlled Dragonite abandoned its opponent, beat its wings, and sped straight toward her.

"Look out!"

It all happened so quickly that Dragonite reached Clair in an instant. Ethan was about to send Gengar out of his shadow to protect her, but Clair kept her composure. She slipped nimbly past Dragonite's attack, leaped into a tree, and sprang between branches like a ninja, avoiding each strike.

Ethan stared in amazement. Apart from Sabrina, he had never seen an ordinary human evade a Pokemon's attacks so calmly.

Chapter 309: A Decisive Strike

Crash. Crack...

One tree after another fell around Clair. Dragonite's relentless attacks were draining her strength, and once it destroyed the trees she had been using for cover, she could no longer evade it so easily. Several times, she escaped only by a narrow margin.

Fortunately, her Charizard noticed her danger and returned in time. It struck the controlled Dragonite head-on and sent it flying, giving Clair room to breathe.

On the mechanical spider, Vicious took another empty Dark Ball from his waist pouch. He watched the battlefield closely, ready to capture a new Pokemon.

The scene was too chaotic for a careless throw. Pokemon from both sides darted around and attacked without any predictable pattern. Unless he found the right opening, his target would dodge and he would waste one of his precious Dark Balls.

Ethan saw all of this. It was time for him to act.

The Dark Balls were the one thing making Vicious so dangerous. While he held one ready, everyone had to be wary. Take those balls away, though, and Vicious would be a tiger with neither teeth nor claws. The mechanical spider beneath him would mean little against so many Pokemon. Once Dragonite was free, it could tear the machine apart by itself.

Without drawing attention to himself, Ethan spoke softly. "Gengar, it's your turn."

Gengar lifted only half its head from Ethan's shadow. Apart from Sabrina, no one nearby noticed.

"Hey, I've been waiting forever. What do you need me to do?"

It sounded eager. Ethan narrowed his eyes at Vicious.

"The Dark Balls decide this fight. Any Pokemon that approaches him openly gets stopped by his controlled Pokemon or caught by a Dark Ball. You have to get close without a sound and strike from the shadows. Break every Dark Ball before he can react."

"Heh heh heh!" Gengar gave a wicked grin. "That's what I do best. Leave it to me!"

As it prepared to go, Ethan added a warning. "Be careful. Don't let a Dark Ball catch you. If it does, none of us will be able to beat you. And remember, smash the balls so the Pokemon under their control are freed."

"Don't worry." Gengar thumped its chest. "No one can catch me unless I let them!"

It vanished into the shadow and sped beneath the ground toward the spider.

On the open battlefield, Lance was still trying to break through Vicious's defenses. He pointed to a gap.

"Aerodactyl, go through there!"

Aerodactyl was a fierce Pokemon from ancient times. For years, people had accepted the theory that a great meteor impact had driven its kind to extinction. More recently, evidence had suggested some survived. They were occasionally found deep underground or in remote canyons.

Its gray body looked like a pterosaur from a science-fiction film, and it had a savage temperament. Ethan thought its Mega-Evolved form looked even more striking. He had never managed to encounter one himself, and he had no idea where Lance had caught his.

At Lance's command, Aerodactyl dived past Beedrill and Ursaring and came at Vicious from the left.

Vicious showed no fear as it closed in. This was exactly the chance he had been waiting for.

You think you found an opening? I left it there for you. Come closer, Aerodactyl. You're about to be mine.

Aerodactyl reached him in moments. Before it could attack, Vicious raised his arm to throw the Dark Ball.

But his plan did not survive contact with the shadow that flashed before his eyes. It came so close and so silently that the shock nearly stopped his heart.

Whoosh!

A breeze brushed past him. Before he understood what had happened, his hand was empty. He looked down. The Dark Ball had vanished.

"What?!"

He looked up and saw Gengar floating above him with a grin. Several Dark Balls were clutched in its hands.

"My Dark Balls!"

Vicious was horrified. Whose Pokemon is that? When did it appear? How did it get so close?

He had no time to think. He shouted at his controlled Pokemon, "Quick! Get those Dark Balls back!"

They never had a chance to move. Gengar laughed in Vicious's face, revealing four gleaming white fangs. Black light gathered in its left hand and formed a Shadow Ball. It slammed the attack into the Dark Balls held in its right.

Bang!

The Shadow Ball's energy shattered them at once.

"No!"

Vicious stared in fury. Ever since Mewtwo had awakened and destroyed the laboratory where it was made, these had been among Team Rocket's last Dark Balls. They were the basis of his power.

He could recruit more grunts if his followers died. He could capture more Pokemon if they escaped. Broken Dark Balls, however, could not be replaced.

With the balls destroyed, the black light vanished from the eyes of the controlled Pokemon, Dragonite among them. They paused, then regained their own minds.

Only now did Lance and Clair understand what had happened. They looked from Gengar in the air to Ethan, standing calmly below, and both breathed a sigh of relief.

"Beautifully done!"

Vicious saw that his Pokemon had come to their senses. Knowing he was in trouble, he struck a button on the spider's control panel. Thick smoke poured from the machine and swallowed everything around it.

Two thrusters extended from underneath. Fire burst from them, and the mechanical spider carried Vicious up into the sky.

"He's escaping!" Lance frowned. "Stay here. I'll go after him. We can't let him leave!"

He called Dragonite over and was about to climb onto its back when Ethan stopped him.

"Don't chase him. There's a reason people say not to pursue a desperate enemy. I suspect he still has a Dark Ball. If you go after him alone, you'll walk straight into a trap."

Chapter 310: Charizard's Mega Stone

Vicious had escaped swiftly and without hesitation. He had not even left them a parting threat, much less stopped for the Team Rocket members he had abandoned.

The battle had been short but fierce. Trees had fallen, the ground had collapsed, river water had been cut off, and bushes still burned. Vicious's controlled Pokemon had shown no restraint. Almost half the valley now lay scarred.

Clair looked at the destruction of a place she knew so well and let out a long sigh.

Lance patted her shoulder. "Don't dwell on it. Nature is powerful. It won't take long for this place to recover."

"Lance is right," Ethan said. "And with so many Grass-type Pokemon living here to care for it, I'm sure the Dragon Holy Land will be even more beautiful than before."

Hearing Ethan's formal way of addressing him, Lance grew serious. "After everything we've been through together, do you really need to keep calling me 'Mr. Lance'? Just call me Lance from now on."

Ethan had no reason to refuse the Elite Four member's friendly offer. Counting Lorelei, Lance was the second member of the Elite Four he had come to know well. Even the shrewd Agatha thought highly of him. Little by little, Ethan was building relationships that would be valuable in years to come.

They called their Pokemon back to their sides and looked toward the bound Team Rocket members nearby.

The grunts had begun to wake, but their hands and feet were tied, leaving them no way to escape. They had seen their top executive desert them, and their Pokemon had been defeated. With their resolve gone, they sat dejectedly and waited to learn what would happen to them.

"What do we do with them?" Ethan asked.

Lance glanced their way and snorted. "Take them out of here, then contact Officer Jenny and have them sent to prison. But first, we need to report to the Elder."

They left Pokemon to guard the prisoners and walked back toward the Dragon Shrine.

Along the way, Lance praised Gengar's timely intervention again and again. Ethan, meanwhile, could not contain his curiosity about Clair.

"How did you do that back there?"

"Do what?" Clair asked, unsure what he meant.

Ethan waved his hands and made a few swishing sounds. "Jumping from branch to branch like a ninja. How did you get so quick and light on your feet?"

"Oh, that." Clair smiled. "I've trained alongside Pokemon since I was little. Over time, my own body grew stronger too. And I know Dragonite's attack rhythm, so I could avoid it more easily."

"Is it really that simple?" Ethan was doubtful. "Those didn't look like movements an ordinary person could make."

Lance, walking ahead of them, laughed. "Clair really is an ordinary person. If you ever meet Bruno, you'll find out what a truly powerful human looks like. He can wrestle with Pokemon. His strength will astonish you!"

Bruno... right. Ethan rubbed his chin. As he remembered it, Bruno's strength was extraordinary. He had even beaten an Onix with his bare hands. The man was practically a human Pokemon. Ethan ought to meet him if he got the chance. Bruno was the only member of the Elite Four he had not dealt with yet.

A few minutes later, they reached the Dragon Shrine.

The Elder and Kabu had watched the battle from a distance. Seeing the group return unharmed, they came out to meet them. Lance explained what had happened. The Elder praised him and Clair for their efforts, then turned to Ethan.

"Young man, you've helped us greatly. Our Dragon Tamer clan always repays its debts. Given your bond with the path of dragons, I would like to give you one of these as your reward."

He drew two stones from his clothes.

One was yellow and the other blue. Both were round and translucent, polished until perfectly smooth. A pattern like a genetic lock lay inside each, and faint light glimmered in them under the sun.

Ethan recognized them at once. They were both Mega Stones for Charizard. The yellow Charizardite Y would let Charizard take its Y form, while the blue Charizardite X would produce its X form.

Seeing his astonishment, the Dragon Elder seemed to think he did not understand what they did. "A Dragon Tamer brought these remarkable stones back from a journey through Kalos. They hold a great power that lets Charizard evolve further for a short time. To use one, though, you'll have to travel to Kalos yourself and learn how. Their powers conflict, so the two cannot be used together. You may choose only one as your reward."

"This is... rather valuable, isn't it?"

"Don't hesitate. You've earned it."

Ethan still wavered. He looked up and saw Lance and Clair smiling encouragement at him. After a moment, he reached out, but his hand hovered between the stones.

One of two... It was a difficult choice.

Mega Charizard X and Mega Charizard Y each had their own strengths, and both looked magnificent. Ethan loved them both. But the Elder had offered him one choice, not two, no matter how much he wanted each of them.

He thought for a long time, then made up his mind. He gritted his teeth and took the yellow Mega Stone from the Elder's hand.

Mega Charizard Y. That's the one!

Mega Charizard X changed its body's color and its typing from Fire/Flying to Fire/Dragon. Mega Charizard Y kept its Fire/Flying typing, but greatly increased the power of its moves. That suited the balance of Ethan's team better. Neither form was simply stronger than the other; the decision came down to a Trainer's preference.

Once he had chosen, Ethan could finally stop agonizing over it. Together with the Gyaradosite he had obtained earlier, the Charizardite Y was his second Mega Stone. Even before beginning his journey through Kalos, he had gained another head start over other Trainers.

Chapter 311: A Clever Deception

By the time they left the Dragon Holy Land, dusk had fallen.

After the battle, Ethan felt relieved. As they came out of the mountains, the group looked up. Beneath orange-red clouds, a dozen or so Pidgey flew leisurely across the peaks in a V formation. Everything looked peaceful.

Blackthorn City lay not far away. Lights had come on in its buildings, cars, and streets, while neon signs added splashes of color. The city seemed newly alive against the silent forest around it. Ringed by mountains, it offered a striking view at this hour every day.

No one looking at that scene would have guessed that a fierce battle had just torn through a valley nearby.

They marched the Team Rocket prisoners onward for some time before meeting Officer Jenny and the police officers she had brought to receive them outside the city.

Jenny praised the group for capturing so many Rocket members at once. Like them, she regretted Vicious's escape.

"You nearly caught him. It's a shame Team Rocket's top executive got away. Still, arresting this many grunts deals the organization another serious blow. You've done us a great favor."

"Vicious won't remain free for long," Lance said gravely. "He carries those dangerous Poke Balls, and he attacked the Dragon Holy Land. The Elder has ordered the rest of our clan to pursue him as soon as they receive word. We won't stop until he's caught."

Lance spoke freely to Jenny about the Holy Land and the Elder. The Dragon Tamers and Blackthorn's police must have had a close relationship. After centuries of influence here, Ethan thought, perhaps the clan effectively controlled the whole city.

Lance and Jenny spoke a little longer. Then Lance left Kabu to complete the handover while he and Clair led Ethan and Sabrina toward Blackthorn.

Before leaving, Ethan looked back at the Rocket members. Jenny's officers were putting them in handcuffs. They would likely spend years, perhaps decades, in prison. Jenny was right: their arrests had weakened Team Rocket again.

Many of the grunts looked about Ethan's age. They had gone astray and sacrificed the best years of their lives for one man's absurd ambitions. It hardly seemed worth it. Ethan hoped they would come to understand that while they served their sentences.

They reached Blackthorn Gym after sunset. The day's battle and the journey back and forth had exhausted everyone, so Clair postponed Ethan's Gym match until the next day.

Lance and Clair warmly invited Ethan and Sabrina to stay. The two accepted rather than find other lodgings.

Though called a Gym, the place was really a large manor converted for that purpose. It had guest rooms, dining rooms, baths, and every other convenience, all luxuriously furnished. Inviting two outsiders who were not Dragon Tamers to stay there showed how thoroughly the clan had accepted them.

Hmm. If this were another game, my reputation with the Dragon Tamer clan would have reached Friendly by now, at least.

After a brief rest, Ethan and Sabrina were invited to dinner.

A five-meter wooden table stood in the dining room, set with tall glasses and spotless white plates. Ornate draperies, Pokemon ornaments, and a marble floor gleamed beneath the bright lights. Even their knives and forks were finely worked silver. Ethan took it all in, impressed by the clan's wealth.

At the other end of the table, Lance and Clair had changed out of their battle clothes into everyday wear.

Clair was quite attractive, Ethan thought, once he looked past her unusual hairstyle. Her firm muscles also gave her a less delicate appearance than many other female Trainers.

"A toast to our victory!"

"Cheers!"

Conversation flowed through dinner, though Lance and Ethan did most of the talking. Clair added a comment now and then. Sabrina said nothing, eating her meal with her usual elegance.

They drank expensive red wine. Ethan could not tell good wine from bad; it all tasted bitter and sharp to him. The fine food was delicious, but he could not say exactly what made it better than ordinary ingredients. Even so, he thoroughly enjoyed the meal.

Afterward, Clair arranged a separate room for each of her guests. Evidently, she had not realized Ethan and Sabrina were a couple. She thought they were simply traveling companions.

Ethan could hardly explain it to her. He gave Sabrina a rueful smile as she teased him with a look, then went helplessly into his own room.

Once inside, he put the awkwardness behind him and went straight to wash the dust from his body. Humming a song he had liked back on Earth, he soaked comfortably in a hot bath until the day's fatigue eased away.

He had only just lain down on the bed when Gengar sidled over with a leering grin.

"What's with you?" Ethan gave it a puzzled look. "Why are you smiling like that? Did you find some money?"

"What would I do with money? It's just paper." Gengar rolled its eyes, then chuckled. "I've got something good to show you!"

It reached into its body and pulled out a black Poke Ball from somewhere inside.

Huh? Ethan looked closer. It was one of Vicious's Dark Balls.

"A Dark Ball?!" His eyes widened. "How did you get that?"

"Heh heh." Gengar grinned, clearly proud of itself. "Everyone saw me destroy the Dark Balls Vicious had. What they didn't see was that I secretly kept the unused one."

"Well, look at you." Ethan shook his head. "That was quite a trick. You fooled me, Sabrina, Lance, and Clair. We really thought you'd smashed every ball you had in your hands."

He took the Dark Ball and examined it. Apart from its black shell and a dozen or so gray stripes, its shape was much like an ordinary Poke Ball.

He turned it over in his hands, but felt no excitement. With a Master Ball already in his possession, he had no desire to use this device. As an honorable Trainer, he would never steal someone else's Pokemon. Besides, a Pokemon captured by a Dark Ball became evil, its mind badly affected.

"Well, now that I have it, I'll keep it as a souvenir. A collector's piece." Ethan sighed. "I hope I never have to use it."

Chapter 312: The Girl in Kalos

After putting the Dark Ball safely away, Ethan leaned back against his soft pillow and looked out the window.

Stars filled the night sky. A clear moon faced the window, spilling a pale beam across his bed.

A faint snore came from the foot of the bed. Jolteon was curled up on the rug, fast asleep. Dratini lay coiled close against Jolteon's belly and slept just as soundly.

Perhaps their drowsiness was catching, or perhaps the warm, reassuring room was getting to Gengar too. It gave an enormous yawn, wished Ethan good night, and slipped into the shadows.

Everything fell quiet. For a moment, it felt as though he were the only person left in the world.

Ethan smiled faintly, turned off the light, and picked up his phone from the bedside table.

Several new messages waited on the screen. He opened the first one in his messaging app. It was from Misty.

She, Ash, and Brock had set out from Pallet Town, crossed the Indigo Plateau, and reached Johto. Their first stop was Professor Elm's lab in New Bark Town. They planned to give him the mysterious GS Ball, which had left both Professor Oak and Professor Ivy at a loss, so he could study it.

Misty had sent the message at noon. By now, Ethan supposed, Ash had probably reached New Bark Town and registered for the Silver League.

As for Brock being with them again, things appeared to have played out much as Ethan remembered from the animated series. Tracey had returned from the Orange Islands with Ash and Misty, then stayed at Professor Oak's lab after meeting the professor he admired. Brock had rejoined the journey, bringing the old trio back together.

Ethan sent Misty a short reply and scrolled through the older messages. He went back dozens of entries. Apart from greetings and good wishes from his friends, nearly all of them came from a girl far away in Kalos.

He sighed.

His feelings about her were complicated. After winning the Indigo League tournament, they had exchanged messages often for a while, and their relationship had begun to improve. Then he had become intimate with Sabrina. Spending every day with her, with his thoughts fixed on her, Ethan had gradually stopped writing to the girl in Kalos.

She still sent a message every few days, but he usually acted as though he had not seen it. He and Sabrina had been inseparable, and between traveling and battling, he had not had the energy to consider the girl's feelings.

Now that he was alone, his mind settled. For the first time in a while, he let himself think about what had happened between them. Reading line after line of her messages, he could almost see her standing before him, speaking sadly in a low voice.

He sighed again. After hesitating for a long time, he could not bring himself to keep ignoring her. His fingers moved across the screen.

All Is Bubbles: Are you asleep?

A few minutes passed without an answer. She must have gone to bed, Ethan thought.

He set the phone aside and stared at the ceiling, remembering the time they had spent together.

Her name was Serena. Yes, the beautiful girl who would one day travel with Ash and have feelings for him.

When Ethan first arrived in the Pokemon world, he had landed unconscious in a heap of grain on her family's farm. Serena found him there and pulled him away from a group of Rhyhorn that had gathered around him.

He stayed at her home for a time, and both Serena and her mother took good care of him. Over the following months, he and Serena spent nearly every day together. They grew steadily closer.

He played with her, helped her learn to ride Rhyhorn, went shopping and ate meals with her, and accompanied her to movies. He comforted her when she was unhappy and encouraged her when she lost heart. In schoolwork and daily life alike, he gave her attention and help until he had become part of nearly every corner of her world.

Eventually, Ethan earned some money from part-time work and saved enough to move out. He rented a place nearby. Moving away did not weaken their bond. With her mother no longer watching them so closely, they grew even closer.

On her way to school, Serena would let herself into his place with a key, wake him up, and bring breakfast her mother had prepared. After school, she would come back and stay until late at night. She spent much of each day following him around; the two were almost inseparable.

Even when Ethan went to work with a theater company, the others would see Serena trailing behind him like a little shadow. Crew members often teased them about it.

Whenever they did, Ethan would joke, "I'm raising my future wife from childhood!"

Serena never contradicted him. She would blush and keep following him. As time went on, she smiled and laughed more around him. She was so comfortable with him that when she was thirsty, she would drink straight from his cup without a second thought.

What Ethan had meant as a joke began to feel less like one. Perhaps his constant care for her had worked as quietly as spring rain. By the time he realized it, he had filled her heart without leaving room for anyone else.

Sometimes he would turn and find her watching him, head tilted, her clear eyes full of affection. That was when Ethan understood how serious her feelings had become.

Of course he liked her. Serena was beautiful; how could he feel nothing? But she was still underage. He could not act on those feelings.

He had cared for her partly out of gratitude for taking him in. She was also an important character from the animated series, and talking with her helped him understand and settle into this world. Her beauty had played a part in how close they became too. Yet he had always thought of her as a younger sister.

From then on, Ethan deliberately put distance between them.

Serena was perceptive. When she tried to visit him as usual, he repeatedly refused to see her. She soon sensed that something was wrong. Confused and frightened, she grew visibly worn over the next several days. At last, one night, she gathered her courage and confessed her feelings, hoping to bring him back.

Ethan still remembered the moonlight that night. It had been as clear as the moonlight falling into his room now.

Serena told him, "I like you." He answered coldly, "I only think of you as a little sister."

She broke down at once. Crying, she went home in a daze. For days she shut herself away, the light gone from her eyes.

Ethan had regretted what he said, but he could not undo it. After struggling with his guilt for a long time, he left Kalos. With the Ralts Diantha had given him, he traveled to Kanto, hoping time would soften what had happened.

As his memory reached that point, the phone beside him lit up. Its dim glow seemed startlingly bright in the dark room, like Serena's eyes used to be. He had not seen them in so long.

He picked up the phone. Serena had written, Pink Dream: I was taking a bath. You're still awake?

She had added a cute smiling face, as if she needed to assure him that she really had been bathing and had not deliberately delayed her reply.

Ethan sighed. How had the girl who used to be so happy become so anxious around him?

His finger hovered over the screen for a long time. At last he changed the subject.

All Is Bubbles: I've reached Johto. I've seen lots of Pokemon that are completely different from the ones in Kalos.

Pink Dream: Johto? I only learned about it in geography class, and I wasn't paying much attention, so I don't know a lot. Is there anything fun to see there? (laugh)

All Is Bubbles: It's not so different, really. Mountains, rivers, and lakes. In many ways it's like Kanto or Kalos.

After a moment's thought, Ethan opened his photos and sent her several pictures he had taken recently.

Pink Dream: Wow, it's beautiful! I'd love to see it. Oh, and congratulations on winning the Orange League! (playful)

Ethan blinked. He had not told Serena about that. How did she know? As though she had guessed what he was thinking, a second message arrived.

Pink Dream: I looked you up! That's how I found out you have a second trophy now. I watched every match. You won them all and became champion! That's amazing!

Ethan sighed again. He had lost count of how many times he had done that tonight.

After rejecting her confession, he had expected Serena to resent him and turn away. Yet her messages still showed how much she cared.

He had thought the passing of time would weaken her feelings, but looking back, he had been too cruel. Even if he had to refuse her, he should not have done it so harshly.

His travels and the time he had spent with Misty and Chloe had changed his perspective. Perhaps instead of avoiding Serena, he could face the problem, help her work through the hurt, and find another way for them to be in each other's lives.

Maybe, after the Silver Conference, I should go back to Kalos and see her.

Chapter 313: Lance's Confidence in Ethan

The next morning, Ethan and Sabrina washed up and ate another generous breakfast. Then Clair led them down to the first floor of the Gym.

Sabrina noticed that Ethan still looked sleepy. She lowered her voice. "Why do you look so worn out? Didn't you sleep well?"

"I slept fine. Why?"

"You have dark circles under your eyes."

Dark circles? Ethan was silent for a long moment. He rubbed his eyes without thinking, then gave her a rueful smile and shook his head.

He had talked with Serena until after one in the morning before they finally said good night. Of course he looked tired after so little sleep. He could not tell Sabrina that, though.

They crossed a long corridor, descended marble steps, and entered a broad hall. This was Blackthorn Gym's battlefield.

It was a combined land-and-water field. The outer area was bare, ordinary ground. In the center stood a square pool of clear water, some four or five meters deep, enough for most Water-type Pokemon to battle in.

Many of Clair's Dragon-type Pokemon were also Water types and fought well in water. Ethan guessed that was why the field had been built this way.

He and Clair walked toward the opposing Trainer areas. On the second-floor spectator balcony, Lance appeared. Rather than sit down, he leaned casually against the railing. He seemed keen to watch their match.

Lance nodded when Ethan and Clair looked up at him. He had barely greeted them when he heard footsteps behind him.

A middle-aged man had climbed the stairs and was walking slowly toward him. He carried himself with a steady authority. His hair was the same color as Lance's, and there was a resemblance in their faces.

"Uncle?" Lance looked surprised. "Clair said you had gone to the Pokemon League. How are you back so soon?"

The man was Blackthorn Gym's former Leader, the current chief of the Dragon Tamer clan, and Lance's uncle.

"I finished my business there, so I came home. I had just walked in when I heard there was a Gym challenge. I came to have a look and found you here."

He stopped beside Lance and studied him. "It has been a long time since I saw you. You look just as you did before."

Lance laughed. "At my age, my face isn't going to change much except to grow older!"

After a few more pleasantries, his uncle changed the subject. "I heard someone broke into the Holy Land yesterday?"

"Team Rocket. I came back because of it," Lance said. "Their leader was a top executive named Vicious. We've dealt with the attack. He escaped, but we handed all the others over to the police. The Elder has ordered the Dragon Tamers still in Johto to hunt him down."

"Well done." The man praised him, then his expression hardened. "Team Rocket dared to enter the Dragon Holy Land. We'll make them answer for it."

He looked toward the field. "I'm surprised you're interested in a Gym battle, though. I didn't think your work as a Pokemon investigator left you time to watch matches."

"It usually doesn't," Lance said with a smile. "I ought to return to Kanto as soon as possible. But the challenger is a friend of mine, and we owe him a great deal for keeping the Holy Land safe yesterday. I wanted to see his match before I go."

"Oh? Your friend helped us that much? He must be strong, then." The man glanced at Ethan.

"He is." Lance's voice turned serious. "Clair won't be able to beat him. She's certain to lose this match."

Clair can't beat him? His uncle was astonished. He knew her strength better than anyone. He would never have stepped down and handed the Gym to her otherwise. How could someone so young be even stronger?

"I've never known you to praise a person this highly," he said. "It isn't like you."

"I can't help it." Lance shrugged. "He's strong, and the speed at which he improves is extraordinary. He impressed me the last time we met. Now I don't even know how far he's come."

The man studied Ethan. It was the first time he had seen that face in person, yet it looked familiar.

"You've said a great deal about him. Which of you is stronger?"

"We haven't battled, so I couldn't say." Lance looked toward Ethan on the field and spoke almost to himself. "But I think we'll find out soon after the Silver Conference."

Ethan had no idea they were discussing him. His attention was on Kabu, who was approaching the field with a small red flag and a green one in his hands.

Taking his place as referee, Kabu raised his voice. "The Blackthorn Gym challenge will now begin! On the green side, Ethan Vale, winner of the Indigo League tournament! On the red side, Clair, Leader of Blackthorn Gym! This will be a three-Pokemon elimination match. The battle ends when all three Pokemon on one side are unable to continue!"

He paused, then added, "Only the challenger may switch Pokemon during the match. Clair, please send out your first Pokemon!"

"Please wait," Ethan said.

Clair and Kabu both turned toward him.

Ethan smiled at Clair. "I'd like to change the rules a little, if you're willing."

"Oh? What would you change?"

"I'd rather not battle the Pokemon you ordinarily use to defend the Gym. Please use your best Pokemon. And let's both send out our first Pokemon at the same time. That seems fairer."

Clair thought back to the power Ethan's Pokemon had shown in the Dragon Holy Land. He had also won the Indigo League tournament. An ordinary Gym team would not be the right test for him.

"All right. I agree. I want to find out whose Pokemon are stronger too."

Up on the balcony, the former Gym Leader finally recognized Ethan when he heard Kabu announce his name.

Of course. That's why he looked familiar!

The young champion had drawn so much attention recently that his name appeared in the news again and again. Even after leaving the Gym and paying less attention to League competitions, the man had heard about Ethan often.

Chapter 314: As If She Knew the Future

Once the two Trainers had settled the rules, Kabu raised his red and green flags level with both hands.

"Both sides, send out your Pokemon together!"

Clair seemed to have chosen already. She took out her first Poke Ball without hesitation and stood waiting, anticipation bright in her eyes.

Ethan smiled. She looked almost impatient to begin. For all her confidence, he did not think her Pokemon would be hard to predict.

This might be a blind selection, but Clair was a Dragon Tamer and a Dragon-type specialist, battling at Blackthorn Gym. She was unlikely to use anything outside the Pokemon she favored for that role. Many Dragon-type Pokemon also had the Flying type, and the field had a pool at its center. A Water/Dragon Pokemon was likely to be somewhere on her team as well.

Those clues narrowed the possibilities and let Ethan rule out several of his own Pokemon with poor matchups. But whom should he choose for the first round?

After thinking it over, he drew the first Poke Ball from the pouch at his right hip.

"I choose you!"

They threw their balls at the same moment. Blue light flashed, and two Pokemon appeared on opposite sides of the pool.

Clair had chosen the Charizard that fought so well against Vicious in the Dragon Holy Land. Ethan had sent out his first partner, Gardevoir.

Like their Trainers, the two Pokemon watched each other across the water, eagerness for battle growing in their eyes.

In terms of typing, Gardevoir's Fairy-type moves would deal only half damage to a Fire-type Charizard. Apart from that, neither had a clear type advantage over the other. Ethan seldom relied on Fairy-type moves anyway, so the resistance would have little effect on his plan. Their raw ability would matter more.

Kabu looked from one side to the other. Both were ready. He swept his flags down.

"The match begins!"

The two Trainers called orders almost at once.

"Gardevoir, Confusion!"

"Charizard, take to the air!"

Gardevoir struck from a distance with Confusion. Charizard had just lifted off and twisted aside, narrowly avoiding it. As it circled overhead, it found its chance and opened its mouth to use Flamethrower.

Fire poured toward Gardevoir. Facing the rolling heat, it used Teleport and reappeared safely out of the flames.

The first exchange had been a test. Neither Pokemon had landed a hit.

Clair frowned and ordered Charizard to keep attacking, hoping to force Gardevoir to spend all its time fleeing and set the pace herself. But Gardevoir escaped every attack with Teleport.

"Tch. Psychic-type Pokemon are such a nuisance," Clair muttered. She was hardly the first Trainer to feel that way.

Outside a game, psychic powers could be formidable. Confusion and Teleport caused an opponent no end of trouble. Worse, Clair could not tell where Gardevoir would reappear, so she could not prepare her next command. She could neither strike it nor pin it down.

Gritting her teeth, she called, "Charizard, cover a wider area! Don't leave it anywhere to escape!"

The battle went on. Ethan, unlike Clair, looked calm.

He knew Charizard too well to panic. He had one of his own and spent every day with it. Though Clair's Charizard differed in temperament and build, its moves and habits were much the same.

Ethan imagined himself in Clair's place, thought through how he would attack Gardevoir with a Charizard, and let Gardevoir read those thoughts through their telepathic connection. That gave it warning of what Clair's Charizard might do next.

Gardevoir defended, then counterattacked. Charizard began to take damage. To Clair, it felt as though Gardevoir could foresee its every move. The longer they fought, the worse the battle felt.

This is trouble... Only by facing Ethan herself did Clair fully appreciate how powerful his Pokemon were. The undefeated Indigo League tournament champion was a difficult opponent indeed.

More unsettling still, she had never felt this helpless even when battling Lance. Could Ethan be stronger than him?

Charizard's stamina was running low, and its breathing had grown ragged. When it exposed an opening, Gardevoir fired Psybeam, leaving it briefly confused. Then Psychic locked its body in place. Gardevoir followed with Hypnosis, sending it falling from the sky.

Charizard's eyelids grew heavy. Its awareness faded, and its strength seemed to leave it all at once. Headfirst, it dropped toward the pool in the center of the field.

Whoosh!

Tens of meters remained. Then only a dozen. Then a few.

"Charizard!"

It showed no sign of waking. Clair's expression changed. Another Pokemon might wake from the shock of hitting the water, but Charizard was vulnerable to it. Above all, the flame on its tail could not be allowed to go out.

She snatched out its Poke Ball and recalled it just before it struck the pool. In doing so, she conceded the round.

Kabu raised the green flag, his expression complicated. "Charizard is unable to battle. Gardevoir wins the first round!"

Looking down at the Poke Ball in her hand, Clair sighed. She could not entirely hide her disappointment. "I lost."

Ethan smiled and shook his head without speaking. After Hypnosis, he had intended to use Dream Eater to finish Charizard. Clair's recall had given him the victory before he needed it.

Chapter 315: A Traitor to Dragon Types

From the spectator balcony, Lance applauded softly when the first round ended. He turned to his uncle.

"See? I told you he was strong. Clair couldn't do a thing against him!"

"He does have some skill."

The middle-aged man gave Ethan another look. After that restrained praise, he turned and left.

As chief of the Dragon Tamer clan, he had little desire to watch a member he valued fight a battle that seemed destined to end in defeat. Better to spare himself the frustration and leave.

On the field, Clair took out her second Poke Ball. Ethan called Gardevoir back to his side, but did not return it to its ball. It would watch the next round from nearby.

They released their Pokemon at the same time again. Clair chose Altaria, and Ethan chose Crystal Steelix.

As the blue light faded, each studied the other's Pokemon with interest.

Clair admired Crystal Steelix. No matter how many times she saw its clear, crystalline body, she found it beautiful. If only there were a Dragon-type Pokemon that looked like that...

Ethan was seeing a real Altaria for the first time. He gave it a longer look too. Its sky-blue, birdlike body was almost hidden by soft wings resembling cotton clouds. It looked charming and light.

For a Dragon type, it looked far more like a bird. Almost by instinct, Ethan raised his Pokedex toward it.

"Altaria, the Humming Pokemon. Dragon and Flying type. On clear days, Altaria flies freely among the clouds. Its cottony wings carry it high on rising air currents, and it sings in a beautiful high voice. Listeners can become so enchanted by its melody that they feel as if they are dreaming."

Despite Altaria's Dragon type, its birdlike appearance had once earned it a joking place beside Druddigon and Flygon among Pokemon called embarrassments to dragons. Once it Mega Evolved into Mega Altaria, its type changed to Dragon/Fairy. It could then turn its Fairy power against other Dragon types, earning an even grander joke title: traitor to dragons.

Ethan had not expected Clair, a Dragon Tamer, to make such a famously unusual Pokemon one of her best partners.

The matchup was awkward for him. Crystal Steelix's Ground-type moves would not affect a Flying-type Altaria, while Flying-type moves could still hit Steelix normally. That took away a large part of his offense. Fortunately, Steelix still knew Rock-type moves after evolving, and its Steel type gave it another way to fight back.

Clair saw the matchup too and smiled. "Looks like I have the type advantage this time."

Ethan nodded. He could have chosen Charizard or Gyarados and tried to overpower her more easily. Instead, he wanted to use the match to train his other Pokemon, especially in even or unfavorable battles.

As soon as the round began, Clair attacked with the same boldness she had shown before. Altaria pressed Crystal Steelix hard.

Ethan did not have Steelix burrow rapidly in and out of the ground as he had done against Falkner at Violet Gym. He kept it on the surface, counterattacking and watching for a gap in Altaria's movements.

But Rock Tomb failed to catch Altaria, and it easily dodged Stone Edge. Every Rock-type attack missed.

Altaria kept striking. Crystal Steelix's enormous body made it seem clumsy by comparison, leaving it largely on the defensive.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

Attacks landed across Steelix's body. Its crystalline surface was exceptionally hard, though, and Altaria had no move that could exploit a weakness. Even when its strikes connected, they dealt little damage.

The standoff lasted some time. When Altaria came in once more with Wing Attack, Ethan narrowed his eyes.

"There's our chance! Dragon Breath, now!"

Crystal Steelix raised its head and breathed a blue-green flame that engulfed Altaria.

"What?!" Clair was startled. Then she shook her head at herself. "My mistake. I so rarely battle a Steelix that I forgot it can use Dragon Breath."

"I can't blame you," Ethan said. "There are so many kinds of Pokemon. Who remembers every move outside the ones they see often? Even a Pokemon professor must forget some."

Ethan himself did not know why an Onix that evolved into Steelix could learn Dragon Breath. Perhaps the old stories of snakes becoming dragons were not mere tales. A Pokemon that lived deep underground might have picked up a trace of dragon power.

He turned his attention back to the field. The surprise Dragon Breath had caught Altaria unprepared and dealt double damage, finally making up some of Steelix's disadvantage.

Altaria emerged with burns over much of its body. It flew back into the air and regarded Crystal Steelix warily.

"Don't be afraid!" Clair shouted. "Just be careful. Come in again with Wing Attack!"

Altaria dived once more, but its newfound caution dulled the fearless force of its earlier attacks.

"Crystal Steelix, Gyro Ball!"

Steelix's great body began to spin faster and faster. The force of its rotation made the whole Gym tremble.

Altaria could not pull out of its dive in time. It struck the spinning Steelix and was thrown back onto the ground.

Ethan seized the opening. "Iron Tail!"

Crystal Steelix swung its heavy tail down at Altaria before it could rise.

Bang!

The blow drew a pained cry from the Pokemon whose voice was usually so beautiful.

Chapter 316: Why Isn't It Afraid of Water?

"Altaria!" Clair cried.

Before Altaria could rise from the ground, Ethan gave Crystal Steelix another command. "Crunch!"

Steelix opened its enormous jaws and bit down. Its mouth was so large that it caught most of Altaria's body.

Creak...

Everyone could hear the pressure of its teeth against Altaria's skin. Altaria screamed again, the sound making Clair's heart clench.

Fortunately, Steelix's teeth were not especially sharp, and Altaria's thick, cloudlike feathers cushioned it. The bite did not break the skin or damage its bones or organs. It still hurt terribly. Caught in those jaws, Altaria might as well have been trapped in an enormous vise.

"Altaria, break free!"

Clair's voice seemed to rouse it. Altaria gathered its strength despite the pain and attacked from inside Steelix's mouth. A violent explosion forced the jaws apart, and it escaped.

It was battered and barely able to stay upright, with little of the grace it had shown when Ethan first saw it.

Crystal Steelix stood tall, head raised. Black smoke poured from its mouth. Altaria's attack had done considerable damage; blood flowed, and Steelix was dazed for a moment. Its brain had not been injured, though. As soon as it recovered, it turned its huge head and glared at Altaria.

Ethan sighed softly. Before evolving, his Crystal Onix had been a calm Pokemon, the sort to lie quietly in a corner without making a fuss. After evolution, both its shape and its temperament had changed. It had grown more savage. Its fierce appearance now rivaled Gyarados's. Anyone faced with that giant head and its blood-red eyes would feel a chill.

Its hard, rocklike body felt cold too, without a trace of warmth. It almost seemed cold-blooded. Magikarp had changed just as dramatically when it evolved. Perhaps the more harmless a Pokemon appeared at first, the fiercer it became later.

Ethan had only ten Pokemon in his regular team, and two had already drifted toward that savage temperament. He was not sure whether that was good or bad.

Across the field, Clair clenched her jaw. Altaria looked too exhausted to go on. One more attack from Crystal Steelix might finish it.

I can't let this continue. If she lost, Ethan would lead two rounds to none. Winning three straight with her final Pokemon would be nearly impossible. She had to turn the situation around. Even if Altaria fell, it needed to take one of Ethan's Pokemon down with it, leaving her some chance in the last round.

Her gaze fell on the pool, and she had an idea.

"Altaria, Take Down! Knock it into the water!"

Ethan nodded to himself. Given the situation, it was a good choice. Altaria would be hurt too, and wet feathers would keep it from flying again. But if it pushed such a heavy Steelix into the deep pool, surely Steelix could not climb back out. Clair hoped for a mutual knockout.

Against an ordinary Steelix, the plan might have worked. Against Crystal Steelix, it would not.

Altaria caught its breath, spent the last of its strength, and charged as though it had no intention of returning.

Bang!

Crystal Steelix did not dodge. Its solid body tilted backward, and both Pokemon fell into the pool behind it.

Yes! It worked! Clair's eyes lit up. In her excitement, she did not wonder why Ethan, who clearly understood her plan, had never told Steelix to evade.

Water surged over the edge of the pool and spread across the field until it soaked the tops of Clair's shoes. She ignored it and stared into the water.

The pool churned. Then a huge, translucent crystal head rose from beneath the surface. Clair could scarcely believe what she was seeing.

"Crystal Steelix? How is that possible? Why is it all right in there?"

She had imagined two outcomes: both Pokemon falling and the round ending in a draw, or Steelix remaining trapped underwater while Altaria somehow won. Steelix emerging unharmed had never occurred to her.

Then a thought struck her. "When your Steelix changed form, did its typing change too?"

"No," Ethan said, shaking his head. "It just gained a resistance to water. When I first found it, it was already living underwater."

Clair stood stunned for a moment, then sighed. "I see. Life really is remarkable..."

Altaria had still not surfaced. Worry creased Clair's brow.

Seeing her concern, Ethan snapped his fingers.

Snap!

Crystal Steelix raised its tail from the pool. Altaria was wrapped tightly around it, unconscious.

Altaria...

Ethan gave a small wave. Steelix flicked its tail and tossed Altaria down in front of Clair. It rolled a few times, then lay limp and motionless. Water spilled from its mouth. It was completely out cold.

At the edge of the field, Kabu spared his young mistress a few moments of sympathy before raising the green flag again.

"Altaria is unable to battle. Crystal Steelix wins the second round!"

Another loss... Clair recalled Altaria. She looked at Ethan with a complicated expression, thought for a moment, and made up her mind.

"Ethan, before we start the third round, I have a request."

"A request?"

Clair nodded. "Will you send out your Gyarados for the final battle?"

Chapter 317: Which Gyarados Is Strongest?

"You want to challenge my Gyarados?" Ethan was surprised. "Why that one in particular?"

"No special reason," Clair said, shaking her head. "I watched it fight in the Dragon Holy Land yesterday. Its opponents weren't strong enough to bring out everything it could do, but its scars and the way it fought left a deep impression on me. I want my Gyarados to face it and see which one is stronger."

A match between two of the same species... Neither Pokemon would have a type advantage. It would come down to their strength and their Trainers' decisions. In a fair contest, it might be Clair's only chance to recover the match.

But did she truly want this?

Ethan considered it. Among the Pokemon he currently owned, Gyarados was one of his strongest, behind only Gengar and the three Legendary birds. It had grown through battle after battle. Jolteon's type advantage made it a difficult opponent for Gyarados, but even Gardevoir and Charizard could not beat it.

Clair, you've chosen the wrong opponent.

Hoping to spare the Dragon-type Gym Leader some embarrassment, he asked once more. "I don't mind, but are you sure?"

Clair answered with a solemn nod.

"All right." Ethan shrugged. "If it's what you want, I have no reason to refuse."

He recalled Crystal Steelix and took out Gyarados's Poke Ball. They released their Pokemon together. Two flashes of blue light brought a pair of Gyarados onto the field.

Clair's was clean and unmarked. Ethan's body was covered in scars, giving it a far more fearsome appearance.

Roar!

As soon as it appeared, Ethan's Gyarados threw back its head. Its piercing cry echoed through Blackthorn Gym. Lance and Clair both frowned, taken aback by the sheer force of its presence. Having grown up among powerful Pokemon, they knew that a Gyarados's bearing often reflected its strength. This one was clearly exceptional.

Kabu was older and closer to the field. The roar hurt his ears enough that he covered them at once.

Clair's Gyarados stiffened under the other's display. It roared back, but its voice lacked the same force. In presence alone, it had already fallen well behind.

Kabu rubbed his ears. Seeing both sides ready, he signaled the start of the third round.

Clair began to give orders. Ethan, however, stood with his hands in his pockets. He said nothing and simply watched, as if the match had nothing to do with him.

Why isn't he commanding his Pokemon? Clair barely had time to wonder before Ethan's Gyarados charged on its own.

She focused on the field. "Gyarados, Dragon Rage!"

A blue-violet wave burst from her Gyarados's mouth. Ethan's ducked under it, closed the distance, and opened its jaws to bite.

Clair's Gyarados fought back. The two Pokemon bit and slammed into each other, wrestling from the ground into the pool and back onto the ground. Water sprayed across the field, and the force of their struggle made the Gym tremble.

Before long, Clair's Gyarados began to falter.

The one before it seemed unable to feel pain. Whatever blows Clair's Pokemon landed, Ethan's ignored them and answered more savagely, striking vulnerable spots with frightening precision. Scales fell away. Blood ran. In moments, Clair's Gyarados was covered in wounds.

"This..."

Clair and Kabu stared. Even Lance, watching from above, looked grave after his first shock. Ethan's Gyarados was extraordinarily strong.

It fought with raw instinct, as though engaged in a fight for survival. It scarcely seemed like the same creature Lance had watched the day before. He glanced at Ethan. The young Trainer remained calm; clearly, this was not the first time his Gyarados had fought this way.

But why was it not using moves? Was this its usual style?

Clair's Gyarados could barely fight back now. It struggled under one attack after another. Clair could not bear to watch it take any more punishment.

She clenched her teeth. "Stop. I concede..."

The match had been one-sided. Ethan's Gyarados had not knocked its opponent out in a single blow, but its easy dominance over another of its kind showed the difference between them. Blood from Clair's Gyarados was close to staining the pool red.

Only now did Clair understand why Ethan had seemed reluctant to use his Gyarados. It was too late to change her request.

Normally she would never concede in the middle of a round. This fight had forced her to set aside her pride as a Dragon Tamer and bow her head.

Kabu raised the green flag high. "The Gym challenge is over! Clair has conceded. Ethan Vale is the winner!"

Ethan recalled Gyarados at once. He feared that the sight of blood might drive it into a frenzy and make it attack Clair's Pokemon again.

Lance came down the stairs and joined them. "Well, Clair? Was that defeat convincing enough for you?"

His teasing earned only silence. For Clair, the loss had been complete. After the last two rounds, she thought she would have trouble watching Pokemon bite one another for some time. Both Altaria and Gyarados had fallen beneath an opponent's enormous jaws.

Lance turned to Ethan. "How did you raise that Gyarados? It's powerful, but more than that, its presence and ferocity are unlike any Gyarados I've seen."

Ethan had wondered the same thing. Had he somehow struck its head too hard when it evolved? Was there something loose in its mind that left it eager to grapple and bite instead of fighting at a distance?

"Maybe it kept more of its wild nature," he said with a smile after a moment. "Pokemon raised by Trainers often come to focus on the power of their moves. They don't like trading injury for injury, and they rely less on instinct."

There was something else Ethan did not say. To many Pokemon, a battle was a contest fought by rules. To his Gyarados, it was always a struggle for survival.

"I see." Lance's eyes lit up. "Well, you have an Elite Four member standing right here. Why not make the most of it and battle me? I'm getting impatient to try!"

"No, thanks." Ethan shook his head without hesitation. "I'm not planning on that yet. Let's wait until after the Silver Conference."

Chapter 318: Larvitar

As Sabrina came over to Ethan's side, Clair took the Blackthorn Gym Badge from inside her clothes.

"Please accept this. It's the Rising Badge, proof that you defeated me."

Ethan thanked her and took the still-warm badge. He turned it over a few times in his hand.

The Rising Badge was shaped like a blue dragon's head. Its smooth, mirrorlike surface had no facial features or other clear details, though, so he could not tell which Dragon-type Pokemon it was meant to resemble. At first glance, it looked more like a dark steel mask.

After he placed it in his badge case, Lance asked casually, "Where are you going next? Another Gym?"
