Ethan weighed his answer. Lance had fought Team Rocket alongside him before, so Ethan told him about his plan to go to Mahogany Town and take on the organization. As an Elite Four member and a Pokemon investigator, Lance was hardly someone from whom he needed to keep it secret.

"What? There's a Team Rocket base there?"

Lance, Clair, and Kabu were all stunned. Mahogany Town was close to Blackthorn City and its Gym. Team Rocket had been hiding almost under their noses, and none of them had known.

"That's frightening," Lance said with a sigh, rubbing his chin. "Team Rocket is so close, and we haven't noticed a thing. Have they infiltrated the region that thoroughly?"

Clair suddenly thought of something and frowned. "If there really is a base in Mahogany Town, Vicious's hideout could be there. Now that he's escaped, there's a good chance he'll return to it."

"You're right." Lance considered this for a moment, then looked seriously at Ethan. "Are you certain the information about the base is reliable?"

"It should be. Looker told me, and I expect the International Police have already verified it." Ethan nodded. "He asked me to help deal with Team Rocket's executives. Do you want to come? I'm sure Looker would welcome you."

Lance thought it over. At first he seemed ready to agree, but in the end he shook his head.

"I'm afraid I can't this time. I'd like to arrest those people and repay them for the attack on the Holy Land, but there's something more important waiting for me in Kanto. I've already spent too long here, and I can't put it off any longer. Besides..."

He drew out the last word and smiled. "Besides, with you there, those Rockets won't stand a chance. You won't need me!"

"..." Ethan gave a wry smile and shook his head. "I'm just an ordinary person. You think too highly of me."

"Ordinary?" Lance laughed. "An ordinary person wouldn't beat Clair so thoroughly that she couldn't fight back!"

Clair rolled her eyes at being made the butt of his joke, but she could not argue.

Ethan knew Lance had a point. Looker and the others must have planned the operation carefully. Lance's presence would help, but the raid could go ahead without him. Ethan had invited him on a whim, so he did not press the matter.

"Well, I've accomplished what I came for. I should be going."

"Take care!"

Lance and the others escorted Ethan to the entrance before waving him goodbye.

...

Ethan and Sabrina left Blackthorn City and headed northwest. Their destination was Mahogany Town, a little over half a day's journey away.

This time Ethan did not retrace their route through the Ice Path. Instead, they crossed the mountains by the road on the surface, which was not particularly rough.

Several days remained before the ten-day deadline Looker had given him. They had more than enough time, but Ethan no longer felt like sightseeing anywhere else. With a major battle approaching, he had grown more settled and focused.

They walked in the sunshine while Charizard flew overhead with their luggage. Ethan and Sabrina traveled light, carrying only a small backpack each.

As they left the mountains surrounding Blackthorn, the forests on either side of the road grew thinner. They walked shoulder to shoulder, talking and laughing all the way. Anyone who saw them might have thought they were a couple in love out for a trip together.

Going downhill was easier than climbing, but the forward pull of their weight and the need to watch every step still made it tiring. After a little more than an hour, Sabrina's clothes were soaked with sweat.

Fortunately, the mountain road was not steep. They stopped now and then and treated the journey as a chance to enjoy the scenery.

Just as they were coming out of the mountains, Ethan caught a glimpse of a Pokemon darting among the loose stones beneath a cliff.

"Huh? Was that...?"

He turned to look. A small gray-green Pokemon was rummaging through the rocks, turning them over as though searching for mushrooms or something else to eat.

"It's a Larvitar!" Ethan recognized it at once.

He took his Pokedex from his waist pouch. Sabrina noticed and looked at him in surprise.

"You want to catch it?"

"Of course!" Ethan nodded. "Like Dragonite, its final evolution, Tyranitar, is incredibly powerful. It's a pseudo-legendary!"

Pseudo-legendary?

Sabrina had never played the Pokemon games, so she had no way of knowing what he meant. But it was rare for Ethan to show interest in catching a Pokemon, and that made her examine this one more closely. She had never seen a Larvitar before.

A long, blunt horn rose from the top of its head. Its eyes were red, its arms short, and each of its feet had a single toe. A diamond-shaped red plate covered its belly. The center of its tail tapered to a point, surrounded by a ring of flat plates.

It was small, but it had a cool, defiant look. Its eyes in particular made it seem perpetually unwilling to back down.

Unfortunately, Pokemon other than Psychic-types did not interest Sabrina.

Meanwhile, Ethan crept toward Larvitar. Before it noticed him, he pointed his Pokedex at it.

The Pokedex chimed. "Larvitar, the Rock Skin Pokemon. Rock and Ground type. It is said to be born deep underground and to grow by absorbing nutrients from the soil. Eating earth as it goes, it makes its way toward the surface where its parent awaits. After consuming roughly a mountain's worth of earth, it becomes a pupa aboveground and sleeps while it grows."

To be honest, Ethan had never heard such strange information from his Pokedex before. Surely the part about eating dirt was a joke?

Chapter 319: The Fourteenth Pokemon

Ethan stepped lightly toward Larvitar, closing the distance a little at a time. Careful as he was, once he came close enough, it noticed him.

At the sight of a human, Larvitar started and immediately ran.

Trying to escape? No chance!

Larvitar was one of the few Johto Pokemon Ethan especially liked. He had finally found one; he could hardly let it get away.

He snapped his fingers. After Larvitar had run a dozen meters or so, Charizard dropped from the sky and blocked its path.

Larvitar looked up at the much larger Pokemon and its unfriendly expression. Deciding Charizard was best avoided, it hurriedly changed direction and kept running.

It had gone only a few steps when Gengar rose from the shadow of a rock ahead, cackling.

Startled, Larvitar turned again, only to find Ethan in its way. It tried the last direction and came face to face with Sabrina. Surrounded on all four sides, it had nowhere left to run.

Ethan smiled. For some reason, the scene reminded him of a television program in which people cornered a wild boar in a watermelon field. Larvitar was the little boar this time. On a closer look, it even wore much the same expression.

Larvitar opened its mouth and made a threatening sound, apparently hoping to frighten them off. But at only sixty centimeters tall, it did not look threatening at all. Charizard, Gengar, and Sabrina paid it no mind.

"Calm down, Larvitar." Ethan crouched and spoke gently. "Don't be afraid. I'm no good person."

What?

Larvitar bared two rows of sharp teeth. Beside Ethan, Sabrina struggled not to laugh. That opening line sounded strangely familiar.

Ethan took out a Poke Ball and released his Dratini.

Now he truly sounded like a villain trying to tempt someone. "You have two choices. Battle my Pokemon. If you lose, you come with me from now on. If you win, I'll leave you some food to make up for bothering you, and I won't trouble you again. What do you say?"

Larvitar looked around. It could not escape. Then it looked at the rather cute Dratini in front of it and made a series of grunting sounds.

Ethan could not understand. He turned to Gengar. "Hey, Gengar, stop standing there and translate. What did it say?"

Gengar shrugged. "It said, 'In your dreams!'"

"Dreams?" Ethan rubbed his chin and gave Larvitar a sunny smile. "Sorry, that isn't one of the choices. You look rather lonely wandering around looking for food. Come travel with me and you'll have food and lodging covered. You can live in a comfortable Poke Ball, stay out of the wind and rain, and eat delicious meals until you're full every day. I guarantee your quality of life will improve!"

Larvitar was unmoved. If anything, it became still more wary.

Gengar clutched its belly and laughed at Ethan's failed pitch. Ethan rolled his eyes at it, cleared his throat, and stood up.

"All right, enough joking. If you won't choose, I'll take that as agreement to battle!"

He waved Dratini forward. "This capture battle is yours. Go on, Dratini. Show it what you can do!"

Dratini had been eager to begin and charged at once. Larvitar could hardly stand there and take the attack, so it fought back. The two Pokemon collided and were soon locked in a struggle.

Ethan watched Larvitar closely as they fought. He had no intention of giving Dratini further commands. Neither Pokemon had evolved yet, and the fight was unlikely to cause much damage; he could let them work it out for themselves.

After a while, he could see that Larvitar was still weak and knew no advanced moves. It used Bite and Tackle over and over, with an occasional Rock Throw. Even so, it was a little stronger than most wild Pokemon at its stage, or it would not have survived here alone.

Against Dratini, that strength was still no great threat. More importantly, Larvitar kept glancing at Charizard and Gengar while it fought, apparently afraid that they might abandon fair play and attack it from behind. With its attention divided, it could not fight at full strength and gradually fell behind.

After taking several of Dratini's attacks, Larvitar seemed ready to try breaking out again. It knocked Dratini back with a Tackle, then suddenly used Sandstorm.

Keeping that move hidden until now worked remarkably well. Yellow sand surged up without warning. Grit and small stones whirled around the people and Pokemon, briefly blinding them all.

Larvitar chose a direction and ran. But perhaps it made too much noise. Dratini quickly broke free of the sand, caught up, and used Wrap to pull Larvitar to the ground. Exhausted, Larvitar fainted.

"Well done!"

Ethan praised Dratini, then threw an empty Poke Ball at Larvitar. As red light enveloped the wild Pokemon, a white glow suddenly surrounded Dratini too.

Hm? That light... Is it evolving?

Dratini had been with him for a long time and fought many battles, large and small. It was certainly time.

The white light soon faded. A few seconds later, the Pokemon before Ethan was no longer Dratini, but Dragonair. Its body had grown longer and slimmer, covered in sapphire-blue scales. It had bright black eyes, a small white horn on its forehead, and two little winglike fins on either side of its head.

Delighted, Ethan took out his Pokedex again and pointed it at Dragonair.

"Beep! Dragonair, the Dragon Pokemon. A Dragon type. Like its unevolved form, Dratini, it lives in beautiful seas and lakes and is regarded as a sacred Pokemon. Though it has no wings, it is sometimes seen flying through the air. It can control the weather and ride the wind into the sky. A mysterious aura surrounds it, and it stores tremendous energy within its body. By concentrating that energy in the crystal-like orbs on its neck and tail, it can alter the weather."

Larvitar's Poke Ball flashed a few more times, then stopped with a click.

Good. He had caught it too. Today he had gained his fourteenth Pokemon, Larvitar, and Dratini had evolved into Dragonair. Two happy events at once, and another increase in his team's strength.

There was just one problem. The number of Pokemon he carried had reached the limit. Under the rules, he had to send one back immediately.

So which one should he transfer?

Chapter 320: Chain Reaction

Ethan picked up Larvitar's Poke Ball from the ground.

At first, he had planned to send Gyarados back to Cerise Laboratory so it could rest at Cerise Park.

With Blastoise already there, sending Gyarados too would leave him without a Water-type Pokemon and make his team less complete. But Gyarados was overwhelmingly strong. Even at a League tournament, it would be an MVP contender. Bringing it to a Gym battle or a casual fight was practically unfair.

Rather than let it occupy a place on his team, he could send it back and focus on training his other Pokemon, giving them more chances to battle.

But then he reconsidered. They were about to fight Team Rocket. He could not show the three Legendary birds, and Mahogany Town stood beside a lake. This was precisely the sort of place where he might need Gyarados.

I'll send it back after we clear out the Team Rocket base.

Instead, Ethan transferred Venusaur to Cerise Laboratory. It had not battled much lately and was among the second tier of his Pokemon in strength.

Including Blastoise, two of his Kanto starters were now at the laboratory. Both were mild-tempered, so they could keep each other company and should get along well in Cerise Park.

Six Pokemon still isn't enough to carry...

He shook his head, took out his phone, and messaged Chloe.

Ethan: I sent Venusaur back. Could you look after it for me?

She replied almost instantly.

Chloe: Leave it to me! I'll go to the lab when school lets out at noon and take it into the park!

She ended the message with three image stickers: Mission accepted! Salute! Cute!

That girl...

Her reply had come so quickly that Ethan suspected she had set a special alert for his messages. Otherwise, given her personality, she would hardly have been watching her phone constantly during school.

...

Mahogany Town had an atmosphere of suspicion and fear. It was known as a hiding place for ninjas. In ancient times, the town was said to have been their home. As the ages changed, ninjas had gradually lost their place and vanished into history. Apart from people like Koga, who still studied the ninja arts, they were rarely seen.

Once Ethan and Sabrina left the Blackthorn area, the road grew level and the mountains receded far behind them.

A day or two remained before their agreed meeting time. Before entering Mahogany Town, Ethan called Looker to say that they had arrived early.

When Looker heard that Ethan intended to challenge the Gym first, he hurried to stop him.

"Wait a little," Looker said hesitantly over the phone. "Team Rocket has been established here for who knows how many years. They have eyes everywhere. You're a sensitive target and already one of their greatest enemies. If you show yourself in Mahogany Town now, you'll put them on alert."

One of their greatest enemies? Already?

"The International Police and the Elite Four are ahead of me," Ethan said, surprised. "If Team Rocket wants to go after someone, why would they start with me?"

"It's easy to understand." Looker laughed as he explained, sounding almost pleased at Ethan's misfortune. "If I were Team Rocket, I'd go after you first too. The International Police are an official force, and Elite Four members move around so much that they're difficult to find. You won a championship, but you're still a newcomer without a firm base. Isn't it best to stop you before you grow any stronger?"

That made sense. Still, Team Rocket must have thought highly of him to make him a top enemy.

"All right. I'll stay outside town for now. But..."

Ethan told Looker about fighting Vicious alongside Lance in the Dragon Holy Land.

"What? Lance is in Blackthorn City? And the two of you defeated one of Team Rocket's highest executives?"

Ethan confirmed it. Looker's tone changed at once.

"That complicates things... You and Lance appeared in Blackthorn at the same time. Team Rocket won't believe that's a coincidence. They'll think you're coming for them! Damn it. They've been clearing out the base these last two days. Now I see why: they're preparing to evacuate!"

So it set off a chain reaction...

Before Ethan could speak, Looker continued. "We'll have to change our plan. I'll send you my coordinates. Come meet me immediately!"

Looker hung up and quickly sent his location. Ethan and Sabrina exchanged a look and smiled helplessly. They had no choice but to change their destination.

Following the map, they went around Mahogany Town and reached an unnamed forest outside it. From a rise overlooking the trees, they could see the town on the left and Johto's famous Lake of Rage on the right.

The Lake of Rage was also called Gyarados Lake. So many Gyarados lived there that people dared not approach it lightly; that was how it had earned its name.

As they entered the ordinary-looking forest, Ethan could sense many people watching from concealment. He did not even need Sabrina to warn him. They had to be International Police officers guarding the perimeter.

The two fell silent. After several more minutes among the trees, they reached the coordinates and found an International Police camp.

As before, the officers had set up a temporary command post, with patrols moving through the camp. Perhaps they had already been told to expect Ethan and Sabrina. No one stopped them after checking their identities, and they were allowed straight inside.

Ethan put on his Gengar mask before entering the camp.

Heh. At last, that moment has come again!

The instant he donned it, he felt like a theatrical young hero, his whole bearing changed. The mask no longer hid his identity from Team Rocket; everyone there knew who he was. But he was not about to skip the ritual.

An officer guided them into the largest tent. Inside, Ethan found Looker and the bald commander whose codename was Zero. Seeing Zero reminded Ethan of Danmo, but he looked around and did not see Danmo anywhere.

Zero came over first and offered them both a hand.

"We meet again. First, welcome to the operation. On behalf of the International Police, I sincerely thank you both for all the help you've given us."

"You're too kind." Ethan smiled and shook his hand. The mask muffled his voice until he sounded like another person. "Team Rocket has committed countless crimes. The whole world knows it's an evil organization, and it often comes after me as well. Looker is a good friend of mine, too. For public and personal reasons alike, I couldn't stand aside from an operation like this."

Chapter 321: Will and Karen

Ethan greeted Looker. While they were talking, the tent flap lifted and a man and a woman came inside.

Ethan turned toward the sound. He had never met either of them, yet he recognized them at once.

The man had shoulder-length purple hair and wore an elaborate red-and-black outfit. A half-mask concealed his eyes, making his age hard to judge and lending him an air of mystery.

The woman had long, wavy, pale-blue hair. Her yellow strapless top left her waist bare. She was beautiful and smiling, but there was a shadow about her brows that made her look calculating.

"You're here!" Looker stepped forward to introduce them. "These are two more people we've invited to help. On the left is Mr. Will, an expert in Psychic-type Pokemon. On the right is Ms. Karen, who specializes in Dark-types."

It really is Will and Karen. Ethan had not expected them to come too. As in the previous operation, the International Police were clearly taking this one seriously.

Will and Karen were no ordinary Trainers. Both were exceptionally strong. A few years from now, they would join Koga and Bruno as the new Johto Elite Four. It was said that no one had ever seen Will's true face beneath his mask.

Perhaps thinking that Ethan and Sabrina would not know the newcomers, Looker added, "Will and Karen are highly respected here in Johto. We went through the Pokemon League and made quite an effort to contact them. We specifically asked them to join us against Team Rocket."

After introducing Will and Karen, Looker pointed toward Ethan and Sabrina. He began with Sabrina.

"This is Ms. Sabrina, the Gym Leader of Saffron City in Kanto. Like Mr. Will, she's an expert with Psychic-type Pokemon. She's also a master of psychic powers herself."

Will brightened at the name of the beautiful, long-haired woman standing before him.

"So you're Ms. Sabrina!" He bowed like a courteous gentleman. "Meeting you is even better than hearing about you. You're as lovely as your name. Even though you're in Kanto, you're famous among us psychics in Johto. I'm Will. It's a pleasure to meet you!"

Smiling, he waited for a reply. Sabrina merely frowned a little and said nothing.

What? What's going on?

Is she really that rude? He had been polite and praised both her beauty and her skill. Surely she could at least greet him.

After two awkward seconds, he suddenly remembered that Sabrina was known not only for her powerful psychic abilities but also for her icy, unapproachable manner.

He stiffened. With the others watching in barely concealed amusement, he straightened up in embarrassment.

Looker cleared his throat, pretended not to notice, and pointed to Ethan instead.

"And beside Ms. Sabrina is a good friend of mine. His name is Eth"

Ethan raised a hand and cut him off. He adjusted his mask, then spoke in an exaggeratedly dramatic voice. "Hello. My codename is Player. It's a pleasure to meet you both!"

Not this again!

Looker slapped a hand to his forehead. Even Sabrina turned her eyes away uneasily. Why did Ethan keep doing this? As soon as he put on that mask, he seemed to become someone else.

"Player?" Will and Karen both paused at the codename.

Will looked at the man whose face was covered even more thoroughly than his own and was astonished by his choice of name.

Karen studied Ethan from head to toe with interest. "Player, as in the person playing a game?"

"Exactly!"

"Why choose a name like that?"

Ethan grinned. "Part the clouds and look at reality. This world is one game that never ends, and we're nothing more than data inside it!"

"An interesting way of putting it!" For some reason, Karen nodded solemnly at his plainly absurd joke. "To me, the world really is like a playground. And honestly, compared with Mr. Looker's codename, yours sounds much more normal. Let me introduce myself again. I'm Karen, and I love Dark-type Pokemon. They're strong and wild. I especially love their wicked looks and their powerful dark energy!"

Oh, no. There's something wrong with the way this woman thinks! Ethan was only pretending to be dramatic. Karen, it seemed, was entirely serious.

Will struck a flamboyant pose. "Let me introduce myself again too! My name is Will. I'm a magician who travels the world, training with Psychic-type Pokemon day and night!"

He flicked his hand and produced a bright rose from nowhere, then held its stem between his teeth. To his dismay, the tent fell silent again.

Once the introductions were over, Zero cleared his throat and drew everyone's attention.

"Back to the matter at hand. We've called you together because there's an urgent development."

The mood in the tent turned serious. Everyone stopped joking.

Karen looked at Zero. "Why have we assembled early? Has something changed?"

Zero glanced at Ethan without showing what he thought. For several reasons, he chose not to reveal the cause.

"The situation has changed. Team Rocket has begun evacuating, so we must act immediately."

Looker went to the tactical board and called the others over to assign their roles.

"We'll split up again. Mr. Will and Ms. Karen will lead a team of International Police officers in a raid on a store in Mahogany Town. It may look unremarkable, but it's an important entrance to Team Rocket's underground base."

"And Eth" Looker caught himself. "Player and Ms. Sabrina will come with me and lead the other officers in a raid on a research facility on the shore of the Lake of Rage. That's our most important target. Remember, Team Rocket may put up fierce resistance. We may even encounter their executives. Stay alert and be careful at all times!"

Chapter 322: Raid on Team Rocket's Research Facility

The Lake of Rage was Johto's largest lake. According to local accounts, it had once been a volcanic crater. When the ground collapsed, rainwater filled the hollow and formed the lake they knew today.

To the west lay a vast maze of trees. Whenever it rained, the lake rose and water spread through the forest until the whole place became a marsh. Amphibious Pokemon emerged in numbers, and the woods filled with life. When the rain stopped, the water slowly receded and the trees returned to their usual quiet.

But at some point, the lake's Gyarados had grown unnaturally violent. They began wrecking boats and attacking swimmers, and nearby residents no longer dared approach the water.

Half a month ago, the local police had sent several officers to find out why the Gyarados were behaving this way. They found no answer, but they did stumble upon Team Rocket. Wisely, they made no move against the group. They reported the discovery to the International Police, setting this operation in motion.

Looker led Ethan, Sabrina, and a team of officers to a hillside near Team Rocket's base.

They hid behind the rise and looked toward the lake. A broad clearing had been cut into the once-dense forest, and Team Rocket's secret base stood within it. Even from this distance, the complex looked substantial.

At its center was a modern three-story research building. A signal tower, warehouses, and a power station stood nearby. Team Rocket had even built a small dock for easy access to the Lake of Rage. They had prepared thoroughly; clearly, they had meant to stay for a long time.

Too bad for them. Today, it all ends.

When Ethan's group arrived, many Rocket members were carrying crates out of the warehouses and securing them on vehicles, apparently preparing to leave. Yet they worked without urgency.

"That's a fortunate sign," Looker said with relief. "It's been more than a day since you and Lance appeared in Blackthorn, and nothing has happened to them in the meantime. Maybe they've decided it was only a coincidence. They may be packing in case the worst happens while they wait to see how things develop."

Ethan nodded. Looker's reasoning was clear and made sense.

Sabrina tugged at his sleeve. Following her gaze, Ethan spotted Vicious at once.

He really is here!

Vicious was still riding his mechanical spider. He seemed to be on his way out of the base. Was he going to hunt more Pokemon?

"It's Vicious," Ethan said quietly. "He's leaving. Should we catch him now?"

Looker studied Vicious and thought for a moment. "Leave him for now. We can send someone to follow him. Our people haven't finished surrounding the base. If we reveal ourselves now, the others could escape."

Besides the team with Looker, two more International Police teams were circling in from other directions to seal off the research facility. Their routes were long and would take time. Until they were in place, no one could risk alerting the Rockets.

Ethan had no choice but to watch Vicious go. He could not stop him, but he could send Clair a message at once.

Lance had already set off for Kanto after Ethan left Blackthorn City. Clair was the only one he could tell where Vicious had gone. With luck, she could join forces with other Dragon Tamers and catch him.

She replied quickly with only a short Got it. Even so, Ethan could feel her determination.

The sun sank toward the west. The group waited quietly for more than half an hour before Looker received a signal that the other two teams were in position.

He did not give the order to move yet. Instead, he checked his phone again. Soon Karen called, and because Ethan stood beside him, he could hear her report clearly.

In Mahogany Town, Karen and Will had already led the International Police into Team Rocket's underground base. Their raid was going well. At first they had met almost no resistance and had taken more than half the base. The Rockets had regrouped and counterattacked, but still could not stop their advance.

They had also found the Rocket executive Lance there and fought him. Karen assured Looker that even an executive was no match for her and Will. She expected to finish clearing the base soon and then come to reinforce the team at the lake.

Ethan nodded. If they captured that Lance, he would be the second Rocket executive sent to prison, after Lambda, whom Ethan had caught earlier.

Suddenly the research facility became restless. Its people had probably learned that the Mahogany base was under attack. Some of them left to reinforce the town, while the others began moving their supplies faster.

Now!

Looker's eyes sharpened. "Begin the operation!"

At his command, International Police officers burst from the forest on three sides and ran toward the research building. Speedboats raced across the lake toward the dock as well. The net was closing.

As the officers approached, Team Rocket's detection equipment found them. Alarms rang through the base, and the members who saw the advancing police fell into obvious panic.

"Damn it! The International Police are here too!"

"We're surrounded!"

While they milled about, a tall, rough-looking man in a vest and black gloves came out of the research building with a group of Rocket members.

"What are you panicking for?" he snapped at those trying to flee. Then he looked at Looker and scoffed. "They're only International Police. What is there to fear? Just wipe them all out!"

Either the man had complete faith in his own strength, or he had another advantage in reserve. Ethan knew it had to be the latter. Apart from Team Rocket's familiar trio, its members were rarely foolish enough to rush into a fight they could not win.

Chapter 323: Project R

Looker was not frightened by the man's threats. He studied him, trying to place him among the Team Rocket members in the International Police files. No matter how quickly he searched his memory, he could find no match. The man was clearly no low-ranking officer, though.

"Who are you? Are you in charge here?"

The man folded his thick arms across his chest and stared coldly at Looker. "I'm Tyson, captain of Project R's operations unit. I'm also the man who's about to kill you."

"Project R?" Looker frowned. The unfamiliar name unsettled him. "What is that? Another of your evil schemes?"

"Pathetic," Tyson mocked. "You came charging in without even knowing what Project R is. I suppose you've had quite a stroke of 'luck.'"

"Tell me. What is Project R?"

"Heh. The dead don't need to know so much."

Tyson had no interest in talking further. He swept out a hand, and the Rocket members around him released their Pokemon. More than a hundred Golbat and Weezing filled the sky and the ground. Opposite them stood the International Police officers' Arcanine: a dark wave facing a golden one.

A battle was about to break out. Looker took a deep breath. The Rockets were stubbornly resisting, just as he had expected. He called to them one last time.

"Team Rocket! Give up! You're surrounded, and you cannot escape! Surrender now and spare yourselves needless suffering!"

The only answer was an attack from their Pokemon.

"Arrest them!"

Looker gave the order for a full assault. More than a hundred Arcanine surged toward Team Rocket's Pokemon.

Ethan and Sabrina stayed beside Looker instead of joining the charge. The officers could handle this kind of melee. They needed to save their own Pokemon for the more difficult opponents ahead.

But just as the police began to move, something changed. The running Arcanine looked as though they had been struck by an unseen blow. Every one of them stopped with pain on its face and curled up on the ground, trembling violently.

"What?"

Looker was caught completely off guard. He looked around, unable to see what had happened. No matter how their Trainers urged them on, the Arcanine could not stand. They were in agony and had lost their strength. Team Rocket's Pokemon, meanwhile, were unharmed and kept charging.

"What's going on? Were they attacked?"

Looker's carefully prepared plan had encountered a disastrous complication. He was no longer as composed as before. If the operation failed and the International Police suffered heavy losses, he would bear much of the responsibility.

"Ha ha ha!" Tyson laughed at the disorder among the police. "That's one part of Project R! Weren't you certain you had us beaten a moment ago? Let's see what you can do now!"

Ethan felt the shadow beneath him shifting. He looked down. Even Gengar had emerged from it, clutching its head with both hands, its face contorted in pain.

"My head feels awful!" Gengar's voice shook. "All my strength is draining away, and it feels like something is stirring inside my skull!"

Ethan frowned. Gengar was so powerful that another Pokemon could hardly hurt it without its noticing. Unless...

"Is it some kind of supernatural force?"

"No..." Gengar shook its head with difficulty. "It feels like a radio wave is affecting me. It appeared all at once, and I can't resist it. Wait... I can't take any more. I have to hide somewhere!"

Gengar opened the Poke Ball on Ethan's belt itself and slipped inside. It was the first time since Ethan had caught Gengar that it had stayed in its Poke Ball. The wave had to be hurting the Pokemon badly.

But a radio wave? Why couldn't Ethan feel anything? Was it aimed only at Pokemon? If so, even releasing the three Legendary birds might not help.

"Damn it!" Looker heard Gengar's explanation and understood. "Most of their Pokemon are Golbat and Weezing. If this is a radio-wave attack, they must have adjusted the frequency so their own Pokemon aren't affected!"

While they spoke, Team Rocket's Pokemon reached the officers and began attacking the helpless Arcanine. Arcanine fell one after another.

One officer called urgently to Looker. "Should we withdraw?"

Looking at the field, Looker did not know what to do. Reason told him they had to retreat, but he could not bear to abandon the operation. He turned to Ethan, hoping the friend he trusted might see a way out.

Ethan scanned the base and finally looked up at the signal tower beside the research building.

"A small device couldn't affect this many Pokemon at once. Look there. I think that tower is broadcasting the wave. If we destroy it, the Pokemon should recover."

The signal tower. Looker followed Ethan's gaze, then looked back at the Rockets.

"But how do we destroy it now?"

The signal covered the whole base. Rockets stood between them and the tower, and without their Pokemon, the police could not get near it.

Ethan sighed softly and looked at Sabrina. If the wave did not affect humans, he would need her help.

He did not want the woman he loved to take such a risk in the middle of a chaotic battle. Even Sabrina could not withstand attacks from so many Pokemon without cover.

Sabrina met his gaze, gave no sign of fear, and nodded lightly. After so long together, they understood each other without needing to speak. She knew that with everyone else at a loss, she was the one who would have to act.

Chapter 324: Professor Sebastian

Looker knew that all their hopes now rested on Sabrina.

"Ms. Sabrina, we're counting on you," he said earnestly.

She kept her eyes ahead and did not answer him, but she stepped forward.

For the International Police alone, or even simply to destroy Team Rocket, Sabrina would not willingly put herself at the center of the fight. But Ethan wanted this, and that gave her a reason to act. What mattered to him mattered to her.

Seeing that she would help, Looker ordered the officers to provide cover. They threw smoke bombs in every direction and tried to draw Team Rocket's Pokemon away from her.

Bang, bang, bang...

Dozens of smoke bombs landed in the clearing. Thick white smoke poured out and swallowed the battlefield, finally giving the International Police a moment to breathe. The officers hurriedly recalled their Pokemon, sparing them both the Rockets' attacks and the radio waves.

The remaining officers fired their guns to draw Team Rocket's attention. Their weapons were far weaker than Pokemon attacks. Often they had barely fired a few shots before Golbat and Weezing struck back, pinning them down. Still, the gunfire distracted the Rockets, which was enough to help.

Watching it, Ethan once again marveled at how unevenly military technology had developed in this world. Perhaps people had put their efforts in the wrong direction or relied too heavily on Pokemon. Firearms had advanced slowly; there were few kinds, and their power was limited.

If the operation had helicopter support and plenty of advanced weapons, they might not have been trapped like this.

Then again, Looker and the others were police officers with Pokemon of their own. Who could have expected the battle to turn out this way?

Smoke drifted across the clearing. The poor visibility slowed Team Rocket's advance. Tyson ordered his Golbat to beat it away with their wings, but the delay had already given Sabrina precious time to approach the tower.

A few Pokemon tried to block her path. With a wave of her hand, she knocked each of them aside with psychic power.

Tyson noticed. He frowned at her and ordered the Rocket members beside him, "Don't just stand there! Stop that woman!"

Several of them leaped in front of Sabrina. Before they could do anything, a psychic force hurled them away.

"Move."

Sabrina's hair rose without a breeze. She floated a dozen centimeters above the ground, psychic power wrapped around her in a protective field, and her eyes glowed red. Neither people nor Pokemon could stand against her.

"Tch!" Tyson cursed as his people fell. He had to enter the fight himself.

He released his Pokemon and rushed toward Sabrina, but he was too late. She had reached the tower. Raising both hands, she lifted a Team Rocket truck high into the air with her power.

She glanced at the unfortunate driver inside. His face had gone pale with panic, but she sent the truck hurtling into the signal tower all the same.

"No!"

Tyson arrived in time only to watch it strike. The tower tilted under the impact, and its transmitter exploded.

Boom!

The result was immediate. Without the radio waves, the International Police officers' Pokemon regained their strength. Tyson stood frozen, staring wide-eyed at Sabrina. He could not understand how these people had accomplished so much after losing their Pokemon. That woman in particular was absurdly strong. Could a human truly do this?

With the waves gone, it was finally the International Police's turn to fight back. They released their Arcanine again and launched an all-out attack on Team Rocket.

Ethan considered the field and sent out several of his Pokemon as well. The officers' Pokemon had suffered injuries, and their numbers no longer gave them the advantage they had at the beginning. Without something to change the balance, reaching the research building might still be difficult.

Blue light flashed, and four Pokemon appeared: Charizard, Gyarados, Jolteon, and Scyther. Ethan left the others in their Poke Balls so they could rest and remain ready if needed.

Flamethrower. Discharge. X-Scissor.

His Pokemon often needed only two or three moves to bring down one of Team Rocket's.

Gyarados was especially destructive. Like a tiger among sheep, it charged across the field with its jaws wide, impossible to overlook. Even the Arcanine steered clear, afraid their own ally might hit them by mistake.

With Ethan's Pokemon helping, Team Rocket's defeat came faster and more decisively.

"Damn it!" Tyson gritted his teeth and backed away.

The Rockets gave ground before the assault. As more of their Pokemon fell and more members were arrested, Tyson had no choice but to order everyone into the research building.

The police soon captured every facility outside it. Only at the research building's entrance did a few Rocket members still resist, trying to hold their last line of defense. It was close to breaking.

Inside the building, alarms blared. Researchers and ordinary Rocket members ran in every direction.

One man remained calm. Dressed in a Team Rocket research uniform, he stood before the monitors in a laboratory and watched the screens. He wore small glasses, had bowl-cut hair and a goatee, and had a delicate-looking face. The panic around him seemed to have nothing to do with him.

Tyson and several Rocket members stumbled through a heavy security door and hurried to his side.

"Professor Sebastian! They've taken everything outside the base, and the Mahogany Town base has fallen too. We need to leave through the secret passage!"

Sebastian glanced at him and snorted. "Useless. You couldn't even hold one base, and I gave you Project R to help!"

Tyson flushed but could not argue.

"Have you destroyed all the data?"

Sweat broke out on Tyson's face. "It happened so suddenly that we haven't managed to destroy all of it..."

"Then destroy the rest physically," Sebastian said coldly. "Whatever you do, leave none of the data for the International Police."

He turned away from Tyson and looked with interest at a monitor. On it, a Gyarados had just defeated the last Golbat outside the building and was roaring at the sky.

Light flashed across Sebastian's glasses. He spoke almost to himself, excitement in his voice. "What a magnificent body. What perfect power! Compared with the flawed specimens in the Lake of Rage, this is the Gyarados I truly want!"

Chapter 325: Gyarados Out of Control

Boom!

Gyarados smashed through the research building's doors. The force of the impact sent the Rocket members and their Pokemon behind them sprawling. The last defense outside the building had fallen.

International Police officers poured in and arrested the Rockets who had not escaped. Looker left some officers to handle them, then pressed deeper into the building with Ethan and the rest.

Perhaps the Rockets had lost their will to fight. The group passed through a lobby, corridors, a lounge, and a dining area without meeting any serious resistance. With Gyarados clearing the way, they reached the building's central laboratory in only a few minutes.

The room was filled with electronic equipment. Ethan immediately spotted a man standing at a raised control station, looking down at them. He was so composed that he seemed to have been waiting for their arrival.

Apart from him, the laboratory was empty. No researchers remained, and even Tyson was nowhere to be seen.

Computers had been smashed all around the room. Papers lay on the desks, left behind in the rush, and chairs had been knocked over. The others had fled in a hurry.

Ethan also noticed a small device resembling a signal transmitter beside the man. Electricity danced across it with a dangerous crackle.

Before Ethan could look more closely, the man gave an elegant bow and introduced himself without waiting for Looker to speak.

"Hello. I'm Professor Sebastian. Welcome to my research facility. I've been expecting you for quite some time."

Sebastian was one of Team Rocket's important scientists. Unlike the hot-tempered Dr. Namba or the stern Dr. Zager, he spoke calmly and had a quick mind. He rarely acted on his own, preferring to leave tasks to his subordinates.

"So you're the one in charge?" Looker studied him. "What exactly are you doing here? What is Project R? Tell us the truth!"

Ever since he had heard the project's name, the question had gnawed at Looker. He needed to know the answer.

Sebastian chuckled, showing no desire to conceal it. "The complicated explanation would be wasted on you. In simple terms, Project R stimulates the DNA of Pokemon at their initial stage, forcing them to evolve and making them stronger and more destructive."

"I thought so!" Looker said. "Then you're responsible for the recent Gyarados attacks at the Lake of Rage? And for the reports of a red Gyarados there?"

Apart from the story Ethan remembered, he had heard Blackthorn residents discussing that red Gyarados when he passed through the city. Someone had discovered it recently, and word had spread quickly. According to the witnesses, it was stronger than an ordinary Gyarados, and its red scales made it easy to spot in the lake.

Ethan remembered, too, that Lance would eventually catch that Gyarados and make it an important member of his team.

"That's true," Sebastian admitted, then shook his head. "But it wasn't what we intended. That Gyarados evolved suddenly when we exposed it to the waves as a Magikarp. A great deal of Magikarp DNA remained in its body, turning its scales red. At best, it's a failed specimen. We meant to dispose of it, but it broke out of its enclosure and escaped. That's why you found your way here."

Because Sebastian was speaking so freely, Looker began to think he might be ready to surrender. He let his guard down a little.

"Are you telling us all this because you plan to give up and confess, hoping for a lighter sentence?"

"Give up? No, no, no..." Sebastian smiled softly. "We built this facility here after a thorough search because we saw potential in Magikarp. We thought Project R could make them evolve into even stronger Gyarados for Team Rocket to use. But after so many experiments, the results never met my expectations. I was preparing to change to another Pokemon altogether."

His voice rose with excitement. "Then I saw that Gyarados of yours. I finally understood that my research had been aimed in the wrong direction. If I experiment on a Gyarados that's already powerful, it will become stronger still!"

He fixed a burning gaze on Ethan's Gyarados.

Looker did not understand every detail, but he knew he had to stop whatever Sebastian intended. He pulled a pair of handcuffs from his belt and hurried toward him.

Sebastian gave him a sidelong glance, snorted, and worked the controls in front of him.

The small transmitter beside him swung around. It pointed at Ethanor, more precisely, at the Gyarados behind him.

"What are you doing?" Looker shouted. He rushed forward, but after only a few steps, he saw Sebastian press a red button.

A visible wave burst from the transmitter and struck Ethan's Gyarados directly. It came too fast for Gyarados to dodge, and neither Ethan nor Sabrina could react in time.

Gyarados howled in pain. It twisted violently, its face contorted. Ethan frowned and started toward it, but then he saw the old, scarred scales along its body fall away and new ones take their place. Its whole body swelled visibly, growing far larger, and turned crimson red.

"Ha ha ha! It worked! It worked!" Sebastian burst into laughter before anyone could recover from the shock. "I should have realized it sooner. Experimenting on those dull-witted Magikarp was useless! Only an already powerful Gyarados can become the perfect ultimate weapon. Now you'll see the true power of Project R!"

"You madman!" Looker cried. "What have you done?"

Before anyone could collect themselves, a deafening roar filled the room.

"Raaah!"

Gyarados reared up, its eyes bloodred, and roared toward the ceiling. Its voice was more frightening than ever, and its enlarged body made it an overwhelming sight.

At last, it lowered its head. Everyone looked into its eyes and saw that every trace of reason had vanished. Only limitless anger and a savage urge to attack remained.

Gyarados had lost control completely.

Chapter 326: Nothing but Fury

Gyarados swept its tail sideways, sending several International Police officers flying.

"Gyarados, wake up!" Ethan shouted. "Don't attack our own people!"

No matter how he called, Gyarados gave no sign of hearing him. Its huge tail swung again and nearly knocked Ethan over. Sabrina caught him with her psychic power and pulled him to her side before he could be hurt.

"It's no use..." Blue light flashed, and Gengar emerged from its Poke Ball. It had recovered from its earlier pain. "I saw what happened from inside the ball. That wave has taken control of Gyarados. It can't hear anything you say!"

Gengar was right. Ethan knew the wave was the cause. He turned to the transmitter and told Sabrina, "Destroy it!"

Without hesitation, she waved a hand. Her power swept forward, slicing the transmitter in two as though with an invisible blade. It was ruined.

Yet Gyarados remained in a frenzy. It showed no sign of regaining its senses.

"Ha ha ha!" Sebastian laughed. "Don't waste your strength. It's too late! Endless rage has filled your Gyarados's mind. It will destroy everything it sees. Tremble before its power!"

Ethan took out Gyarados's Poke Ball and tried to recall it. A red beam struck its body but had no effect.

What? It didn't work?

He looked down at the ball. It was still linked to Gyarados; their bond had not been severed. But the Pokemon had become so powerful that, if it resisted, the Poke Ball could not draw it back.

Even the Poke Ball can't stop it...

Gyarados opened its jaws. A great mass of energy gathered in its mouth. It looked ready to use Hyper Beam.

Oh, no! Ethan knew a Hyper Beam unleashed indoors could destroy the entire research building. Before he could act, the energy reached its peak.

Roar!

A thick beam burst from Gyarados's mouth and punched through the laboratory wall. It turned its head, sweeping the attack across the room. Tables, chairs, equipment, and masonry in its path melted under the heat. Windows shattered and walls collapsed.

Everyone fled in confusion to avoid the beam. When it ended, they looked up at the wreckage of the laboratory in horror.

That was far too powerful...

Looker stared. Even Ethan was shocked. This was still Hyper Beam, but its force had increased by more than a single level.

More astonishingly, Gyarados could still move freely after using it, ignoring the move's usual aftereffect. Was this the true power of Project R?

Much of the building had been destroyed. Its damaged structure shed broken pieces of wall from above.

"This place is going to collapse!" Looker shouted to everyone. "Get out, now!"

He himself ran forward, still hoping to arrest Sebastian. Before he could reach the stairs, a support beam fell across his path.

Sebastian gave Looker a meaningful smile. He glanced once more at Gyarados, nodded in satisfaction, opened a hidden door, and disappeared through it.

"Don't run!"

Looker tried to pursue him, but another beam fell and fire sprang up ahead. He had no choice but to turn back to Ethan.

Ethan looked at Gyarados with mixed feelings. It was still tearing the room apart. With a sigh, he ran out of the building alongside Sabrina and Looker.

The collapse had spread into the long corridor. They had covered barely a dozen meters before pieces of ceiling and wall began falling around them. Sabrina held the debris back with her psychic power so they could pass safely.

"Whew!"

They had only just emerged when a tremendous crash sounded behind them. The imposing research building had become a heap of broken walls.

Gyarados rose above the wreckage, still pouring out energy and venting its fury without tiring. To keep the officers out of its path, Looker ordered them to scatter.

"Spread out! Search for the Rockets who escaped. There must be a secret passage nearby. Block them before they get too far!"

Footsteps approached. Will and Karen had arrived with another team of International Police officers to reinforce them. Even from a distance, they had seen the extraordinary Gyarados laying waste to everything around it.

"Incredible..." Karen looked up at it in surprise. "That Gyarados is incredibly strong. Is it Team Rocket's final weapon?"

"No..." Looker glanced at Ethan and smiled bitterly. "It's actually EthMr. Player's Pokemon. Team Rocket took control of it, and now it's out of control."

"Your Gyarados?" Will looked at Ethan and shook his head with a show of composure. "Whatever the cause, we can't let it keep wrecking the place. We just need to stop it, right?"

He released his Exeggutor. "Sorry, but your Pokemon may get hurt!"

Will gave the order. "Exeggutor, use Confusion!"

He expected a simple fight. A frenzied Gyarados would attack in predictable ways and could no longer think clearly.

But Exeggutor's psychic force struck Gyarados and seemed to vanish into it. Gyarados showed no pain or reaction until it noticed the attacker. Then one long-range Hydro Pump knocked Exeggutor out immediately.

What?!

Will stared at his fallen Pokemon. "You're joking! Exeggutor is a Grass- and Psychic-type. It resists Water-type attacks. How could one hit take it down so easily?"

Looker gave him a rueful smile. "Now you understand why we're all standing here with no idea what to do..."

Chapter 327: Surrounded

Gyarados had always been one of Ethan's most dependable partners. In battles large and small, when he left a fight to it, it never disappointed him.

True to its name, its ferocity left a lasting impression on every opponent. Now that it had become their enemy, Ethan and the others understood just how frightening it could be.

"Hey!" Will called to the people still staring. "Stop standing there. Attack together and calm it down!"

He recalled the unconscious Exeggutor and released Jynx and Slowbro. Both were among his main Pokemon. He was taking the fight seriously, determined to recover some pride.

"What a nuisance," Karen muttered. "Can't we just wait until it's finished making a mess and calms down on its own?"

Even as she complained, she released Houndoom and Murkrow.

Looker was Ethan's friend and the officer in charge of the operation. He could hardly stand by. He sent out Arcanine and Croagunk to help.

Seeing the three of them release Pokemon, Sabrina turned to Ethan. "Do you need me to act?"

"Not yet..." Ethan sighed. "Stay back and watch the field. Keep an eye out in case the Rockets return to cause trouble."

He released Gardevoir, Jolteon, and Dragonair. Nine Pokemon spread out and surrounded Gyarados.

It stood amid the ruins, still destroying everything within reach. Nothing around it remained intact after its repeated charges.

Will saw that everyone was ready. "Jynx, Slowbro, use Psychic!"

Two psychic attacks shot toward Gyarados. Ethan knew they needed to calm it as quickly as possible, so he too ordered his Pokemon to attack, though Gyarados was his own partner. Karen and Looker followed. Powerful moves flew from every side and struck Gyarados.

"Raaah!"

After taking so many hits, Gyarados threw back its head and roared. It turned toward its attackers with still more fury in its eyes and gathered water for a counterattack.

"Careful! Its moves are incredibly strong. If one hits you, it might"

Will tried to warn them, remembering what had happened to Exeggutor. Before he could finish, Looker's Arcanine failed to dodge the Hydro Pump and went down in one hit.

Will fell silent.

Looker stared at his fallen Arcanine, scratched his head, and opened his mouth without finding anything to say. He quietly recalled it.

Will shook his head, drew a breath, and forced himself to continue. "...It might knock you out at once. Don't let your guard"

Gyarados charged. Croagunk had leaped forward to fight it at close range, and Gyarados slammed into it before Will could finish. The impact sent Croagunk flying more than a dozen meters, as if a speeding tank had struck it. It crashed into a broken wall and slid down unconscious.

Will fell silent again.

Looker felt Will's speechless gaze and turned away in embarrassment. Both of his Pokemon had been defeated before the man could finish a sentence. It was hard not to feel he had let everyone down.

His Arcanine and Croagunk were experienced battlers, but even with Looker serving as an International Police officer, they were far below Gyarados. It was the Pokemon of a champion, and Project R had made it stronger still.

"Sorry. Arcanine and Croagunk are the only Pokemon I have. I can't help with the rest of this fight."

"Never mind," Will said with a wave. "Leave it to us."

The remaining Pokemon kept fighting. It was less a direct battle than a series of attempts to keep Gyarados busy. They dodged its attacks, worked together, and struck whenever an opening appeared. A single hit could end the fight for any one of them.

The struggle went on. Gardevoir's Psychic landed. Jolteon's Discharge hit too. Yet Gyarados continued to stand after taking attack after attack.

Karen stared in disbelief. "Is that really a Pokemon? It's taken more than a dozen hits and it's still fighting like this. What kind of monster did you raise?"

Ethan rubbed his nose and did not answer. He felt embarrassed for causing everyone so much trouble.

When the fight still showed no sign of ending, he said quietly to Gengar, "You go too."

Gengar nodded gravely. "I understand."

It vanished from Ethan's sight and appeared behind Gyarados an instant later. Gengar formed a ball of dark energy and struck while Gyarados was unaware. As Gyarados turned to bite, Gengar disappeared again and escaped.

With its target gone, Gyarados vented its anger on the others. Jynx was preparing another attack when Gyarados rushed in and swept it away with its tail. Murkrow tried to attack from above, but a Hyper Beam struck it and sent it spiraling to the ground.

Both were unable to battle. In only a few minutes, the group had lost four Pokemon. Will looked miserable. He had a sinking feeling that they might not be able to stop Gyarados.

Karen alone showed a flash of admiration. As she had said when she introduced herself, a powerful, wicked-looking Pokemon held a special appeal for her.

Fortunately, Gengar reappeared and traded attacks with Gyarados, drawing its attention. On Ethan's order, Jolteon used Thunder.

Dark clouds gathered overhead. More than ten seconds passed. Then, with a tremendous crash, a huge bolt of lightning struck.

Perhaps its rage had blinded it to danger. Gyarados made no attempt to dodge and took the full force of the long-charged attack. At last, the rampage ended.

Chapter 328: A Blessing in Disguise

Thud!

Gyarados's enormous body crashed to the ground, and its weight made the earth tremble under everyone's feet.

After all that effort, and an entire Team Rocket research building collapsing, we've finally stopped it...

Still, so many Pokemon had surrounded one frenzied Gyarados, only for several of them to be knocked out. It would be embarrassing if word spread. These were not wild Pokemon; they were the trained mainstays of their Trainers' teams.

They were fortunate that Will and Karen, future Elite Four members, had joined the fight. If everyone had been an International Police officer like Looker, dozens or even a hundred Pokemon might have fallen, and there might have been other casualties too.

Yet judging from Will's and Karen's Pokemon, they still had some distance to go before reaching Elite Four strength. They needed more time to train their teams.

Ethan walked to Gyarados's head. After exposure to the wave, it had grown so much that its enormous head was nearly as tall as he was.

Its body was charred, but it had not fainted. It lay helplessly on the ground, weakly holding its eyes half open. Thankfully, the madness had gone from its gaze. Ethan saw its old gentleness there and finally let out a breath.

"Calm again, are you? You caused everyone a lot of trouble, even if you didn't mean to..."

The battle reminded Ethan of when Gyarados had first evolved from Magikarp. It had gone mad then too, and only settled down after taking a beating much like this one. They had somehow gone through the same thing again.

Even so, something good had come of the disaster. The wave had driven Gyarados out of its mind and caused all that destruction, but it had also changed its size and color and increased its strength.

Every aspect of Gyarados now seemed to have reached its limit. Ethan felt certain there was no stronger Gyarados anywhere in the world. If he used its Mega Stone to evolve it into Mega Gyarados someday, it would become a nightmare even more opponents would never forget.

He returned Gyarados to its Poke Ball and thanked Will, Karen, and Looker.

"Thank you, all of you. I'm glad there was no damage we can't put right. Otherwise, I'd feel terrible."

If they had not been there, Ethan would have released the three Legendary birds, and handling Gyarados would probably have been easier. Still, they had helped him without hesitation, and he owed them his thanks.

"Don't call me the next time something like this happens," Karen said with a snort, then teased him. "Honestly, your Gyarados was harder to deal with than Team Rocket!"

Ethan laughed and offered a few more words to placate them.

Officers who had been pursuing the Rockets returned one after another. They had searched all around but found no trace of Sebastian or Tyson.

Looker shook his head in disappointment. The two men in charge of the research facility had escaped, casting a shadow over the operation.

He turned to Karen, bringing the conversation back to their mission and giving Ethan a way out of the teasing. "How did things go on your side?"

Karen shrugged. "We cleared the base completely. But that executive called Lance got away."

"He escaped too?"

Looker was disappointed again. Still, they had destroyed a base and a research facility, stopped Project R, and arrested a great many Rocket members. The operation had weakened Team Rocket and met its main objectives. Nothing in the world could go perfectly every time.

Looker thanked the others again. He offered to take them back to camp for a celebration, but Ethan, Will, and Karen declined. Looker left some officers to secure the site and see if anything useful could be recovered. Then he led the prisoners away.

After he was gone, Ethan exchanged contact information with Will and Karen and said goodbye to them. The Team Rocket matter was settled for now. He could return to challenging Gyms in peace.

...

Ethan and Sabrina left the shore of the Lake of Rage and headed through the thick forest toward Mahogany Town.

The sun was already sinking, dimming the woods. If they did not want to sleep outdoors, they needed to reach town before dark. Fortunately, there was still time before sunset, so they did not have to hurry.

As they neared the far edge of the forest, a Pokemon resting on a branch caught Sabrina's attention. She stopped and looked up at it with open curiosity.

"Hm? What is it?" Ethan asked. When she did not answer, he followed her gaze.

It was a Natu.

Most of Natu's body was a round green head. Its crest, tail, and feet were red. Its bright wings had black stripes. It looked a little vacant, but its almost human eyes gave it an uncanny air.

Ethan recalled that the designers had drawn on the bird worship of Indigenous peoples of the Americas when creating it. More specifically, he thought they had taken inspiration from an attendant crow in Mayan mythology. Its colors had come from other birds regarded as spiritual.

"Interested in it?"

Sabrina nodded lightly, and Ethan could not help smiling. In all their time traveling together, the only other Pokemon she had shown an interest in catching was the talking Slowking from the Orange Islands. This was only the second. How rare.

"Natu is a Psychic-type Pokemon, isn't it?"

As he spoke, Ethan took out his Pokedex and pointed it at Natu.

The device chimed. "Natu, the Tiny Bird Pokemon. Psychic and Flying type. At this stage, its wings are not fully developed, so it cannot fly and moves by hopping. It usually searches for food on the ground, but on rare occasions it hops onto branches to peck at fresh buds. It is said to be forever watching something. If you meet its eyes, it will stare back, but the moment you make the slightest movement, it will hop away."

Chapter 329: Pryce

Like Ethan, Sabrina generally caught only Pokemon she liked. For some reason, the unusual-looking Natu had struck her as adorable, and she wanted it for herself.

This time she did not fight it personally. She sent out Kadabra and chose to have a Pokemon battle.

At Sabrina's signal, Kadabra attacked as soon as it appeared. Natu tried to hop down and flee, but it could not fly. Kadabra caught up with Teleport, and Natu had no choice but to fight back in haste. A wild Pokemon that had not yet evolved was no match for one of a Gym Leader's main partners. Kadabra knocked it down after only a few exchanges.

Sabrina threw a Poke Ball and caught Natu easily.

Ethan had watched the whole thing. He applauded softly and smiled. "That wasn't easy to come by. It's only the second Pokemon you've caught on this journey! And if you hadn't sent out Kadabra, I would have thought you were going to handle the battle yourself again."

Sabrina gave him a small smile. Catching a new Pokemon had put her in a good mood.

"Don't you think it's cute?"

"Cute?"

Ethan raised his brows and tried to picture Natu again. He could not see what about it was cute. But Sabrina's tastes had never been easy to predict. As long as she was happy, it did not matter.

For its part, Natu probably could not understand how a moment's rest on a branch had ended with its capture. It had only looked her way once.

After that brief interruption, the two continued through the forest and reached Mahogany Town before dark.

Near the entrance, Ethan noticed a badly damaged wooden sign lying among some rocks. He brushed away the dirt. It read: Welcome to Mahogany Town, home of the ninjas.

He could not tell how long the sign had lain there. It had fallen apart through years of neglect, cast aside by time like the ninjas who had once lived in the town. Ethan set it aside and shook his head. Mahogany was the home of the ninjas in name only now.

Low wooden houses in a Japanese style lined the streets. Elderly residents talked outside their doors, and young children ran about freely. Life moved slowly here. Few vehicles passed, and Ethan saw surprisingly few Pokemon.

Lights glowed inside the homes, and the smell of cooking drifted into the street. Ethan's stomach growled. He was tired after the long day. Since meeting Looker and raiding the Rocket research facility, neither he nor Sabrina had eaten a proper meal.

First they needed somewhere to stay and something for dinner. The Gym challenge could wait until tomorrow; it was already late. With that settled, they headed toward the center of town.

They passed an ordinary-looking store with a police cordon outside. International Police officers were still coming and going. This had to be the entrance to Team Rocket's Mahogany base.

As they had done with the Celadon Game Corner in Kanto, Team Rocket had hidden the entrance inside a business. Frequent visitors would draw less suspicion there. For all their precautions, the base had still fallen.

Ethan and Sabrina paused to look, then moved on.

Mahogany Town was small and had no Pokemon Center. Following the map, Ethan found a guesthouse near the Gym. He liked the relaxed atmosphere of such places better than that of hotels, and chose them whenever he could.

They ate a filling dinner and bathed together. Planning to visit the Gym early, they went to bed soon afterward. Ethan was not ready to sleep, though. He made love to Sabrina until midnight before finally drifting off.

...

Mahogany Gym was the town's official Gym. Its Leader, Pryce, was an elderly man who specialized in Ice-type Pokemon. Defeating him earned a challenger the Glacier Badge.

Pryce was formidably strong. He had been a competitive Trainer in his youth, and age had only made his vast experience harder to overcome. He trained his Pokemon strictly, kept his distance from them, and avoided forming bonds of trust. That had earned him the name "Pryce of Winter." In some ways, he was the strongest of Johto's Gym Leaders.

Early the next morning, Ethan arrived outside Mahogany Gym with Sabrina, yawning and looking sleepy.

The Gym stood inside a courtyard. From the street, it looked much like an ordinary indoor swimming pool and had no distinguishing features. Given Mahogany's location and small population, there was little reason to establish a Gym here. Rumor said the Pokemon League had created it to persuade Pryce to stay and provide him a place to spend his old age.

The courtyard gate was open, so Ethan went in without ringing the bell. The instant he crossed the threshold, a wave of cold air struck him. Goose bumps rose on his skin, and every trace of sleepiness vanished.

He looked toward one corner of the courtyard. Snow and ice covered the area beside a pool, making it seem like a tiny winter world cut off from everything around it. Dozens of Ice-type Pokemon of various species played there happily.

Outside the wall, the street had been pleasantly warm. Within it, the air felt like winter. A single wall separated two seasons.

As Ethan watched with interest, footsteps sounded behind him. He turned. A stern-faced old man was walking slowly out of the Gym.

The man frowned at the sight of him in the courtyard. "Who are you?"

That must be Pryce.

Ethan gave a small nod and smiled, asking a question whose answer he already knew. "Hello. I've come to challenge Mahogany Gym. Are you its Leader?"

His warm smile stood in sharp contrast to Pryce's cold manner.

Chapter 330: Challenging Mahogany Gym

"That's right. I'm Pryce, the Leader of this Gym."

Age had turned his hair and eyebrows gray, but he was still sturdy. He did not walk with the unsteady steps of many older people, and his eyes remained sharp.

Strangely, in this chilly courtyard, he wore only a T-shirt, shorts, and open-toed sandals, as though he were on holiday on a tropical island. Ethan had to admire the old Ice-type Gym Leader's tolerance for cold.

Still, Pryce's voice had sounded rather stiff. Ethan looked back at the courtyard gate and apologized. "Sorry. I saw the gate was open and came in without announcing myself."

Pryce looked from one visitor to the other, then studied Ethan for a while. Fortunately, the uninvited entry did not seem to bother him much.

"Follow me."

He turned and headed back toward the Gym. Ethan and Sabrina exchanged a look, and Ethan shrugged.

Pryce did not seem fond of conversation and had an odd temper. But Ethan had come to challenge him. As long as Pryce would battle, his cold manner made no difference.

Ethan remembered why Pryce had supposedly become like this. When he was young, he had been burned in a battle and left bedridden. His Piloswine had gone away during that time and never returned. Pryce believed it had abandoned him. He lost trust in Pokemon and became strict and distant.

There was a misunderstanding behind the story, of course. Ethan had no wish to get involved. Ash could sort it out someday; he was always eager to help with such things.

Following Pryce inside, Ethan felt an even colder air than in the courtyard. It must be more than ten degrees below freezing in here.

He pulled his clothes tighter and looked around. An enormous ice battlefield occupied the middle of the Gym. Ice pillars of many sizes stood across it. The field radiated cold like a giant block of ice, making the whole room feel like a freezer.

There was a deep pool beneath the ice, but its surface had frozen solid. Unless someone deliberately broke it, even large Pokemon such as Gyarados or Steelix could battle on top without suddenly falling through.

Ethan's breath became a white cloud that quickly vanished. Beyond the field, the Gym was sparsely furnished. A few rows of seats stood in the spectator area, and the rest of the large room was empty.

At Pryce's direction, Ethan walked to the opposite Trainer's position.

"You've arrived early," Pryce said. "Sheila, our referee, can't get here this soon. I'll handle the judging myself. Any objection?"

"None."

To Ethan, a referee was not essential. He and Pryce could tell who had won. A referee mainly made the match feel more formal and impartial.

Pryce explained the rules as usual. "The Mahogany Gym challenge has three rounds. Defeat all three of my Pokemon and you succeed."

It sounded like any other Gym challenge, but Ethan paused to think. Many Ice-type Pokemon were also Water types. That combination could give several members of his team trouble: Scyther, Crystal Steelix, Dragonair, Charizard, and even his new Larvitar.

Gyarados would have no particular problem, but it was too strong. Ethan did not consider using it. The same went for Gengar, which he kept out of ordinary battles.

At another Gym he would have liked to train Dragonair and Larvitar. Here, Gardevoir and Jolteon looked like his only practical choices.

As Pryce reached for a Poke Ball, Ethan hurried to stop him. He explained that he wanted to face Pryce's best Pokemon.

"Young man, I judged you correctly," Pryce said. "You have confidence enough to ask for my strongest Pokemon outright."

Ethan blinked. Pryce clearly knew something about him. "You know me?"

Pryce nodded, then shook his head. "I've watched a few of your battles on television, nothing more. But Blaine and I have known each other for years. He's spoken of you. From the way he talked, I could tell he thought highly of you."

That explained why Pryce had studied him outside. Still, Ethan found their friendship surprising. Pryce was reserved and used Ice types, while Blaine was warm, excitable, fond of jokes, and favored Fire types. They could hardly have been more different.

Seeing Ethan's understanding, Pryce continued, "You're not like an ordinary challenger. Since you ask, I'll make an exception and battle you with my strongest team."

He put away the Poke Ball he had taken out and chose another from his pouch.

For fairness, Ethan had hoped they might send out their Pokemon at the same time, as he had done in his matches at Violet and Blackthorn Gyms. Before he could suggest it, Pryce threw his ball.

The old man had his pride. Letting a visitor challenge his strongest team already broke his routine; he was not going to change the rest of his rules as well. Ethan accepted it.

He looked at the field. Pryce had sent out a Dewgong.

Dewgong was a fairly common Pokemon, and Ethan had fought them many times. He knew the species well. Still, if Pryce had chosen this one for his strongest team, it was bound to be formidable.

Ethan thought for a moment, then threw his own Poke Ball. Pryce was taking the battle seriously, and Ethan needed to start with a win.

"Go, Jolteon! The first round is yours!"
